# Local-maxxing trove survey — 2026-09-14 (thought-master, first seating)

Produced by the registered workflow `trove-survey` (harness run wf_7df0a578-8fb: 9 readers → 9 adversarial critics → 3-vision hypothesis panel → judge; 22 agents, 2.65M subagent tokens, 518 tool uses, 71 min; own harness, $0 OpenRouter, $0 GPU). Every number below is tagged MEASURED or ESTIMATE by the agent that wrote it; owner verbatim lives on goal:g14, never here.

## Sources (clones live in the session scratchpad only; re-clone by hash)
- https://github.com/project-89/coherence-guided-dead-head-identification @ 583962f (PolyForm-NC + patent-pending: research use only)
- https://github.com/CodexOperator/machinelearning @ d87863c (modularNN, snn_standard_model, snn_applied_finance, spikyNN)
- https://github.com/CodexOperator/Simple-Spiking-Net-Demo @ 5ef6e83
- https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2609.recurrent-looped-transformer (Zhang 2026-09-12; design spec, zero experiments — see the looped-transformer reading)
- Box MEASURED 2026-09-14 02:31Z: 4× arm64-N1 (asimddp, no i8mm), 23 GB, MemAvailable 7.0 GB, SwapFree 239 MB/4 GB, load 16.6 — actively paging; no LLM runtime installed (ollama purged 2026-09-13), no GGUF, no transformers.

## JUDGE (ranked chains, round-0 baseline, Camber plan, coalescence, critiques, gaps)

=== ROUND0 BASELINE
- Tenancy snapshot, taken again at every later measurement and logged on every tok/s row: /proc/loadavg, MemAvailable, SwapFree, pgmajfault delta, top-3 RSS. MEASURED 2026-09-14 02:31Z this session: load 16.64/11.90/11.50 on 4 cores, MemAvailable 7,008,408 kB, SwapFree 239,028 kB of 4 GB, pgmajfault 2,032,465, disk 34 GB free. The box is actively paging; nothing measured here today is a clean number.
- Decide the benchmark host before spending on downloads: record the 8 GB box's lscpu (model, AVX2/AVX-512, i8mm-equivalents), flags, DDR type, OS, what else is resident, and its Doppler/secrets-hub operational constraint (goal:g14.4); bank the 'pause firefox/ffmpeg/VNC on this box' decision for the owner with chain 1 A6's pgmajfault number as the evidence.
- Copy bandwidth with EXPLICIT byte accounting (state whether read+write are both counted), 256 MB numpy arrays, 1 and 4 processes, 3 repeats each, tenancy logged — resolves the critic's factor-2 ambiguity (20.1 vs 16.5 GB/s single; 65.7 GB/s aggregate not reproduced) that flips chain 1 A2's regime prediction.
- fp32 matmul GFLOPS at 1 and 4 threads, bf16 matmul (confirm the critic's 2 GFLOPS — bf16 dead on this box), torch._int_mm int8 (5.5 GOPS MEASURED under load), the NEON popcount kernel (211-242 GOPS/core MEASURED under load) — 3 repeats each, so every later CPU-hour estimate has a sustained-GFLOPS denominator.
- Build llama.cpp from source (-DGGML_NATIVE=ON; gcc + cmake MEASURED present; ~10 CPU-min ESTIMATE), pin the commit hash, and FIRST read ggml-cpu's repack source for the dotprod-only Q4_0 layout — a free answer to 'does repack help N1 without i8mm' before any download.
- Download the minimal ladder (~5 GB ESTIMATE: Qwen3-0.6B Q8_0 + Q4_K_M, Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0, Qwen3.5-2B Q4_K_M, Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M) and VERIFY each loads and generates on aarch64 without i8mm — GDN CPU kernels and Gemma 4 support on this ISA are unverified anywhere, and a failed load voids most chains after the download.
- 5x llama-bench tg128/pp512 on one GGUF spaced 10 min apart with the tenancy snapshot (chain 1 A1): the reproducibility CV that decides whether this box can host any Vision-1 number.
- Thinking-mode control: verify the /no_think toggle and the chat-template switch on Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B, then measure tokens per GSM8K answer on 10 items in both modes — every panel's token/CPU-hour estimate is 3-15x wrong if thinking is on, and 2026 model cards mix modes (4B/9B tables likely thinking-mode).
- Zero-install SNN comparators (~5 min each): the spike-count-readout LIF baseline on the trove's 784-1000-10 MNIST (the 96.10% figure uses a summed-membrane readout a 1-bit-out neuron cannot expose) and one snn.Leaky(state_quant=256) run — the comparator every byte-neuron claim in chain 2 needs; MNIST IDX MEASURED on disk at ~/.cache/torchvision.
- The two director decisions (CP0): llama.cpp runtime choice and the transformers+datasets install — recorded in one node so no seed re-litigates them; note which chains are blocked on each.
- Camber round-0 table (thought-master card ROUND 0 (a)-(d)): confirm 1 credit = $1 and XS = $3/h on cambercloud.com/pricing, GPU type UNKNOWN until the first burst's nvidia-smi; 3 GPU-h/month = $9/month.
- 'qwen3.8 50b' as a ONE-LINE owner question, not a hypothesis: no such id (HF API 2026-09-13: Qwen3.8-27B, Flash-Next 125B-A6B with a 51B n-gram embedding, 2.4T-A95B); it came from a spoken transcript hedged with 'maybe'; the owner's later CPU-only correction (g14.md L108, MEASURED) supersedes it; qwen/qwen3.8-27b is already the account's largest OpenRouter spender ($39.20, 49%, not the loop).
- A doc node under g14.3 recording the three trove clone URLs + commit hashes (dead-head 583962f, machinelearning d87863c, Simple-Spiking-Net-Demo) and the saved pdftotext files (rlt.txt, xlayer.txt) — the clones live only in this session's ephemeral scratchpad and the owner's SNN code exists nowhere else on the box (editable install and symlink both dangling).
- Mint the standing harness (CP5) as an experiment-shaped file before any chain's second hypothesis: 200 items, per-item vectors, McNemar, build hash, tenancy columns; decide whether the evidence gate (evidence_runs >= 1) accepts a scratchpad payload_ref or requires extensions/ or a town branch.

=== CAMBER BURST PLAN
- 1. nvidia-smi + a 60-second bandwidth/matmul microbench in the FIRST five minutes of the first session (~5 min, ~$0.25 at $3/h — 1 credit = $1 MEASURED cambercloud.com/pricing per the critic; XSMALL lists 5 CPU / 50 GB RAM / 3 credits/h with NO GPU type or count in the on-demand table; the owner's '24 GB VRAM' is the owner's word). Closes goal:g14.3(b) and sets every later $/h and tok/s estimate; goal:g14.3 reasoned from a false 50B premise.
- 2. Chain 6 C2 — the looped-depth toy burst (~25-30 min, ~$1.50): 4L-untied, 4L-untied x4 tokens (FLOPs-matched), 4L-x4-fixed, 4L-x4-elastic (LoopFormer recipe, Ouro-vs-Huginn loss fork as two loss functions), 16L-untied, and the RLT cross-token gated-feedback arm (eqs 2.9-2.11) on 100M tokens of three synthetic tasks; export checkpoints < 100 MB. Everything after (C3, C4) is CPU. Gated by CP6 wording only, not by go/no-go.
- 3. Chain 9 V3-B4 — PRM-7B (bf16) scoring of the SAME stored 1.7B/2B samples the CPU scored with PRM-1.5B (~20-30 min, ~$1-1.50). Only after the CPU corpus and the 1.5B pass exist; the one datum a GPU changes because a 7B fp32 fits no box here.
- 4. Chain 4 V2-C4 + C5 — ONE training job, two LoRA adapters on Qwen3.5-4B (the C2 gap-spotter or PoT slot, plus self-distillation on exec-verified traces) (~30 min, ~$1.50). Runs ONLY if V2-C1's kill-test fails (the corpus is trainable) or chain 9 produced >= 1k verified traces; otherwise skipped and the minutes stay unspent.
- 5. Reserve ~85-95 min unallocated for re-runs of 2-4 or a second month's Kuramoto-attention enwik8 replication (osc seed 4) — explicitly NOT for inference benchmarks, KLD reference-logit generation (a 32K reference does not fit this box's use anyway), the 27B line, or anything the owner's CPU-only correction (g14.md L108) puts off-charter. Total planned ~85-95 GPU-min of 180 (~$4.50-5 of the $9/month cap).

=== RANKED CHAINS

#1 [1 (feeds 2 and 3)] Box calibration: bytes-touched-per-token as the ISA constant (regime, GDN hybrid, prefill/cache, co-residency) (kpt=0.92; this box CPU + 8 GB box; ~40 min wall, ~1 GB download, $0 (ESTIMATE); build ~10 CPU-min (ESTIMATE))
  WHY: Every cost and speed line in all three panels silently assumes a regime (bandwidth- vs compute-bound) nobody has measured on 4x N1 with asimddp only; the literature has zero numbers for this ISA at 0.6B-4B, and every on-box microbenchmark so far was taken at load ~10 with swap full (critic reproduced f32 4-thread OpenBLAS collapsing 54 -> 9.9 GOPS). If the box is unbenchmarkable, every later chain saves a second run by moving to the 8 GB box; either way the reusable protocol is free. Failure of A1 is itself the cheapest possible good news.
  H1: A1 (merged V1-A1 / V2-A1 / V3-A1): under current tenancy, 5x llama-bench tg128 on one GGUF (Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0) spaced 10 min apart has CV > 25% on this box and < 5% on the idle 8 GB box — i.e. this box cannot host Vision-1 numbers until tenants are paused or the 8 GB box is the benchmark host. FALSIFIER: CV < 10% here with load avg > 8 and MemAvailable < 7 GB logged at each run (MEASURED 02:31Z: load 16.6/11.9/11.5, MemAvailable 7.0 GB, SwapFree 239 MB, pgmajfault 2,032,465).
  EXP1: cmake -B build -DGGML_NATIVE=ON llama.cpp (record commit hash; gcc+cmake MEASURED present); read ggml-cpu repack source for the dotprod-only Q4_0 layout BEFORE downloading anything (free answer to the 'Q4_0 on N1' question); download one GGUF (~0.9 GB ESTIMATE); 5x llama-bench -t 4 -p 512 -n 128, each wrapped with /proc/loadavg, MemAvailable, pgmajfault delta; same script on the 8 GB box with its lscpu/flags/DDR recorded. Output: the mandatory logging protocol every tok/s in the town must carry.

#2 [1 substrate (3 only at D3)] Byte-neuron kill-tests: state semantics, state width, gather cost, bit order, then the 1-bit channel (kpt=0.9; this box CPU (or 8 GB box); < 5 min CPU, $0 (ESTIMATE); MNIST already on disk for E2 (MEASURED ~/.cache/torchvision/MNIST/raw))
  WHY: The owner's thread is behind dense prior art (DWN ICML 2024, difflogic, Tsetlin, rank-order coding) and the owner's own trove says spike input loses to graded input and stacking micro-nets is a dead end (snn-architecture.md, MEASURED this session). The honest first move is a training-free, seed-free measurement that decides the neuron's semantics before a line of architecture is written; E2 then sizes the state with one flag on an existing harness (snn.Leaky state_quant MEASURED present). Only after three near-free kill-tests does the one genuinely novel claim (bit ORDER as code) earn 3-4 CPU-h.
  H1: E3 (V1-E3 = V3-D2 seed-2 part = snn seed 2): the owner's semantic question (spatial byte over 8 upstream neurons vs temporal byte = one upstream's last 8 bits) decided by emulation — an 8-bit shift register + 256-entry LUT reproduces the spikyNN LIF spike train (beta 0.8, thr 1.0, w 0.8, 200 steps) with <= 20% spike edit distance, and the best-LUT distance scales with beta^8 (0.8^8 = 0.17 of membrane memory lies beyond 8 ticks). FALSIFIER: best of all 256 LUTs > 35% edit distance at beta 0.8, or distance does not track beta^8 across beta in {0.5, 0.8, 0.95}.
  EXP1: Exhaustive 256-LUT sweep in numpy against recorded LIF trains for N1 and N2 (scratchpad/machinelearning/spikyNN/snn_neuron.py), three betas; deterministic, seed-free, no installs, no downloads.
   1. E3 — LUT-sweep semantics test (above). Fail-knowledge: the temporal byte cannot emulate leaky integration, so 'which bits in what order' must mean the spatial fan-in byte = DWN's object, whose training rule (EFD + Learnable Mapping) is reused rather than invented.
   2. E2 (V1-E2 = V2-E2 = V3-D1) — state precision bound: snntorch Leaky state_quant on the trove's 784-1000-10 MNIST, 25 steps, 1 epoch, seed 42 (fixed-beta baseline 96.10% MEASURED, but with a SUMMED-MEMBRANE readout a 1-bit-out neuron cannot expose) loses <= 0.5 pp at 256 levels, >= 2 pp at 16, >= 10 pp at 2 — with a SPIKE-COUNT readout baseline run f
   3. E1 (V1-E1) — the byte-neuron is gather-bound, not popcount-bound: an 8-input LUT network (uint8 state, 256-entry table walk, event-driven, 2% connectivity) sustains < 10% of the synaptic-ops/s of the dense NEON XNOR-popcount kernel (211-242 binary GOPS/core MEASURED at load ~10, scratchpad/pop.c) and the gap widens with N. FALSIFIER: LUT-walk >= 25
   4. E-ORDER (V2-E3 = V3-D2 = osc seed 6) — bit ORDER carries trainable information: a byte-neuron whose state is a shift register of one upstream's last 8 outputs, firing via a 256-entry LUT trained by DWN's Extended Finite Difference + Learnable Mapping, beats an order-blind popcount neuron by >= 5 pts on delayed-XOR (delay 4) and sequential-row MNIST
   5. E-CHANNEL (V3-D3) — a learned 1-bit/tick pub/sub channel makes two independently trained byte-neuron nets cooperate on parity-of-two-streams: learned-channel pair >= 85% where each alone ~50% and a random fixed channel <= 60%; marginal accuracy per channel bit (k in {1,2,4,8}) is concave. FALSIFIER: random-channel pair >= learned pair - 5 pts, or l
   6. E-BUGFIX (snn seed 5, free) — re-running the two artefact experiments with bugs fixed reverses the recorded findings: wiring inhibition=True into snn.Leaky changes accuracy/density measurably, and applying compute_energy_earned + input weight 0.4 (< thr) makes beta learning affect spike rate. FALSIFIER: inhibition still reproduces 96.10%/4.34% to 4

#3 [1 (owner thread)] The coherence statistic: kill it as a head-pruning lever with the paper's own missing controls, then test the only well-posed coupling map (kpt=0.88; this box CPU (~3 GB RAM; run when MemAvailable > 6 GB); ~20 min CPU, ~2 GB download, $0 (ESTIMATE); GATED on the one transformers+datasets install decision (transformers MEASURED absent))
  WHY: The trove's own data — verified this session — already says head pruning cannot be a Vision-1 lever: base-rate lift 1.02/0.99/1.00 (data/base_rate_analysis.json), safe_recall 0.315 on GPT-2, dead-set removal +1.13 nat, structural removal slower at seq <= 256, heads < 8% of params, Qwen GQA g=7 leaves 3/48 KV groups compactable. D1 costs 20 minutes, is the paper's own un-run future work (mean-compensated pruning, paper.tex L716-719), and its outcome is decisive in both directions: null holds -> tau_death is retired as a criterion in one verdict node and the SNN import must source coupling from something causal; null fails -> the map has content and chains 8 and 11 get a real signal. Nothing else in the owner's dead-head thread earns tokens before this runs.
  H1: D1 (V1-D1 = V2-E1 = V3-D4 control = dead-head seed 5 + critic's mean-ablation): null, stated as the paper's missing controls — on Qwen2.5-0.5B (fp32, 1.976 GB MEASURED), removing the 157 bundled tau-dead heads costs an eval delta-loss within 1 std (0.48 nat, 3-seed spread MEASURED) of removing 157 uniformly random non-protected heads, AND mean-ablation (replace each removed head's write-back by its calibration mean) cuts the +1.13-nat dead-set damage by >= 50% — the 'catastrophe' is a LayerNorm/mean-shift artifact, not lost information. FALSIFIER: dead-set removal is >= 1.0 nat better than every one of 10 random draws AND mean-ablation helps < 20% — the dead set is special and carries coupling structure.
  EXP1: Reuse decisions[].dead from scratchpad/dead-head/data/qwen25_05b_head336_small_theory_redundancy_v2_boundary2.json; 11 zero-ablation masks + 11 mean-ablation masks (means from 8 calibration sequences) on 16 x 128 WikiText-2 tokens; one forward per mask; use joint delta-loss, not the bundled 'unsafe' labels (8 x 128 TinyStories tokens at a 1%-of-baseline = 0.023-nat threshold, inside sampling noise). Also record write-norm ratio r_h = ||s_h||/||x|| per head (dead-head seed 1's confound) from the same forwards.
   1. D1 — random-set + mean-ablation controls (above). Fail-knowledge (null holds, expected): tau_death retired as a pruning criterion; the coherence statistic becomes a descriptive quantity with a base-rate caveat.
   2. D2 (V1-D2) — head pruning cannot buy CPU speed even in the best case: deleting all 157 dead heads (47%) removes < 8% of parameters (GPT-2 lost 6.6% params for 29% of heads, MEASURED gpt2_structural_timing_v1.json) and < 7% of KV bytes/token at g=7, so decode tok/s changes < 5% at seq 128 and 1024. FALSIFIER: > 10% tok/s gain at either length. COST 
   3. D-K (V2-E5 = V3-D4 = dead-head seed 2 = osc seed 3; RUN ONLY IF D1's null fails) — the head-head write-back coupling matrix K_hh' = E_i cos(s_h(i), s_h'(i)) over 336 heads predicts joint-ablation interaction: |K| > 4/sqrt(d) pairs sub-additive, strongly negative K super-additive, |K| < 4/sqrt(d) additive, with |Spearman| >= 0.3 over 100 sampled pai
   4. D-CAUSAL (dead-head seed 4; alternative if D-K is null but D1 found structure) — the causal coupling K_{h->h'} = E ||delta s_h'(i)|| under ablation of h is sparse and heavy-tailed, and tau-dead heads have outgoing causal coupling comparable to alive heads. FALSIFIER: mean outgoing causal coupling of dead heads < 25% of alive heads. COST ~1-1.5 h CP
   5. D-SVD (dead-head seed 6, cheap follow-on) — low-rank SVD filtering of dead heads' W_O slices improves Qwen2.5-0.5B loss (replicating the unbundled Gemma 3 4B -1.5% 'mildly toxic' claim) with optimal rank 8-32 of 64; alive-head filtering as control. FALSIFIER: no rank in {4,8,16,32,48} gives delta-loss < 0 on 32 WikiText-2 sequences. COST ~15 min CP

#4 [2 (goal:g14.1 / g14 lattice)] Is the graph's own corpus trainable? Kill-test before any lattice slot, then the gap-spotter, router and one CPU LoRA feasibility number (kpt=0.85; this box CPU; < 20 CPU-min, $0, zero downloads, zero installs (ESTIMATE))
  WHY: goal:g14.1 says 'idea -> hypothesis ONLY, no run scheduled' and the owner's migration path is prose first, scored model second. This is the cheapest decisive test in the whole survey: it uses data the graph already holds, needs nothing installed, and either outcome changes what the town does — a null says the verdict labels are uninformative (a finding about verdict-writing discipline: the graph needs hard negatives before any slot is trained), a hit says there is a labelled dataset for the first learned slot. Everything downstream (gap-spotter, router, LoRA) is gated on it.
  H1: V2-C1 KILL-TEST: no classifier trained on the graph's 170 verdict nodes (hypothesis body + experiment body -> verdict class {proved, lean_proved>=75, lean<=65, lean_disproved}) beats the majority-class baseline by more than 5 pts on held-out nodes, because the label distribution is ~90% lean-proved (MEASURED: 34 proved / ~130 lean-proved / 6 lean-disproved / 0 disproved) and hypothesis text does not predict verdict. FALSIFIER: tf-idf + logistic regression (or a 384-d embedding + logreg) beats majority by >= 10 pts with 5-fold CV grouped by goal — the corpus carries signal and fine-tune slots are worth scheduling.
  EXP1: python3 + sklearn 1.8 (MEASURED installed) only: parse .agi/nodes/hypothesis/*.md and verdict/*.md, join by parent id, stratified 5-fold CV grouped by goal, report accuracy, macro-F1, calibration; also a 'confidence' regression. Minutes.

#6 [1 + 2 (owner's first-named paper)] Looped depth on CPU: the case is cache residency, not FLOPs — and the one Camber training burst (kpt=0.8; this box CPU + 8 GB box; ~30 min, $0, no downloads (ESTIMATE))
  WHY: Every reader treated looped transformers as a parameter/RAM story and missed the only CPU-specific advantage: a shared block whose weights stay cache-resident across loops costs compute but not DRAM bandwidth. RLT itself has zero experiments (rlt.txt MEASURED: 'does not report measured efficiency or scaling results'), Ouro at 4 loops burns ~10B-dense FLOPs/token (ESTIMATE), bf16 on this box is 2 GFLOPS vs 50 fp32 (MEASURED by the critic), and training even a 10-20M toy pair on CPU is ~100+ CPU-h at 15 GFLOPS sustained (ESTIMATE) — exactly what a 25-minute GPU burst does. C1 is the 30-minute go/no-go (CP6) for spending the month's burst, and it is informative on failure: 'looping = RAM only on these boxes' with a measurement behind it.
  H1: C1 (V1-C1 = V2-D3): this N1 VM has an exploitable cache tier — a batch-1 chain of fp32 matvecs whose total weights are <= 8 MB sustains >= 3x the bytes/s-equivalent of one with 128 MB of weights (L2 1 MB/core; the Ampere host's shared system cache ~32 MB is contended by other tenants — size and residency unknown). FALSIFIER: <= 1.3x between 8 MB and 128 MB working sets (no cache tier the VM can exploit at batch 1; looping buys RAM only).
  EXP1: numpy/torch fp32 (or the scratchpad NEON kernel) over weight sets of 4/8/16/32/64/128 MB, 200 layer-evals each, 1 and 4 threads, A1 logging protocol; repeat on the 8 GB box, whose private L3 should show the knee clearly and calibrate whether the VM's flatness is real or noisy-neighbour.
   1. C1 — cache-residency microbench (above). Fail-knowledge: the CPU-specific argument for looping is dead; looped models are judged on RAM (8 GB box) and accuracy-per-FLOP (Vision 2) alone, and C4 is skipped.
   2. C2 THE CAMBER BURST (V1-C2 = V2-D1 + V2-D2 + looped H1/H2, with the critic's FLOPs-matched arm and H3's cross-token feedback arm added) — a 4-layer tied block looped 4x (d=256, ~3.1M non-embedding params, MEASURED arithmetic) trained with elastic loop count in [1,8] (LoopFormer recipe: random loop count + step conditioning + shortcut consistency) r
   3. C3 (V1-C3 = V2-D4 quant half) — quantization error compounds sub-linearly across loops: int8 dynamic quant of the shared block gives KLD(k loops) <= k x KLD(1 loop) for k in {2,4,8}, and 4-bit fake-quant stays <= 2k x KLD(1 loop); the 16L untied model at matched bits is the control. FALSIFIER: super-linear compounding (KLD at 4 loops > 4x KLD at 1 
   4. C4 (V1-C4) — at batch 1 the 4L-x4 looped model decodes >= 1.4x the tok/s of the FLOPs-identical 16L untied model (weights 4x smaller, cache-resident per C1), tracking C1's curve within 20%. FALSIFIER: < 1.1x tok/s. COST ~20 min, $0. Fail-knowledge: confirms C1's negative at model level; 'looping = RAM only on these boxes' recorded as a verdict with
   5. C5 (V1-C5 = V2-D4 = looped H6) — an off-the-shelf looped model has no elastic depth: ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B at total_ut_steps=2 gives < 60% of its 4-step accuracy on 100 GSM8K items, and a 3-loop-draft / 4-loop-verify self-speculative scheme (LayerSkip/Draft&Verify prior art) gives < 1.3x tok/s at identical greedy output. Requires torch int8 dynamic q

Before the full run: 10 items per model to measure tokens/answer and fix the cost line.

#8 [1 (owner's KV-cache/streaming thread)] KV precision policy for tiny GQA models on CPU: q8 KV as a SPEED lever, K/V asymmetry, additivity with weight quant, per-KV-head sensitivity, and the coherence bridge (kpt=0.72; this box CPU; ~1 h CPU, $0 (ESTIMATE), after chain 1's build)
  WHY: The literature (H2O, SnapKV, PyramidKV, TriAttention, Random Attention) is GPU-only, 4B-32B, not in llama.cpp, and Random Attention drops 6.5 pts vs full cache at 4x (the column the reader omitted). On this box, if decode is bandwidth-bound, KV quant is a speed lever the literature never states, and asimddp gives a concrete prediction (q8_0 faster than f16, q4_0 V no faster) that A3 tests unpredicted by any reader. The chain then asks the one per-head question nobody has asked (D3) and gives the owner's coherence statistic its single KV test (D4).
  H1: A3 (V1-A3 = kv seed 1, re-metric'd per the critic): KV quantization is a SPEED lever on this CPU, not only memory — on Qwen3-0.6B Q8_0 at 8K context (f16 KV 112 KB/token ESTIMATE from HF config 28 x 8 x 128; q8_0 59.5 KB/token), -ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0 -fa on raises decode tok/s >= 15% over f16 KV because int8 sdot is what asimddp gives, with wikitext-2 KLD < 0.01 (7-27B measured 0.0064 in llama.cpp #23470); adding -ctv q4_0 buys < 5% more speed on this ISA. FALSIFIER: q8_0 KV tok/s <= f16 KV tok/s at 8K (the CPU flash-attention path with quantized V is a loss on N1 — no published ARM number exists), or KLD > 0.02 at 0.6B.
  EXP1: llama-bench -p 8192 -n 64 with {f16/f16, q8_0/q8_0, q8_0/q4_0} and -fa on/off; llama-perplexity -c 2048 --kl-divergence-base against an f16-KV reference (0.6B f16 weights 1.2 GB + 8K f16 KV 0.9 GB fits in the ~7 GB available). #10 [1] Below 4 bits on a dotprod-only CPU: ternary LUT kernels without i8mm, bigger-at-fewer-bits at tiny scale, and the trove's role-aware recipe (kpt=0.6; this box CPU + 8 GB box; ~2 h wall, ~3 GB download, $0 (ESTIMATE); director approval for a second build)

=== COALESCENCE
- CP0 (before anything: two director decisions taken ONCE, recorded in a node) — (i) build llama.cpp from source with commit hash pinned; (ii) pip install transformers + datasets (+ peft later). Five chains gate on (ii) (3, 8-D3/D4, 9-PRM, 4-LoRA, 11-E4) and seven on (i). Re-litigating either per seed is the failure mode; taking both once unblocks ~60% of all proposed experiments.
- CP1 — the bytes-touched ledger (after chain 1 A1-A5 + chain 10 B1, ~week 1): one table per model on each box — GGUF bytes, KV bytes/token, tok/s at ctx {512, 8K}, pp/tg ratio, effective GB/s, KLD vs f16 — tests the second-order hypothesis 'tok/s on these boxes = f(bytes touched per token) with one ISA constant'. Holds -> every later lever (KV quant, GDN, ternary, looping, per-head KV) is evaluated as a bytes-touched delta before it runs, and the CPU cost law for cooperation (V3-CP1: cooperation pays only where it converts decode tokens into prefill tokens or cache hits) follows. Fails -> compute-bound; re-rank toward kernels (chain 10 B1, chain 2 E1) and batched sampling becomes nearly free for chains 7/9.
- CP2 — precision x reuse (chain 6 C3 with chain 8 B5 and chain 10 B3/B4, after the Camber burst): 'what is the RAM-minimal architecture for the 8 GB box' — looped + int8/Q4 + constant-state hybrid vs dense + IQ3 + q8 KV — decided from C2's checkpoints and B3/B5 KLDs with no new training.
- CP3 — one per-head statistic or none (chain 3 D1/D-K with chain 8 D3/D4, after both run): if D4 holds the coherence observable enters Vision 1 through the KV door; if D1's null holds and D4 fails, the statistic is scoped out of Vision 1 in ONE verdict node and the owner's oscillator-map thread is closed or re-sourced from causal coupling — either way chains 3 and 8 merge into 'KV precision policy for tiny GQA models on CPU'.
- CP4 — substrate or not (chain 2 E1/E2/E3 with chain 3 D-K and chain 11 E4): only if BOTH the byte-neuron is a viable integer substrate with known state semantics AND the coupling map has transferable structure does the second-order hypothesis 'coupling map as the byte-neuron subscription wiring' exist; if either is negative the SNN thread is re-homed as an encoder/front-end (Vision 1) or a test-time-steps question (chain 11 V2-E4) and stops competing for Vision 3 tokens. Bit-order (E-ORDER) and the 1-bit channel (E-CHANNEL) join with chain 7's process-diversity result into 'how many bits per tick must a cooperation channel carry before two nets beat one'.
- CP5 — the standing harness and the rising-ceiling ledger (adopt before ANY chain's second hypothesis): 200 items (GSM8K + ARC-style MC + IFEval slice), thinking mode fixed off and logged, per-item correctness vectors + token counts + answer-token margins in one file, paired McNemar/bootstrap over items with a power statement (SE ~3.5 pts at n=200), llama.cpp commit + load + MemAvailable + pgmajfault on every row. Chain 1 A5 decides whether prompt caching may be used inside it without changing logits. Every chain reports against it so Vision 2's 'tiny-model ceiling rising month over month' becomes a dated curve; second-order hypothesis: the ceiling rises more per month from inference-side chains (7, 9, 6) than training-side (chain 4 C4/C5) at 3 GPU-h/month.
- CP6 — Camber go/no-go (after chain 6 C1, before C2): C1 on both boxes decides whether the stated reason for looped depth is 'CPU speed' or 'RAM on the 8 GB box'; C2 runs either way (elastic depth and quant-across-loops stand on their own) but its verdict node is worded by C1's answer.
- CP7 — the test-time compute allocator (chain 7 A2/A3/replays + chain 9 headroom/exec-BoN/cascade, after both have per-item data): one second-order hypothesis — for a fixed CPU-second budget the optimal split across (thinking tokens, parallel samples, verifier calls, escalations) is a function of the small model's pass@1 alone — tested as one four-knob Pareto sweep over the same stored samples, with CIs, before any MVP. Chain 4's 'one scorer, three heads' (verifier V2-B4, router V2-C3, gap-spotter V2-C2 as one encoder + per-slot linear heads) is the companion second-order set and is where 'lattice, not ladder' gets its first measurable shape.
- CP8 — iteration-to-convergence as the universal test-time knob (chain 7 ESC replay + chain 6 C2 elastic exit + chain 11 V2-E4 sync halting): one latent-difference criterion governs accuracy-vs-compute saturation in all three; falsified if the three curves saturate at unrelated points.
- CP9 — restate Vision 3 in the graph (after chains 5, 7, 9; final before any MVP): assemble every (scheme, CPU-seconds, accuracy, CI) row into one Pareto table (V3-E3); fit concave vs convex segments with bootstrap over items; mint vision:local-maxxing-3 with the operational target 'marginal accuracy per CPU-second beyond the best member, bootstrap CI over items' and the graph-as-coordinator as the learned router trained on the town's own correctness vectors; 'exponentially smarter' is retired unless some scheme shows an accelerating segment with CI excluding zero.

=== CRITIQUES OF OWNER PLAN
- 'qwen3.8 50b' does not exist and should not enter any node as a candidate. All three panels and five readers agree (HF API 2026-09-13): the open Qwen3.8 line is 27B dense (Q4_K_M 17 GB), Flash-Next (125B total, 6B active, with a 51B n-gram embedding — the likeliest origin of '50b'), and 2.4T-A95B. The source is a spoken transcript ('Canberra cloud' in the same line) hedged with 'maybe', and the owner's own later correction — 'all models tiny enough to run on CPU + regular RAM' (g14.md L108, MEASURED this session) — puts the entire >= 27B line off-charter except as a burst. Nothing in it fits this box (~7 GB available) or the 8 GB box. The on-charter ladder is Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B (Apache-2.0, hybrid GDN), Gemma 4 E2B/E4B, SmolLM3-3B, Phi-4-mini, LFM2. Separately: qwen/qwen3.8-27b is already the account's largest OpenRouter spender ($39.20, 49%, 5257 req) and it is not the loop — the biggest cost lever is outside the research plan.
- The dead-head coherence equation is not a head-pruning lever on CPU, and the trove proves it with its own artifacts (re-verified this session): base-rate lift over random head selection 1.02/0.99/1.00 (data/base_rate_analysis.json — on GPT-2 Medium the threshold is BELOW random), safe_recall 0.315 on GPT-2, AUC(z_h -> unsafe) 0.17-0.47 on the three small models, removing the dead set together costs +1.13 nat on Qwen2.5-0.5B (+0.87 to +2.84 across models; the paper text says +1.4 to +2.1), structural removal is SLOWER at seq <= 256 (GPT-2 Medium -46% at 128), heads are < 8% of parameters (MLP dominates), and Qwen-class GQA at g=7 leaves 3/48 KV groups compactable. The 'scaling collapse' is tautological (z = sqrt(d) c), the 0.679 = 4/r constant is never derived (r undefined), the ground-truth labels come from 1024 TinyStories tokens at a 1%-of-baseline threshold inside sampling noise, and removing dead+redundant heads hurts LESS than dead alone on GPT-2 (1.85 vs 2.84) — damage that is distributional, not informational. Treat it as a descriptive statistic; run the paper's own missing controls once (chain 3 D1, 20 min) and give it exactly one KV-head test (chain 8 D4). Licence: PolyForm-NC + provisional patent 64/031,983 — research use only.
- 'Generate coupled-oscillator maps from the pruning equation' has no well-posed object in the source. The paper's oscillators are TOKEN positions on S^(d-1) with K_ij = attention weights (Lohe flow); heads are couplings, not oscillators, and the statistic is one scalar per head — no head-head or neuron-neuron matrix exists anywhere in the repo, and c_bar_h conflates coupling magnitude with phase alignment (K*R*cos), so a head writing new orthogonal information reads as 'dead'. The only well-posed map the equation can generate is the head-head write-back cosine matrix K_hh' tested against joint ablation with a mean-ablation control (chain 3 D-K, ~30 min) — and it is gated on D1 finding the dead set special at all, which the base-rate data predicts it will not. If D1's null holds the SNN import must source coupling from something causal (ablation Jacobians), not cosine.
- 'Two or more tiny models are exponentially smarter than the sum' has no measured instance in the 2023-2026 literature the trove read, and should be restated before its vision node is minted. Coverage is log-linear in samples over four orders of magnitude (Large Language Monkeys); error correlation caps Kish effective votes at ~2-3 regardless of ensemble size; Self-MoA beats heterogeneous mixing; debate is a martingale and HURTS at the owner's scale (Llama-3.2-1B GSM8K -9 pp, 94% miscorrection — the reader had misattributed this paper); all-8B MoA 51.2% vs SC@10 68.7%, and it is small PROPOSERS not aggregators that wreck MoA (Wunderlich Table 2, which the ensembles reader inverted). The only exponential available is Condorcet convergence, which needs per-member accuracy > 0.5 AND independent errors; models agree 60% of the time when both err. The supported half is homogeneous sampling + an external verifier on verifiable tasks — and the headline '0.5B > GPT-4o' depends on a 7B PRM (12x the policy) the town cannot run on CPU, at N=256 budgets that cost 450-900 CPU-h to reproduce here. Restate Vision 3 as 'marginal accuracy per CPU-second beyond the best member, with CI' (CP9) and spend on verifiers and routing, not debate or diverse-model mixing.
- The byte-neuron / SNN thread is behind dense prior art AND the owner's own negative results, and is currently framed as both a Vision-1 substrate and a Vision-2/3 lever when it has earned neither. Prior art: DWN (ICML 2024) already trains 6-input LUT neurons with learnable wiring (MNIST 98.31% at 23.4 KiB), difflogic, Tsetlin machines, rank-order coding, Numenta HTM/Kanerva SDM; recurrent bit-logic nets reach 4.39 BLEU on WMT14 vs ~27 for a transformer, and DWN's fully-connected CIFAR-10 is 57% — LUT/bit nets lose badly as soon as the task needs composition, which is the owner's target. The trove: 'continuous (graded) input consistently outperforms spike mode across all V2 variants' and 'stacking specialist micro-networks at the prediction level is a dead end' (snn-architecture.md, MEASURED this session); spike-count readouts killed > 85% of V4 runs; the 96.10% MNIST baseline uses a summed-membrane readout a 1-bit-out neuron cannot expose; the corpus' 'learnable timescales' headline is an epoch-count artefact and its 'inhibition has no effect' result is a wiring bug. The 242 binary-GOPS popcount figure measures a dense BNN, not the gather-bound LUT neuron (32 ms/tick at N=8192). Honest scoping: three kill-tests that need no models or installs (chain 2 E3/E2/E1, < 2 CPU-h total, the 8 GB box is idle), one genuinely novel claim (bit ORDER as code — no prior art either way), and a stated training rule (a gradient-free local rule is the only path that makes the 'integer CPU' claim true end-to-end and nobody has costed it). Route accuracy/cooperation questions to chains 7/9, not to the neuron.
- The looped / depth-recurrence thread was briefed from the wrong lineage and with a dead precision plan. The Recurrent Looped Transformer (Zhang, 2026-09-12) is a design spec with ZERO experiments (rlt.txt MEASURED: 'does not report measured efficiency or scaling results'), no code, no parameter count; it is the Feedback-Transformer / cross-token lineage (decoder applied ONCE per token, no adaptive loop count), not the Ouro/Huginn intra-token lineage the 'how many loops' framing assumes. Ouro-2.6B at 4 loops runs ~192 layer-evals/token — roughly a 10B-dense model's FLOPs (ESTIMATE): looping saves parameters/RAM, not compute, so for Vision 1 it is a footprint win only; its 'last-step KV reuse < 0.15% loss' is the favourable benchmark (MATH-500 loses 2.0 pts); loop degradation is ~0.6-0.75 pt/extra loop, not 1-3%. bf16 matmul on this box is 2 GFLOPS vs 50 fp32 (MEASURED by the critic), so every 'bf16 on CPU' plan is dead on arrival; Ouro-1.4B needs transformers < 4.56 + trust_remote_code and int8 dynamic quant (fp32 5.6 GB does not fit), and its remote code is unverified on torch-CPU. The one CPU-specific reason to care — cache residency of the shared block — has never been measured by anyone and costs 30 minutes (chain 6 C1); quantization compounding across loops is unmeasured anywhere and gates both boxes (C3). Training the toy pair on CPU is ~100+ CPU-h (ESTIMATE); it is the single correct use of the month's Camber budget (C2, 25-30 min, ~$1.50). RLT's actual novelty — cross-token state feedback, one output per tick, gated merge — is the closest thing in the survey to the owner's byte-neuron thread and should be tested as an arm of C2, not as 'the wrong lineage'.
- 'Fold the KV-cache and streaming papers in' imports GPU literature at 4B-32B with zero CPU or sub-4B numbers: H2O, SnapKV, PyramidKV, TriAttention, Random Attention are none of them in llama.cpp (only a design proposal, #28170), Random Attention drops 6.3-6.5 pts vs full cache at 4x (the column the reader omitted), TurboQuant is forks-only with its main fork archived (though the reader's 'CPU fallback is slow' and 'Metal 8x slower' claims were superseded numbers from the same source), and the cross-layer KV-sharing study's configs are uptrained on 100B tokens — orders of magnitude beyond 3 GPU-h/month. What the literature never says and this box can test: if decode is bandwidth-bound, KV quant is a SPEED lever (asimddp gives int8 sdot, so q8_0 KV should be FASTER than f16 and q4_0 V no faster); at the owner's real prompt lengths (2-8K) KV is < weights until ~5K tokens on a 0.6B and the binding wall-clock cost is PREFILL (40 s-3 min for 8K), so prompt caching and the hybrid GDN architecture (12 KB/token, 9.3x less than dense, constant-state — the owner's compression thread made concrete) matter more than any eviction method. Use the levers that ship in the runtime and measure them (chains 1 and 8) before anything that needs a fork.
- 'Many hypotheses chased in parallel' collides with the box and with two unbudgeted gates. MEASURED 02:31Z: load 16.6 on 4 cores, swap 239 MB free of 4 GB, pgmajfault 2.03M, ~7 GB available of 23 — firefox/ffmpeg/VNC hold 10+ GB RSS; every microbenchmark the readers cite (242 GOPS, 54 f32 GOPS, 20.1 GB/s, 2-3x encoder jitter, a 61 s test that should take 30) is a load artifact, and the critic reproduced f32 4-thread at 9.9 vs 54 GOPS. There is no LLM runtime at all (ollama purged 2026-09-13 on a disk GO; the local stack that existed produced zero graph experiments — a runtime that mints no experiment node on first run gets purged as dead weight again), no GGUF, no transformers. Step 0 everywhere is a llama.cpp build + ~5 GB of GGUFs + one transformers install decision — director decisions, not research steps — that gate ~60% of all proposed experiments and were being re-litigated per seed. Thinking mode must be forced off or every token estimate is 3-15x wrong; 200-item slices give SE ~3.5 pts so every 1-2 pt falsifier is unfalsifiable without paired McNemar/bootstrap and a power statement. The cheaper path: serial runs on the idle 8 GB box for anything under 5 GB, this box only after tenants are paused with owner say-so, and every row carrying load, MemAvailable, faults and build hash.
- 'Fine-tune lattice slots on the corpus this graph already holds' presumes labels with variance. MEASURED: 170 verdict nodes are 34 proved, ~130 lean-proved, 6 lean-disproved, 0 disproved; the gap-spotter has roughly three loops of COMPLETE.md category labels; goal:g14.1 correctly schedules no run. Any slot trained on this learns the prior. The 20-minute kill-test (chain 4 V2-C1) decides it with data already on disk; the first learned slot, if any, should be a verifier/router trained on per-item correctness the town generates itself (chains 7/9), and the graph should start writing hard negatives (real disproved verdicts) before scheduling any fine-tune. Camber cannot fix a label problem.
- Camber's 3 GPU-hours/month ($9 at $3/h; XS GPU type unknown — nobody has ever run nvidia-smi) buys exactly three decisive things: the looped-depth toy burst (25-30 min), PRM-7B scoring of stored samples (20-30 min), and one LoRA/distill job on the 4B (30 min, conditional). Nothing else in any vision needs a GPU; spending minutes on inference benchmarks, KLD references, or the 27B line is waste, and goal:g14.3 as charted reasoned from a false 50B premise. The gate lifted 2026-09-07 was held deliberately under delegated authority until L3 closed (not 'found by accident', as one reader put it) — but six days of zero research motion on a $9/month budget is a cadence problem the ONE-pi-round-at-a-time rule should fix, not a spend problem.

A Tsetlin-vs-DWN-EFD head-to-head on one bit task is the natural first experiment and is absent.

- Unverified numeric claims still carried: HF 'Llama-3.2-1B beats 8B / 3B beats 70B on MATH-500' (Space would not render; N, PRM, method unfetched); MoDr '+7.2%' (third-party notes); the DWN ATmega328P pair (97.9% @ 16.5 inf/s) and difflogic '97.62% @ 23.4 KiB' (paper shows other configs); T2MLR 'Jacobi iterations' (not in the abstract); q5_1/q4_0 KLD 0.017557 (not found in #23470); 'Gemma 3 KV to ~1/6' (no source); Skywork PRM-1.5B int8 RAM fit on CPU; Weaver's 400M distilled verifier — is the checkpoint public?

- Where 0.679 = 4/r comes from: r is never defined in paper.tex; the referenced Kosterlitz 1973 / Kuramoto 1984 do not obviously yield it; chi_c = 0.96 may be fitted on GPT-2 and declared universal. Also unanswered: why the reference vector is the pre-LN residual rather than the normalized state the theory is about, and whether the massive-activation/sink direction dominates late-layer cosines (which makes any residual-stream order parameter R_l ill-posed as stated).
- Whether tau_death applies to linear-attention (Gated DeltaNet) layers at all: the derivation assumes softmax heads as couplings on the LayerNorm sphere and was validated only on softmax families, yet the on-charter Qwen3.5 ladder is 75% GDN. Deliberately not promoted (the 27B is off-charter), but it is the one open, cheap, genuinely new question about the equation on the models the town will actually run.
- Gemma 4 E2B's speed tier (5.1B real params with per-layer embeddings at 3.11 GB Q4 vs 2.3B compute) is a one-line llama-bench comparison against Qwen3.5-2B that appears only inside V2-A3; and Gemma-3-1B SWA vs --swa-full (kv seed 8) is parked pending a load test.
- Statistical power is absent from most falsifiers across all panels: 200 items -> SE ~3.5 pts; 100 items -> ~5; many thresholds are 1-2 pts. Every chain needs paired per-item tests and a stated power, or half the falsifiers are unfalsifiable at their N.
- Licensing and provenance hygiene: the dead-head method is PolyForm-NC + patent-pending (research fine, nothing product-shaped); the trove clones exist only in an ephemeral scratchpad with no node recording URLs + commit hashes; the runtime and install decisions (CP0) are recorded in no node.

## READINGS + CRITIQUES (one per source)

### dead-head-paper
SOURCE: /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/scratchpad/dead-head (Sharpe 2026, "Coherence-Guided Dead-Head Identification in Frozen Transformers", git HEAD 583962f 2026-04-07; paper.tex, README.md, AGENTS.md, data/*.json, supporting/*.json, scripts/coherence_anatomy_scan.py, scripts/98_coherence_pruning_harness.py, Makefile). Read 2026-09-13.

The paper defines one observable per attention head: s_h(i) = sum_j A_ij^(h) V_h(x_j) projected through head h's slice of W_O into d_model space (the head's write-back), c_h(i) = cos(s_h(i), x_i) where x_i is the block-INPUT residual (pre-LN, hooked at block forward-pre), and c̄_h = mean over positions and calibration batches. Dead iff c̄_h < tau_death(d) = chi_c/sqrt(d_model), chi_c = 0.679*sqrt(2) = 0.96025, AND (harness) below-threshold for >=3 consecutive batches (scanner: >=50% of batches), AND not protected (boundary: first 1-2 + last 1 layer; bridge veto: per-layer Fiedler-cut bridge mass > mean+2sigma and top-3). chi_c comes from an XY/BKT bond-death condition cos(dtheta)=4/r=0.679 with r unspecified, divided by the S^1 fluctuation scale 1/sqrt(2), times the S^(d-1) scale 1/sqrt(d). Normalized coordinate z_h = sqrt(d)*c̄_h; dead iff z_h < 0.96.

Measured results (data/*.json, all run on Apple MPS, seq_len 128, 8 calib + 8 eval sequences of TinyStories): GPT-2 42/144 dead, precision 95.2%; GPT-2 Medium 114/384, 98.2%; Qwen2.5-0.5B 157/336, 95.5%; SmolLM2-360M 261/480 (paper table says 234), 99.6%; OpenLLaMA-7B 286/1024, 100%. "Precision" = fraction of dead-marked heads whose single-head ablation delta-loss <= 1% of baseline loss. The paper concedes base rates of 93-99.9% make precision non-discriminative; my re-analysis of the bundled per-head data confirms: Spearman(z_h, delta_loss) is 0.02-0.29 and AUC(z_h -> unsafe) is 0.43 / 0.17 / 0.47 on GPT-2 / GPT-2M / Qwen0.5B (chance or worse), 0.83 / 0.89 on SmolLM2 / OpenLLaMA (10 and 1 unsafe heads). On Qwen0.5B alive heads are 95.3% safe vs dead 95.7% safe; z<-3 heads are only 85% safe. The "scaling collapse" holds by construction (threshold defined as 0.96/sqrt(d)); distributions do not collapse (std z = 2.9-6.0). Removing all dead heads at once: dead-only eval delta-loss +2.84 (GPT-2), +1.94, +1.13 (Qwen), +0.87 (SmolLM2), +1.19 (7B) — paper text says +1.4 to +2.1. Structural removal gives no speedup at seq<=256 (GPT-2 Medium -46% at 128 on MPS), +14-23% at 1024 on GPT-2; Qwen/SmolLM2 timings are masked proxies (no param reduction). Only GQA Level-2 KV-group compaction on SmolLM2 gives real gains: -27.5% KV bytes, +31% prefill at 1024, but delta-loss +0.53 (30% relative).

Compute: the standalone scanner is one forward pass per calibration batch (32 x 128 tokens, WikiText-2), default --device cpu, fp32; Qwen2.5-0.5B weights are 1.976 GB. Everything runs on CPU; no CUDA was used anywhere in the bundle. The full harness imports an external `coherence_lattice` package not in the repo, so only the scanner and random-init control are runnable as-is.

Owner's oscillator idea: the paper's own map puts oscillators at TOKEN positions with K_ij = attention weights (Lohe flow on S^(d-1)); c̄_h is not a coupling strength K but an order-parameter-weighted alignment K*R*cos(psi-theta): it conflates coupling magnitude with current phase-locking, so heads writing orthogonal (new) information read as "dead" — consistent with the null AUC. Precise conditions for a Kuramoto map are given in the seeds.

KEY FACTS:
- MEASURED (paper.tex L38-46, L232-247): c_h(i)=cos(s_h(i), x_i), s_h(i)=sum_j A_ij^(h) V_h(x_j) via head slice of W_O; tau_death(d)=chi_c/sqrt(d), chi_c=0.679*sqrt(2)=0.96025; z_h=sqrt(d)*c_bar_h; dead iff z_h<0.96 (plus protections).
- MEASURED (scripts/coherence_anatomy_scan.py L150-206): reference vector is the block INPUT residual (pre-LayerNorm) captured by register_forward_pre_hook on the block; write-back = einsum(head_view, W_O slice) captured by pre-hook on the output projection; cosine averaged over batch and positions.
- MEASURED (paper.tex L186-197): BKT constant cos(dtheta)_death = 4/r = 0.679 with r left as an unspecified 'lattice-dependent scale factor' (r=5.89 implied). chi_c is asserted, not derived from first principles in this document.
- MEASURED (data/*_redundancy*.json clr_theory): dead/total, precision: gpt2 42/144 0.952; gpt2-medium 114/384 0.982; Qwen2.5-0.5B 157/336 0.955; SmolLM2-360M 261/480 0.996 (paper Table 1 prints 234); open_llama_7b 286/1024 1.000. Gemma 3 4B 30/272 has NO artifact in data/.
- MEASURED (data/*_redundancy*.json): ground-truth 'safe' = single-head ablation delta_loss <= 1% of baseline calibration loss (0.0276 gpt2, 0.0229 Qwen0.5B); seq_len 128, 8 calibration + 8 eval sequences, device 'mps' on every artifact.
- MEASURED (my re-analysis of bundled decisions+head_results, non-protected pool): Spearman(z_h, delta_loss) = +0.02 gpt2, +0.22 gpt2-m, +0.29 Qwen0.5B, +0.22 SmolLM2, +0.10 7B; AUC(z_h -> unsafe) = 0.43, 0.17, 0.47, 0.83, 0.89. On Qwen0.5B: z<0.96 dead precision 0.957 vs z>=0.96 alive safe-rate 0.953; z<-3 heads only 0.852 safe.
- MEASURED (data/base_rate_analysis.json): random-selection precision 93.2/99.4/95.5/97.8/99.9% vs threshold 95.2/98.2/95.5/99.6/100%; lift 1.02/0.99/1.00/1.02/1.00. Paper Sec 'Base-Rate Analysis' concedes this.
- MEASURED (clr_theory.eval_summary vs baseline_eval): removing ALL dead heads simultaneously: gpt2 2.739->5.579 (+2.84), gpt2-m 2.515->4.459 (+1.94), Qwen0.5B 2.246->3.378 (+1.13), SmolLM2 2.115->2.986 (+0.87), 7B 1.857->3.051 (+1.19). Paper text claims '+1.4 to +2.1'.
- MEASURED (supporting/repeatability_summary_v1.json): 3 seeds: gpt2 dead 42/47/46 precision 0.952-0.957; Qwen0.5B dead 156/159/156 precision 0.929-0.981, dead-only delta-loss 1.13/2.08/1.75 (std 0.48).
- MEASURED (data/gpt2_structural_timing_v1.json, gpt2_medium_structural_timing_v1.json; MPS): structural dead-head removal speedup gpt2: -9.6% @128, +1.2% @256, +16% @512, +14% @1024; gpt2-medium: -46% @128, -42% @256, -14% @512, +10% @1024. Qwen0.5B and SmolLM2 timing files are 'masked_proxy' (parameter_count unchanged) and show -3% to -21% (no speedup).
- MEASURED (data/smollm2_level2_kv_compaction_v1.json): SmolLM2 GQA g=3: 44/160 KV groups fully dead, KV bytes/token 81920->59392 (-27.5%), prefill speedup +4%/-15%/+15%/+31% at 128/256/512/1024, delta-loss +0.38/+0.44/+0.50/+0.53. open_llama_7b (MHA, g=1): -27.9% KV, speedup +4 to +12%, delta-loss +1.03 to +1.22.
- MEASURED (paper.tex Sec GQA): Qwen2.5-0.5B has 14 q-heads / 2 kv-heads (g=7); only 3/48 KV groups fully dead -> Level-2 compaction nearly useless on this model.
- MEASURED (paper.tex Table randinit; scripts/98_random_init_control.py): std(z_h) trained vs random-init: gpt2 4.17/0.47, gpt2-m 5.92/0.55, Qwen0.5B 2.71/0.20, SmolLM2 3.84/0.22 (ratio 9-18x). Random-init below the geometric null of 1.0 because W_O init is small.
- MEASURED (redundancy_pass): second pass on alive heads uses per-layer head-write-back Gram matrix (collect_head_contribution_gram, harness L927-968), pairwise threshold 4/sqrt(d) (0.134 Qwen); redundant precision 0.885/1.0/0.952/0.895/0.986.
- MEASURED (harness L768-814): per-head token-token attention matrix is turned into a graph: diag zeroed, top-k sparsified, symmetrized, normalized Laplacian lambda_2 and Fiedler-cut 'bridge mass' -> bridge veto. This is the only place the paper treats attention as an explicit coupling graph.
- MEASURED (paper.tex Discussion 'Dead Heads Are Mildly Toxic'): on Gemma 3 4B, SVD rank-64 filtering of the 30 dead heads' output improved loss by -0.075 (-1.5%) — no artifact bundled.
- MEASURED (data/qwen25_05b_timing_proxy_v1.json): Qwen2.5-0.5B parameter_bytes 1,976,131,072 (fp32) — fits in the 7 GB free on the A1 box. Scanner default --device cpu, dtype float32, 32 calibration batches x 128 tokens from WikiText-2 (needs `datasets` download).
- MEASURED (scripts/98_coherence_pruning_harness.py L34-37): full harness imports coherence_lattice.{analytics.trajectory,core,metrics.canon_metrics} from a parent monorepo NOT in this repo; the harness is not runnable standalone. Scanner and random-init control are standalone (torch, transformers, numpy, datasets).
- ESTIMATE: Qwen2.5-0.5B forward on 128 tokens ~130 GFLOP; 4-core arm64-N1 fp32 PyTorch ~30-45 GFLOPS effective -> ~3-4 s/batch; scanner (32 batches) ~2-3 min; full single-head ablation ground truth (336 heads x 8 seqs) ~2.5-3 h CPU. No GPU needed.
- MEASURED (LICENSE, NOTICE): PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0; US provisional patent 64/031,983 filed 2026-04-07 — research use fine, commercial use needs a license.
- MEASURED (paper.tex Table paradigm-map, Sec 2.2): the paper's oscillators are TOKEN positions; K_ij = attention weights; Lohe flow dx_i/dt = sum_j K_ij [x_j - (x_i.x_j) x_i]; MLPs are explicitly 'intra-oscillator modes', not couplings. Heads are couplings, not oscillators.

Reusable as the coupling-measurement kernel for any seed below (add a hook that stores the full write-back tensors instead of only the cosine mean).

L927-968 collect_head_contribution_gram(): per-layer head x head Gram of write-backs.

- Same per-head JSONs for gpt2 (144), gpt2-medium (384), SmolLM2-360M (480), open_llama_7b (1024) under /tmp/.../dead-head/data/.
