- /tmp/.../dead-head/data/threshold_transfer_summary.json — 5-model table with exact tau_artifact, dead/protected/alive counts, combined_delta_loss (dead+redundant removal): 1.85/1.43/2.12/2.05/1.60.

FAILURES/LESSONS:
- The headline 95-100% 'precision' is base-rate: random head selection scores 93-99.9% on the same pool (paper's own base_rate_analysis.json). Re-analysis shows z_h has chance-level AUC (0.17-0.47) for predicting ablation-unsafe heads on the three small models incl. Qwen2.5-0.5B. Do not adopt tau_death as a pruning criterion on tiny models; treat it as a descriptive statistic.
- The 'scaling collapse' is tautological: z_h=sqrt(d)c_bar_h makes the threshold line collapse by definition; the head distributions do not collapse (std z 2.7-6.0, dead fraction 11-54% across models).
- The BKT constant 0.679 = 4/r is not derived here (r unspecified). chi_c=0.96 should be treated as a fitted-looking constant until a derivation is found; the town should not build on 'zero-parameter' as an established fact.
- Paper prose disagrees with its own artifacts in places: SmolLM2 dead 234 (table) vs 261 (JSON, timing, base-rate); simultaneous-removal delta-loss '+1.4 to +2.1' vs artifacts +0.87 to +2.84; GPT-2 '41 killed' vs dead_count 42. Always read the JSON, not the table.
- Removing dead heads gives no CPU/short-sequence speedup and usually slows things (structural removal on GPT-2 Medium -46% at seq 128 on MPS; masked proxies never speed up). Real gains only appear from KV-group compaction on GQA with small group size (SmolLM2 g=3), and Qwen2.5-0.5B (g=7) is nearly immune. For this town's target (Qwen-class 0.5B on CPU) head pruning is the wrong lever; KV-cache levers are the right one.
- The full harness is NOT reproducible from this repo (imports coherence_lattice from a private monorepo); only the scanner and random-init script run. Budget reimplementation time for the Gram/graph pieces (they are short, pure numpy).
- The coherence observable conflates coupling strength with phase alignment (K*R*cos). A head that writes new, orthogonal information scores ~0 and is called 'dead'; the paper's own 'dead heads are mildly toxic / SVD rank-64 improves loss' result and the catastrophic joint removal both indicate these heads carry signal. Any oscillator map built on c_bar_h alone will inherit this confound.
- Calibration mismatch: artifacts used TinyStories val text at seq 128 with 8 sequences; the scanner uses WikiText-2 with 32 batches. Dead sets vary by seed (+-3 heads GPT-2, +-2 Qwen) and dead-only delta-loss varies by 0.5 nats on Qwen across seeds — small-sample noise is large; use >=32 sequences.
- Licensing: PolyForm Noncommercial + patent pending — fine for the town's research, but not for shipping a product built on this exact method without a license.

SEEDS:
- CLAIM The coherence c_bar_h confounds coupling magnitude with phase: decomposing each head into write-norm ratio r_h = E_i ||s_h(i)||/||x_i|| and alignment c_h separates ablation-unsafe heads (large r_h, |c_h| small = orthogonal information writers) from truly inert heads (small r_h), and AUC(r_h -> unsafe) > 0.7 on Qwen2.5-0.5B whereas AUC(z_h -> unsafe) = 0.47. // FALSIFIER AUC of r_h (or r_h*(1-|c_h|)) for predicting the bundled unsafe labels on Qwen2.5-0.5B is <= 0.55, i.e. no better than z_h. // EXP Modify coherence_anatomy_scan.py hooks to also record per-head write-back norm relative to residual norm; scan Qwen2.5-0.5B fp32 on CPU (32 x 128 tokens); join with data/qwen25_05b_head336_...json delta_loss labels; compute AUCs. Optionally re-run 336 single-head ablations on WikiText-2 to remove the calibration-text mismatch. // COST ~5 min CPU for scan; +2.5-3 h CPU if ablation labels are regenerated; RAM ~3 GB
- CLAIM A head-level Kuramoto coupling matrix K_hh' = E_i cos(s_h(i), s_h'(i)) (same token position, within and across layers) predicts ablation interaction: pairs with high |K| show sub-additive joint damage (redundant/synchronized cluster) and pairs with strongly negative K show super-additive damage; pairs with |K| < 4/sqrt(d) behave additively. // FALSIFIER Joint-ablation excess delta_loss(a,b) - delta_loss(a) - delta_loss(b) has |Spearman| < 0.15 with K_ab over 100 sampled pairs. // EXP Hook all 336 head write-backs on Qwen2.5-0.5B for 8 x 128-token sequences (336 x 1024 x 896 fp32 ~ 1.2 GB; or stream per layer), build the 336 x 336 K matrix, sample 50 high-|K|, 50 near-zero-K pairs, run 100 two-head-mask forward evaluations on 8 sequences. // COST ~15-20 min CPU, <4 GB RAM
- CLAIM For a depth-recurrent (looped) block the paper's map becomes well-posed: re-applying a fixed span of Qwen2.5-0.5B layers t = 1..8 times yields per-head coherence trajectories c_h(t) that converge (phase-lock) and a sphere order parameter R(t) = ||mean_i xhat_i(t)|| that increases monotonically, so R(t) saturation can serve as a halting criterion for looped inference; heads with c_h(t) < tau for all t are the loop's dead couplings. // FALSIFIER c_h(t) does not converge (oscillates with amplitude > 0.5 in z units) or R(t) is non-monotonic while loss stays within +0.3 of baseline; or looping any span by >=2 raises loss by >1 nat (no useful looped regime exists without training). // EXP Wrap Qwen2.5-0.5B forward to repeat layers [8..15] k times (k=1..8) with hooks recording c_h per iteration and hidden-state mean-direction norm; evaluate loss on 8 x 128 tokens for each k. // COST ~10 min CPU
- CLAIM The causal (Jacobian-like) coupling K_{h->h'} = E ||delta s_h'(i)|| when head h is ablated is sparse and heavy-tailed, and tau-dead heads have outgoing causal coupling comparable to alive heads — explaining why simultaneous removal is catastrophic while single removal is safe. // FALSIFIER Mean outgoing causal coupling of tau-dead heads is < 25% of that of alive heads (then dead heads really are decoupled and catastrophe must come from elsewhere, e.g. norm/LayerNorm statistics). // EXP For each of the 336 heads in Qwen2.5-0.5B, run one masked forward on 4 x 128 tokens with hooks capturing every downstream head's write-back; compare to unablated write-backs; aggregate per source head. // COST ~1-1.5 h CPU (336 forwards on 512 tokens each), ~3 GB RAM
- CLAIM The tau-dead set has no special structure on Qwen2.5-0.5B: removing 157 uniformly random non-protected heads produces the same eval delta-loss (within 1 std of the 3-seed spread, 0.48) as removing the 157 tau-dead heads (+1.13). // FALSIFIER Random 157-head removal delta-loss is > 2 std (~1.0 nat) worse than the tau-dead removal across 10 random draws. // EXP Reuse the bundled dead set (decisions[].dead) from data/qwen25_05b_...json; run 10 random masks and the dead mask through Qwen2.5-0.5B on 16 x 128 WikiText-2 tokens. // COST ~10 min CPU
- CLAIM Low-rank (SVD) filtering of tau-dead heads' W_O slices improves Qwen2.5-0.5B loss (replicating the Gemma 3 4B -1.5% 'mildly toxic' result on a 0.5B model), with an optimal rank in 8-32 out of 64. // FALSIFIER No rank in {4,8,16,32,48} yields delta-loss < 0 on a 32-sequence WikiText-2 eval. // EXP SVD each dead head's 64x896 W_O slice, truncate, evaluate loss; also try the opposite (filtering alive heads) as control. // COST ~15 min CPU
- CLAIM For the byte-neuron SNN, the concentration-of-measure derivation needs d >> 1 and cannot apply to an 8-bit fan-in; substituting time-averaging for dimension gives a well-posed dead-synapse rule: coherence(u->v) = (1/T) sum_t (2*[b_u(t)=b_v(t+1)]-1), null std 1/sqrt(T), dead iff |coherence| < 0.96/sqrt(T). Pruning synapses below this rule in a random recurrent byte-net leaves output bit-stream statistics (entropy rate, pairwise MI to inputs) unchanged versus pruning an equal number of random synapses which degrades them. // FALSIFIER Output entropy-rate / input-MI change after rule-based pruning is not smaller than after random pruning of the same count, at T in {64,256,1024} and network sizes 256-4096 neurons. // EXP Pure numpy: simulate N byte-neurons with bit-subscription fan-in, compute time-averaged bit-agreement coherence per synapse, prune, measure bit-stream statistics; no model download. // COST ~2-5 min CPU, <1 GB RAM
- CLAIM On the paper's own token-oscillator map, the per-layer synchronization order parameter of the residual stream R_l = ||mean_i xhat_i^(l)|| is driven by the layer's summed head coupling sum_h c_bar_h * mean_ij A_ij^(h), and layers whose predicted vs measured R increment disagree are exactly the boundary layers the paper has to hand-protect (first 1-2, last 1). // FALSIFIER Correlation between predicted and measured delta R_l over the 22 interior layers of Qwen2.5-0.5B is < 0.3, or boundary layers are not outliers. // EXP Run Qwen2.5-0.5B with output_hidden_states and output_attentions on 16 x 128 tokens (eager attention), compute R_l per layer and the coupling proxy; one forward pass set. // COST ~5 min CPU

OPEN:
- Where does 0.679 = 4/r come from? r is never defined in paper.tex; the referenced Kosterlitz 1973 / Kuramoto 1984 do not obviously yield it. Is chi_c actually fitted on GPT-2 and then declared universal?
- Why is the reference vector the pre-LayerNorm block input rather than the normalized state on S^(d-1) that the theory is about? Does using the post-LN state change the dead set on Qwen2.5-0.5B (pre-LN residual norms grow with depth)?
- Does the near-chance AUC on the small models replicate with WikiText-2 calibration and 32+ sequences (bundled labels come from only 8 TinyStories sequences at seq 128)?
- Gemma 3 4B row (30/272 dead, 100% precision) and the REAP-25B / Gemma 4 E4B numbers have no artifacts in the repo — unverifiable from here.
- What is the sign structure? On Qwen2.5-0.5B strongly negative z (anti-aligned writers) are the least safe heads (85%); are negative-z heads a distinct functional class (erasure/attenuation heads) rather than dead?
- For the town's KV thread: can per-group partial death (fraction of dead q-heads in a GQA group) drive per-group KV quantization bit-width instead of all-or-nothing group deletion, which is nearly useless at g=7 (Qwen2.5-0.5B: 3/48 groups)?
- Is there a public depth-recurrent model small enough for CPU (Huginn-0125 is 3.5B; bf16 ~7 GB, marginal on this box) or must looping be emulated on Qwen2.5-0.5B untrained?
- Owner's 'qwen3.8 50b' model id was not resolved in this reading (out of this task's scope); needs a separate lookup.
- Licensing: does the town intend anything beyond noncommercial research with this exact method (patent pending 64/031,983)?

CRITIC (confidence 0.82):
unsupported:
- 'Heads that write new, orthogonal information score ~0 and are called dead ... consistent with the null AUC' — interpretive. The near-chance AUC is equally explained by label noise: 'unsafe' = single-head ZERO-ablation delta-loss > 1% of baseline (0.023-0.028 nats) measured on only 8 x 128 = 1024 TinyStories tokens; that threshold is inside token-sampling noise, so the labels themselves are unreliable. The reader never quantifies this.
- The 'dead heads carry signal' argument leans twice on the Gemma 3 4B SVD result (-0.075 loss, -1.5%), which the paper itself labels 'preliminary', has no artifact, no seed spread, and no control. MEASURED only as a sentence in paper.tex L726-732; it cannot support a mechanism claim.
- 'AUC 0.17 on GPT-2 Medium (chance or worse)' rests on n=2 unsafe heads in a pool of 349 (MEASURED: unsafe_in_pool=2). Also GPT-2 has 8, OpenLLaMA 1. Only Qwen (13) and SmolLM2 (10) have enough positives to say anything, and the reader's summary line 'null AUC' over-generalizes: Fisher exact on SmolLM2 dead(1/261 unsafe) vs alive(9/188) gives p=0.0022 (MEASURED, my computation) — a real lift on that model; GPT-2 p=0.71, Qwen p=1.0.
- All CPU cost figures (scan ~2-3 min, ablation ground truth ~2.5-3 h, seeds' est_cost) are ESTIMATES with no measurement on the A1 box; the ~30-45 GFLOPS effective figure is plausible for fp32 PyTorch on 4x arm64-N1 but unmeasured.
- Seed 3 presupposes that a 'useful looped regime' can exist when re-applying untrained Qwen2.5-0.5B layers 8..15 up to 8 times; the falsifier (loss > +1 nat) is near-certain to trigger, so the seed encodes a prediction the reader already expects to fail.
- Seed 7's dead-synapse rule |coherence| < 0.96/sqrt(T) transplants a constant that even the source paper does not derive (0.679 = 4/r, r undefined) into a byte-neuron setting with no task; 'bit-stream statistics unchanged' on a random recurrent net measures nothing about usefulness.
- Reader states the 'scaling collapse' is 'by construction'. Correct that the threshold line collapses tautologically, but the paper's actual empirical claim is that dead/alive MEDIANS in z separate consistently (dead median <= 0, alive > 1.86, paper.tex L423-425); the reader did not check that claim, which is the one non-tautological part.
errors:
- Minor numeric drift: summary says 'std z = 2.9-6.0', key_facts say '2.7-6.0'; MEASURED from artifacts: 2.71 (Qwen) to 5.92 (GPT-2M). Qwen dead precision quoted as 0.957 in one place, 0.955 elsewhere; artifact dead_precision = 0.9554 (150/157).
- Reader missed a second paper-vs-artifact discrepancy on OpenLLaMA 7B: paper.tex L418-419 says 338 below threshold, 273 killed, 34 protected; the artifact has 338 below threshold, 286 dead, 68 protected (MEASURED). The reader only flagged GPT-2 41 vs 42 and SmolLM2 234 vs 261.
- Reader says the artifacts' GPT-2M AUC = 0.17 shows chance-or-worse; the honest statement is 'the 2 unsafe heads on GPT-2M both have LOW z (unsafe_coupling_mean = -1.78 in base_rate_analysis.json, i.e. inside the dead range)' — directionally opposite to the paper, but n=2.
- The reader's 'ground-truth safe = delta_loss <= 1% of baseline calibration loss' is right, but omits that the ablation is ZERO-ablation of the head's o_proj input slice (harness L610-625: masked[:, :, head_idx, :] = 0.0), not mean-ablation. This matters for every downstream claim about 'catastrophic joint removal'.
- No error in the equations, hook placement (block forward-pre for the reference, o_proj forward-pre for the write-back), CLI defaults (--device cpu, float32, n-cal 32, seq-len 128, boundary 2, consistency 0.5), the repeatability and timing numbers, or the KV compaction numbers — all verified.
missing:
- supporting/qwen3_8b_role_aware_comparison.json is never mentioned. MEASURED: model Qwen/Qwen3-8B, baseline loss 3.1365; uniform GPTQ 4.25-bit: loss 3.298 (+0.339); role-aware attn=4/ffn_read=4/ffn_write=6 bits: loss 3.174 (+0.0375), 4.24 effective bits, 3.2x compression; attn=5/ffn_read=5/ffn_write=7: +0.0039 at 4.97 bits. This is (a) the likeliest resolution of the owner's 'qwen3.8' (Qwen3-8B), (b) the only result in the bundle that bears on CPU tiny-model inference (weight quantization is THE CPU lever, head pruning is not), and (c) unverifiable — no code, no seeds, one number per row.
- Zero-ablation confound. The cheapest and highest-value control on the whole paper is mean-ablation (replace each dead head's write-back by its calibration mean) versus zero-ablation of the same 157 heads on Qwen2.5-0.5B: one extra forward set, ~5 min CPU. If the +1.13 nat 'catastrophe' mostly vanishes, dead heads are a mean-shift/LayerNorm-statistics artifact, the 'mildly toxic' story is bias correction, and the whole coupling/oscillator interpretation loses its main evidence. The paper's own follow-on list names 'mean-compensated pruning' (L716-719) as future work, i.e. it has not been done.
- Non-monotone damage that the reader had in hand but did not read: removing dead+redundant heads hurts LESS than removing dead heads alone on the GPT-2 family — GPT-2 1.85 vs 2.84, GPT-2M 1.43 vs 1.94 (threshold_transfer_summary.json combined_delta_loss vs clr_theory eval); same in the timing JSONs at seq 128 (GPT-2 dead_only +3.14, dead_plus_redundant +1.96). Removing more heads reducing loss is a strong sign the damage is distributional, not informational.
- Parameter savings from head pruning are tiny even structurally: GPT-2 124.4M -> 116.2M (-6.6%) for 29% of heads; GPT-2M 354.8M -> 324.9M (-8.4%) for 30% of heads (MEASURED, timing JSONs). MLP dominates; this is the quantitative reason head pruning cannot help a CPU box, independent of the MPS timing noise.
- The paper text never names its calibration corpus, sequence counts, or device; TinyStories val.txt, 8+8 sequences, and mps are recoverable only from JSON paths. TinyStories is a children's-story corpus (Qwen baseline loss 2.25, GPT-2 2.74) — on such narrow text many heads are unused, which is itself a plausible cause of the 93-99.9% base rate, and 'safe' labels may not transfer to real text.
- Attention-sink / position-0 handling: the scanner averages cosine over ALL positions including the BOS/sink token whose residual norm is orders of magnitude larger; the reference is the pre-LN residual, so late-layer cosines are dominated by the shared massive-activation direction. Any residual-stream order parameter R_l (seeds 3 and 8) will be near-saturated by that common direction (residual anisotropy) regardless of layer, which makes seed 8's prediction ill-posed as stated.
- The paper's base_rate_analysis.json reports cohens_d ~1.9-2.1 between dead and alive z — tautological (z is the classifier) but presented alongside precision; worth naming as a hype pattern.
- GQA structure of Qwen2.5-0.5B (14 q / 2 kv heads, g=7) means the town's real per-token KV lever there is not head death but per-KV-head quantization/eviction; the reader's open question on per-group bit-width is the right direction but should be reframed around the well-known KV quantization results (KIVI/KVQuant-style 2-4 bit) rather than this paper's death fraction.
- Licensing nuance: PolyForm Noncommercial covers the code and text; the provisional patent (64/031,983) covers the METHOD, so even a clean-room reimplementation for research is fine but nothing product-shaped can use the threshold rule without a license.
strongest seed: Seed 5 (random-157 vs tau-dead-157 removal on Qwen2.5-0.5B) — ~10 min CPU, one forward per mask, and it directly falsifies the paper's only load-bearing claim on the town's target model with a pre-registered effect size (3-seed std 0.48). Its value roughly doubles if the town adds the mean-ablation control I list above to the same run (same masks, replace zeros by calibration means): together they answer 'is the dead set special?' and 'is the catastrophe an artifact?' for ~15 min of CPU and no new labels. Seed 1 (write-norm ratio vs alignment) is the runner-up because it yields a reusable observable and reuses the bundled 336 labels — but it inherits those labels' noise (13 positives), so its AUC verdict will be wide.
weakest seed: Seed 3 (depth-recurrent looping of untrained Qwen2.5-0.5B layers 8-15 with coherence trajectories and an R(t) halting criterion). Untrained layer re-application is known to blow up loss, so the falsifier is near-certain to fire and teaches nothing the town does not already know; R(t) on the raw residual stream is saturated by the shared massive-activation direction so it cannot serve as a synchronization order parameter without mean-removal; and the paper's map puts oscillators at tokens, not iterations, so 'per-head coherence converging across loops' is not the paper's theory. Seed 7 (byte-neuron rule with the underived 0.96 constant, no task) is nearly as weak: cheap, but its outcome has no interpretation as knowledge about usefulness.

### modularNN
SOURCE: /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/scratchpad/machinelearning/modularNN (git d87863c, auto-commit 2026-08-15; read 2026-09-13): README.md, examples/README.md, ingestion/pipeline.py, ingestion/encoding/*, ingestion/framing/*, ingestion/inputs/plugins/{synthetic,mnist}.py, ingestion/training/*, ingestion/cli/{demo,demo_runner,stage_viz_dispatch}.py, context/impl/{loop-log,handoff-tier3,impl-demo-mvp}.md, experiments/snr_classifier/results.json, outputs/*/…json, autoresearch.jsonl; plus ../CLAUDE.md, ../AGENTS.md, ../log.md. Full pytest run executed once on this box.

modularNN is a CPU-only, numpy/torch DATA-INGESTION pipeline for feeding ANN or SNN models — not a model. Four stages (ingestion/pipeline.py:run_pipeline): input plugin -> RawDataset (pandas frame) -> featurizer chain -> FeatureDataset -> framer (single_sample | sliding_window) -> FramedDataset(inputs, targets, sample_index) -> encoder -> EncodedDataset(tensors, tensor_spec, metadata). Every stage writes artifacts, JSONL events, an HTML report and a terminal summary into runs/<run_id>/. Root-level dirs (encoding/, framing/, ...) are symlinks into ingestion/.

Two encoder tiers exist and they are NOT the same thing. (a) The REGISTRY encoders (ingestion/encoding/__init__.py registers spiking.population/latency/delta/current + standard.identity/sequence) are 25-line "scaffolds": population = x*scale, latency = 1/|x|, delta = x[1:]-x[:-1], current = x+bias. They emit float tensors, not spikes; auto-selection (auto_selection.py) picks among these. (b) The REAL spike-train encoders live in ingestion/encoding/spiking/image_*.py and are NOT registered — imported directly: spiking.rate (Bernoulli/Poisson per timestep), spiking.latency (TTFS, one spike, t=floor((1-I)(T-1))), spiking.burst (K spikes evenly in a window, count ∝ intensity), spiking.phase (cos carrier vs threshold 1-2I + jitter), spiking.population (M Gaussian fields, fan-out F*M, periodic firing), spiking.delta = degenerate stub for static images (status "stub"). All output uint8 (N, F, T) after per-row min-max normalization (_image_common.to_normalized_intensities), seeded via sha256(run_seed, name). Nothing consumes them as spikes: no LIF/snntorch anywhere in modularNN; ingestion/training has SpikeMLP (2-layer torch MLP on flattened tensors) + sklearn wrappers + k-fold/confusion utilities.

Also present: synthetic spike-dataset generator (inputs/plugins/synthetic.py: poisson/burst/adaptive/oscillatory/refractory, linearly-separable channel groups, vectorized) — but its output is flattened to a per-(sample,channel) spike_counts table before encoding, so downstream "spike" tensors are counts. Image featurizers (normalize, grayscale, resize, flatten toggle, on/off split, DoG edge, patch extract) and splitters (fixed/kfold/LOOCV) are real. MNIST IDX loader real; examples synthesize MNIST-like fixtures (no MNIST on disk). `mNN demo` harness is mostly scaffolding: run_demo writes only a log; stage PNGs come from 4x8x8 synthetic fixtures, not from running encoders.

Tests: 491 collected, 489 pass, 2 fail on this box (one import-hygiene test, one LOOCV timing guard 61s>30s). Deps (numpy 2.4.3, torch 2.10 cpu, snntorch 0.9.4, sklearn, scipy) all present. The owner's own SNR experiment (experiments/snr_classifier) DISPROVED "encoder SNR correlates with classifier accuracy" (r=-0.003, p=0.99, n=30) — but it used the scaffold encoders, and the classifier was near chance (0.15-0.46 for 5 classes), so it is weak evidence about real spike codes.

KEY FACTS:
- MEASURED: 491 tests collected, 489 passed, 2 failed in 258.96s on this 4-core ARM box (python3 -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider). Failures: tests/test_inputs/test_registry_entry_point.py::TestRegistryOnlyEntryPointR6::test_no_direct_concrete_imports_outside_plugins ('Found 3 direct import(s) of concrete *Input classes') and tests/test_stage3_visualizations.py::test_loocv_block_diagram_png_large_n_is_truncated (LOOCV render 61.0s > 30s guard).
- MEASURED: project's own last recorded status 'Full demo suite: 142 pass, 3 skip' and '~310 new tests' (context/impl/impl-demo-mvp.md); loop-log says image-pipeline cavekit 'COMPLETE, 163/163 P' (context/impl/loop-log.md).
- MEASURED: registered encoder names = spiking.population, spiking.latency, spiking.delta, spiking.current, standard.identity, standard.sequence (ingestion/encoding/__init__.py L9-14); the image spike encoders (ImageRateEncoder etc.) are never passed to register_encoder — grep over ingestion/ shows only the 6 registrations.
- MEASURED: scaffold 'spiking.latency' = 1/|x| clamp 1e-9 (ingestion/encoding/spiking/latency.py L18); 'spiking.delta' = main[...,1:]-main[...,:-1] (delta.py L17); 'spiking.population' = x*scale (population.py); 'spiking.current' = x+bias (current.py). None produce binary spikes.
- MEASURED: real spike encoders emit uint8 (N,F,T): rate = Bernoulli(rand < intensity*max_rate) (image_rate.py L30-33); latency TTFS spike_t=floor((1-I)*(T-1)) (image_latency.py L30); burst K=max_spikes (default 5) placed evenly in burst_window (image_burst.py); phase carrier cos(2πt/period), threshold 1-2I, N(0,0.02) jitter (image_phase.py); population M=num_fields (8) Gaussian centers, fires every T//10 steps (image_population.py).
- MEASURED CPU timing (this box, 1000x784 float32 input, T=100): spiking.rate 2085 ms (mean rate 0.50), spiking.latency 197 ms (0.01), spiking.burst 190 ms (0.025), spiking.phase 325 ms (0.50), spiking.population 1336 ms, output (1000,6272,100) (0.025). Rate tensor = 78.4 MB uint8.
- MEASURED: no LIF / snntorch / membrane code inside modularNN (grep -rln 'snntorch|LIF|leaky|membrane' hits only context/proposal4/_make_artifacts.py). Sibling dirs ../spikyNN (2-neuron snntorch LIF chain: num_steps=200, beta=0.8, threshold=1.0 per ../log.md) and ../snn_standard_model exist but were not read in depth.
- MEASURED: experiments/snr_classifier/results.json — verdict 'disproved', pearson_r=-0.0027, p=0.9886, n=30; accuracies 0.155-0.455 across 6 scaffold encoders x 5 SNR levels, 5 classes, MLP 30 epochs, 1000 samples.
- MEASURED: outputs/training_example/training_results.json — encode 0.87 ms, MLP val acc 0.23, LogReg 0.21, RF 0.215, SVC 0.21 (near chance for 5 classes); outputs/validation_example: 5-fold CV means 0.19-0.20.
- MEASURED: autoresearch.jsonl (36 lines) tracked metric encode_time_ms only; best 0.0104 ms for standard.sequence; 'Speedup vs slowest 27-59x'. This optimisation loop optimised scaffold encoders, not spike codes.
- MEASURED: `mNN demo` run_demo() writes only 04_encoding_<target>/_run_log.txt (ingestion/cli/demo_runner.py L115-200); stage PNGs come from _FixtureDataset 4x8x8 checkerboards (stage_viz_dispatch.py L44-60); _emit_stage4 draws a block diagram with hard-coded shapes, no encoder is executed.
- MEASURED: synthetic spike plugin flattens (samples, channels, bins) to rows of sample_id/channel/spike_counts/class_label (inputs/plugins/synthetic.py _spikes_to_dataframe), so run_pipeline's 'spike' encodings operate on counts; framed shape in outputs/visualization_demo/run_report.md is [3200, 4] for 800 samples x 4 channels.
- MEASURED: deps declared numpy>=1.26 pandas>=2.2 pyarrow>=16 torch>=2.2 Pillow>=10 matplotlib>=3.8, python>=3.11 (pyproject.toml); on this box numpy 2.4.3, pandas 2.3.3, torch 2.10.0+cpu, sklearn 1.8.0, scipy 1.17.1, snntorch 0.9.4, pytest 9.0.2 all importable.
- MEASURED: ../CLAUDE.md and ../AGENTS.md describe the Colab-notebook conventions for knnVSregression/spikyNN and a GitNexus index (34995 symbols); ../log.md last entry 2026-04-05 (meme-sniper-bot), no modularNN entries — modularNN history lives in modularNN/context/impl/*.md and autoresearch.jsonl instead.

REUSABLE:
- Real spike encoders (CPU numpy, seeded, tested): /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/scratchpad/machinelearning/modularNN/ingestion/encoding/spiking/{image_rate,image_latency,image_burst,image_phase,image_population}.py — call E().encode(FramedDataset(dataset_id, inputs=(N,F) array, targets, sample_index, frame_metadata={}), {'timesteps':T,'seed':s}) -> uint8 (N,F,T). Usable directly as input generators for any SNN / byte-neuron simulator.
- Normalisation + seed derivation helpers: modularNN/ingestion/encoding/_image_common.py (to_normalized_intensities, derive_seed = sha256(run_seed::name)).
- Synthetic spike-train generator with 5 temporal patterns and linearly-separable classes: modularNN/ingestion/inputs/plugins/synthetic.py (generate_class_labeled_spikes -> (samples, channels, bins) counts; use the array before _spikes_to_dataframe flattens it).
- Preview/visualisation hooks: modularNN/ingestion/encoding/image_base.py (_render_raster, _render_rate_summary, _render_heatmap) and modularNN/ingestion/encoding/comparison.py (compare_encoders side-by-side PNG); modularNN/ingestion/reporting/spike_viz.py.
- Image featurizers (normalize, grayscale, resize, on/off split, DoG edge, patch extract): modularNN/ingestion/featurizers/plugins/image/*.py; MNIST IDX loader: modularNN/ingestion/inputs/plugins/mnist.py.
- Deterministic splitters (fixed / k-fold / LOOCV, per-fold JSON artifacts): modularNN/ingestion/framing/splits.py, split_artifacts.py.
- Tiny CPU classifier baselines + metrics (SpikeMLP, sklearn wrappers, k-fold CV, confusion/F1 report, EarlyStopping, CheckpointManager): modularNN/ingestion/training/{mlp,sklearn_models,validation,metrics}.py.
- End-to-end runnable examples (in-memory fixtures, no downloads): modularNN/examples/e2e_image_pipeline_mnist.py, e2e_synthetic_spikes.py, e2e_training.py, e2e_encoding_benchmark.py; experiment template with numpy-only Kruskal-Wallis: modularNN/experiments/synthetic_spike_ml_readiness.py.
- Prior negative result to cite: modularNN/experiments/snr_classifier/results.json (SNR vs accuracy, r=-0.003).

FAILURES/LESSONS:
- Name collision hazard: the registry's 'spiking.latency/population/delta' are float scaffolds, while image_*.py classes with the SAME .name strings are the real spike encoders. Any experiment that goes through get_encoder('spiking.*') or encoder={'name':'auto'} silently uses the non-spiking scaffold. Always import the image_* classes explicitly.
- The project's autoresearch loop optimised encode_time_ms of the scaffolds (0.01-0.7 ms) — a metric that says nothing about coding quality; the resulting 'speedup' claims are about tensor copies, not spikes.
- The SNR-correlation 'disproved' verdict was run with scaffold encoders and a classifier at ~chance (0.2-0.4 on 5 classes); it does not falsify anything about rate/latency/burst codes and should not be cited as such.
- The demo harness (mNN demo, ~210 tests) is test-heavy scaffolding: it produces logs, diagrams and manifests from tiny fixtures and never runs an encoder; do not mistake its green tests for pipeline validation.
- Two tests fail on this ARM box (import hygiene; LOOCV diagram 61 s vs 30 s guard) — the LOOCV one is a real CPU-speed sensitivity on the 4-core N1, worth remembering when time guards are written for this hardware.
- Synthetic spike trains are flattened to spike counts before encoding, so the 'spike' pipeline path through run_pipeline never carries temporal spike tensors; the (samples, channels, bins) array must be intercepted before _spikes_to_dataframe.
- No SNN consumer exists in modularNN; every 'spike' tensor ends in a flattened MLP or sklearn model. The SNN side stops at encoding.

SEEDS:
- CLAIM The five real spike codes (rate, TTFS latency, burst, phase, population) differ in bits-per-spike information delivered to a downstream tiny model by more than 2x, with TTFS latency and burst (mean rate 0.01-0.025) dominating rate/phase (0.50) on MNIST-like data at equal classifier size. // FALSIFIER A fixed 2-layer MLP or LIF readout trained on each code's (N,F,T) tensor reaches within 2 pp of the same accuracy per spike emitted, or rate coding wins per spike. // EXP Use ImageRateEncoder/ImageLatencyEncoder/ImageBurstEncoder/ImagePhaseEncoder/ImagePopulationEncoder on a real MNIST subset (load IDX via inputs/plugins/mnist.py after a one-time download), T in {10,25,50,100}, train ingestion/training/mlp.py SpikeMLP and a small snntorch LIF readout; report accuracy / (mean spikes per sample). ~2 s encode per 1000 samples measured. // COST CPU only, <30 min per code x T grid on this box; 0 GPU-hours
- CLAIM A TTFS-latency encoding maps directly onto the owner's neuron-as-byte model: with T<=8 timesteps the (N,F,T) uint8 tensor from image_latency.py is losslessly a one-byte-per-neuron bit pattern (bit k set iff spike at tick k), so the encoder can serve as the front-end of a byte-neuron simulator with zero conversion. // FALSIFIER Packing spikes[...,0:8] into bytes (np.packbits along T) and unpacking does not round-trip, or a T=8 latency code loses more than 5 pp of linear-probe accuracy versus T=100. // EXP Encode with ImageLatencyEncoder({'timesteps':8}), np.packbits(axis=-1) to (N,F) bytes, unpack, assert equality; then train LogisticRegressionWrapper on packed bytes vs T=100 flattened tensor. // COST minutes, CPU
- CLAIM The registry 'scaffold' spiking encoders (1/|x|, diff, scale) carry no more class information than identity, which is why the SNR experiment found r≈0; replacing them with the real image_* codes will yield a non-zero SNR-accuracy correlation. // FALSIFIER Re-running experiments/snr_classifier_correlation.py with the image_* encoders substituted still gives |r|<0.2 across the 5 SNR levels. // EXP Copy the script into scratch, swap ENCODERS for the five image_* classes applied to the (samples, channels, bins) array from generate_class_labeled_spikes, keep MLP config, compute Pearson r. // COST ~10-20 min CPU
- CLAIM Population (Gaussian receptive field) coding with M fields gives a tiny model the same accuracy as an M-times-wider raw-pixel MLP, i.e. it trades neurons for weights — useful for the 'cooperating tiny models' vision because each field can live in a different tiny model. // FALSIFIER SpikeMLP on population-coded (N, F*M) counts is worse than SpikeMLP with hidden size scaled by M on raw normalised pixels at equal parameter count. // EXP ImagePopulationEncoder with M in {2,4,8}, sum over T to counts, train SpikeMLP; compare to raw-pixel SpikeMLP at matched parameter counts using ingestion/training/validation.py kfold_cv. // COST <1 h CPU
- CLAIM The synthetic 'oscillatory' temporal pattern generator (inputs/plugins/synthetic.py) can stand in as a coupled-oscillator test bed: two channels with the same modulation frequency and a phase offset are separable by a phase-code readout but not by a rate-code readout. // FALSIFIER Rate-count readout (sum over bins) separates the phase-offset classes as well as the phase encoder does. // EXP generate_class_labeled_spikes with temporal_pattern='oscillatory', two classes differing only in phase offset (needs a small config patch in scratch, not the repo); readouts: counts vs ImagePhaseEncoder-style carrier correlation; LogisticRegressionWrapper. // COST minutes, CPU

OPEN:
- Is the registry/real-encoder name collision intentional (kept for the 'stub disclosure' tests) or an unfinished migration? index_manifest.py lists only spiking.delta as a known stub, implying the others are believed real.
- No real MNIST is on disk (datasets/raw holds market data only, 27 MB); the e2e image example synthesises digits. Does the owner have a cached IDX pair anywhere on the box, or must one be downloaded (network, ~11 MB)?
- Which downstream SNN was modularNN meant to feed — ../spikyNN (snntorch 2-neuron LIF) or ../snn_standard_model? Neither is imported by modularNN; the interface contract (N,F,T uint8) was never exercised by an SNN.
- The LOOCV timing-guard failure (61 s vs 30 s) — is render_partition_block_diagram O(N^2) for N=2000, or is this box simply slower than the author's? Matters for any time-guarded CPU benchmarks the town writes.
- How does the owner's 'dead-head coherence' pruning equation relate to anything here? Nothing in modularNN references attention heads, coherence, or pruning; the closest hook is the phase encoder's carrier/threshold structure.

CRITIC (confidence 0.82):
unsupported:
- 'The SNR experiment used the scaffold encoders' — NOT SUPPORTED. experiments/snr_classifier_correlation.py L38 says 'Encoders to test (manual implementations)' and L122-155 hand-implements identity/sequence/current/delta/population/latency inside the script; it never calls get_encoder or the registry scaffolds. The reader's diagnosis of WHY r≈0 is therefore wrong (see errors_found for the real cause).
- 'population = M Gaussian fields' — NOT SUPPORTED. image_population.py L37-39: active = |intensity - center| <= sigma, a hard box window, binary; the module then fires the same binary mask every firing_period steps. There is no Gaussian anywhere; sum-over-T is just (T/period) x a binary bin indicator. Seed 4's framing ('Gaussian receptive field ... trades neurons for weights') rests on this misreading.
- 'No real MNIST is on disk ... must one be downloaded (network, ~11 MB)' — NOT SUPPORTED; the reader only searched inside the repo. MEASURED: /home/ubuntu/.cache/torchvision/MNIST/raw/ and /home/ubuntu/.cache/torchvision/FashionMNIST/raw/ hold uncompressed IDX pairs (train-images-idx3-ubyte 47,040,016 B, t10k-images 7,840,016 B, labels present), readable by inputs/plugins/mnist.py with zero network.
- 'index_manifest.py lists only spiking.delta as a known stub' — path cited as ingestion/encoding/index_manifest.py does not exist; the file is ingestion/cli/index_manifest.py (L60-65). Minor, but the citation is wrong.
- 'seeded via sha256(run_seed, name)' stated for all image encoders — image_latency.py imports only to_normalized_intensities and uses no RNG at all (deterministic); only rate/phase/population(?) call resolve_seed. Minor over-generalisation.
- Seed 2 falsifier 'packbits ... does not round-trip' is a tautology: any {0,1} uint8 tensor round-trips through np.packbits/unpackbits (MEASURED True on T=8 latency output). It cannot fail, so it is not a falsifier.
- CPU timings are presented as if stable; my re-run on the same box gave burst 496 ms vs the reader's 190 ms and rate 1583 vs 2085 ms — 2-3x jitter between runs on this 4-core N1. Treat the reader's ms figures as order-of-magnitude, not benchmarks.
errors:
- ROOT CAUSE of the SNR r≈0 result is misdiagnosed. snr_classifier_correlation.py L74-90 pivots the synthetic plugin's flattened spike_counts table to (samples, channels) TOTAL counts and then `.unsqueeze(-1).expand(-1,-1,50)` — every one of the 50 'timesteps' is the identical total count. There is zero temporal information in the tensor, so 'delta' is all-zero except the prepend, 'latency' (first index where count>0) is a constant 1 for every channel with any spike, and 'sequence'/'identity' are 50 copies of 4 numbers. Noise is then added to that broadcast tensor. The experiment could not have found an SNR-accuracy relation for temporal codes because the codes were fed a constant; with 4 channels x 5 classes and linearly_separable=True, the 0.155-0.455 accuracies (MEASURED) are barely above the 0.2 chance line. The verdict 'disproved' is a measurement of a degenerate pipeline, not of SNR. Seed 3's premise ('scaffolds carry no more info than identity, swap in image_* encoders') attacks the wrong thing — the fix is to intercept the (samples, channels, bins) array before _spikes_to_dataframe, exactly as the reader's own failures_and_lessons #6 says, and the reader did not connect the two.
- Seed 3's cpu_experiment is also shape-incompatible as written: image_* encoders take FramedDataset.inputs as (N, F) intensities and per-row min-max normalise them (_image_common.to_normalized_intensities); handing them a (samples, channels, bins) count array requires flattening bins into 'pixels', at which point the TTFS/rate encoders re-encode already-temporal data as intensities — a category error the seed does not address.
- Seed 1's falsifier ('rate coding wins per spike') is unfalsifiable in practice: the mean rates are already MEASURED at 0.50 (rate/phase) vs 0.010 (TTFS) vs 0.025 (burst) at T=100 — a 20-50x spike-count gap baked into the encoder definitions, so 'accuracy per spike' is decided before any classifier runs. And with a SpikeMLP on the flattened (N, F, T) tensor (78,400 inputs at T=100, ~313 MB float32 per 1000 samples — 10k samples ≈ 3 GB against ~7 GB free) the readout ignores time entirely, so all five codes collapse to 'an MLP recovering per-pixel intensity from a different deterministic transform of the same intensity'; accuracies will converge and the experiment learns nothing about codes. Only a leaky readout (e.g. ../spikyNN's snntorch Leaky chain) makes the comparison meaningful.
- TTFS at T=8 (seed 2) is 3-bit uniform quantisation of per-image-normalised intensity — 'T=8 loses <5 pp vs T=100' is a quantisation-robustness question about MNIST, not a byte-neuron question. The owner's byte-neuron model maps bits to UPSTREAM NEURONS' output bits (fan-in identity), not to timesteps; the reader silently substituted 'bit k = tick k', which is a different architecture. Also per-row min-max guarantees the dimmest pixel of every image never spikes (MEASURED: 1000/1000 rows have a zero-spike pixel) — a quirk any byte-packing must handle.
missing:
- Prior art the town should read before spending tokens on seed 1: Guo, Fouda, Salama, Eltawil 2021, 'Neural Coding in Spiking Neural Networks: A Comparative Study for Robust Neuromorphic Systems', Frontiers in Neuroscience 10.3389/fnins.2021.638474 (https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/neuroscience/articles/10.3389/fnins.2021.638474/full) — compares rate/TTFS/phase/burst on MNIST and Fashion-MNIST with a 2-layer STDP SNN; reports TTFS at 4x/7.5x lower latency and 3.5x/6.5x fewer synaptic ops than rate, phase most noise-resilient. modularNN's five encoders are essentially this paper's four codes plus population; seed 1 should be framed as a CPU replication/extension against those numbers, not as a novel claim.
- The existing SNN consumer is one directory up and was not opened: ../spikyNN/snn_neuron.py (100 lines, MEASURED) is an snntorch Leaky 2-neuron chain with beta/threshold/reset_mechanism parameters. Wiring image_* (N,F,T) uint8 into snn.Leaky is the missing ~20-line bridge that would give every seed a temporal readout instead of an MLP; the reader flagged 'no SNN consumer' as a lesson but not as the obvious first experiment.
- Fashion-MNIST is also on disk (same IDX format) — a harder second dataset for free, and the one Guo et al. used, so replication can be checked on both.
- Information-theoretic framing the owner's 'knowledge per token' moral asks for: mutual information I(label; spikes) per spike per code can be estimated on CPU with a linear probe (sklearn LogisticRegressionWrapper already present) at T in {2,4,8,16} — a curve, not a winner; nobody proposed measuring the curve.
- Coupled-oscillator / phase thread: image_phase.py is a global cosine carrier (period 20) with per-pixel threshold 1-2I — every pixel shares one oscillator, so there is no coupling and no phase diversity to prune or map. The synthetic 'oscillatory' pattern hard-codes 5 Hz / 10 Hz sinusoids with no phase parameter (synthetic.py L235, L374), so seed 5 is not a config patch but a new generator. The honest statement is: modularNN has no oscillator-network primitive at all.
- Dead-head coherence pruning has no hook here; the reader said so in open_questions but did not say the actionable thing: the nearest analogue in this codebase is the population encoder's M-field fan-out (F*M neurons, mostly silent) — a 'silent field' pruning statistic (fraction of T where a neuron ever fires) is computable from the (N,F*M,T) tensor in one numpy line and would be a cheap first test of a coherence-style prune on spike data.
- Memory budget: at T=100 the uint8 tensors are 78.4 MB per 1000 samples (MEASURED); any float32 conversion for torch is 4x that. On this 7-GB-free box, full-MNIST experiments must keep T<=16 or stream in batches; none of the est_cost lines account for this.
- Reproducibility of timing: the LOOCV test failure (61 s vs 30 s guard) and my 2-3x run-to-run jitter on encoders both say wall-clock guards on this ARM box need >=3x headroom; worth writing as a town-wide rule rather than a modularNN lesson.
strongest seed: Seed 1 (five real spike codes compared on real MNIST) — but only after rewriting: MNIST/Fashion-MNIST IDX are already on disk, the encoders are real and CPU-cheap (0.1-1.6 s per 1000 images MEASURED), and Guo et al. 2021 provides published numbers to calibrate against. Replace the pre-decided 'accuracy per spike' with an accuracy-vs-mean-spikes curve at T in {2,4,8,16}, and use the ../spikyNN snntorch Leaky readout (or a leaky linear readout) rather than a flattened SpikeMLP so time is not discarded. Done that way it is the highest knowledge-per-token: real data, zero downloads, minutes of CPU, and a direct check of a published claim on this hardware.
weakest seed: Seed 3 (re-run the SNR experiment with image_* encoders) — its premise is wrong on two counts: the original experiment did not use the registry scaffolds (it hand-implements encoders and feeds them a count tensor broadcast over 50 identical timesteps, so no temporal code could have shown anything), and the image_* encoders take (N,F) intensities, not (samples,channels,bins) spikes, so the proposed substitution is a shape/category error. Tokens spent here would confirm a misdiagnosis. Seed 2 is a close second: its falsifier (packbits round-trip) is tautological, and it swaps the owner's bit-per-upstream-neuron model for bit-per-timestep without saying so.

### snn-experiments
SOURCE: Owner SNN corpus, read 2026-09-13: scratchpad/machinelearning/spikyNN (3 files), scratchpad/machinelearning/snn_standard_model (TODO.md, reports/SNN_Progress_Report.md, experiment_plan.py, experiment_infrastructure.py, colab/README.md, colab/phase1_results.csv, 92 experiments/*.json, research/deep_analysis/synaptic_alpha/REPORT.md), scratchpad/machinelearning/snn_applied_finance (snn_energy_topology/{README,neurons,surrogate,energy}.py + tests, run_p2_experiments.py, p2_experiment_results.json, spiking_transformer_phase1_results.json, run_ann_topology_comparison.py, research/deep_analysis/*.md + 2 REPORT.md, pipeline_builds/*.md, notebooks/*.ipynb, conversations/ — last 3 critic files read fully, rest grepped), scratchpad/Simple-Spiking-Net-Demo (App.tsx, services/snnEngine.ts, types.ts). All paths under /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/scratchpad/.

Four artefacts, one lineage. (1) spikyNN: a 200-step two-LIF chain (snnTorch Leaky, beta=0.8, thr=1.0, subtract reset, w_syn=0.8) with a cumsum sliding-window "activation" readout - a toy, no learning. (2) snn_standard_model: the real asset - 92 logged runs (5.0 CPU-hours measured) on FC 784->1000->10 with snnTorch Leaky/Synaptic/Alpha, rate or latency coding, loss = CE on SUMMED output membrane, default snnTorch surrogate (ATan, not fast-sigmoid as the finance code uses), single seed 42, mostly 1 epoch. Findings: accuracy landscape on MNIST is flat (94.5-96.5% fixed params); 25 steps beats 50/100 everywhere; hidden membrane variance explodes 0.39->86.8 at beta=0.99/100 steps; subtract reset > none > zero (96.10/95.24/93.26); learnable timescales are the only thing that moves the needle (Leaky 97.36% in 3 epochs, Synaptic 97.69% in 5 epochs at LOWER spike density 2.2%/1.6%); rankings invert on Fashion-MNIST (Leaky 84.73 > Synaptic 83.69 > Alpha 81.69) and lower beta wins there. Two report claims are artefacts: the "inhibition has no effect" result is identical to 4 decimals because `inhibition` is never passed to snn.Leaky in build_snn_model; the learnable-Leaky run was 3 epochs, not 5. N-MNIST (Phase 4) and the promised "Rhythm Neuron" Part II never happened. (3) snn_applied_finance: five notebook generations (V1-V4, equilibrium, limbic, spiking transformer) documented mostly as prose/diagrams; the V1-V4 notebooks and their accuracy numbers are NOT in this checkout. The only measured finance result is spiking_transformer_phase1_results.json: on SOL 1h 3-class triple-barrier, 3-fold walk-forward, LGBM acc 0.643 / lift 1.76x vs Spiking Transformer acc 0.361, bull recall 0.003, lift 0.32x - the LIF-gated Q/K (T=8 repeats, beta=0.85, thr=1.0, BN-before-LIF fix) gated the minority class out entirely; Dense Transformer and Temporal CNN also sat at 0.36-0.37 (16-candle windows). Documented structural reasons for the finance failures: population coding is a lossy ~21-35-bit-per-step bottleneck on already-noisy z-scored features; dead/saturated networks (rate <0.02 or >0.98) are only detected, never corrected; the limbic reward_sensitivity params get zero gradient (energy detached); exponential decoding caps confidence at |p-0.5|<=0.348. The energy-topology module (EnergyAwareLIF, pure PyTorch, 49 tests) is clean, but run_p2_experiments.py never calls compute_energy_earned, so "all neurons energy-negative" is by construction, and with thr=1.0 and unit input spikes the neuron is a pass-through (output rate ~= f_in: 0.039@0.05 ... 0.490@0.50), so Exp B's beta drift 0.51->1.016 (past its own clamp) changed spike rate only 0.168->0.188. (4) Simple-Spiking-Net-Demo: a 4-neuron React/TS LIF sandbox (stepSNN in services/snnEngine.ts, ~40 lines, clamps membrane at 0) with a Gemini "analyze" button - the ideal place to prototype a byte-neuron visually. Byte-neuron mapping: EnergyAwareLIF.forward(x, mem, energy) is the natural signature to fork (mem:float -> byte:uint8; integrate -> shift/set bits from 8 subscribed upstream outputs; threshold -> popcount/pattern LUT; reset -> clear); the surrogate is the open problem.
