FAILURES/LESSONS:
- Pricing prediction never beat a tree: the only measured finance result has Spiking Transformer at 0.361 acc / 0.003 bull recall vs LGBM 0.643 / 1.76x lift (spiking_transformer_phase1_results.json). Cause per notebook sec 12: LIF gating on Q/K at small scale (dm=32, T=3 in the run) sparsified the minority class away; 16-candle windows carry too little signal for any sequence model (TCN, dense transformer also ~0.36).
- Population coding is a self-inflicted bottleneck: 7 z-scored features -> 21/35 Bernoulli bits per candle, round-trip explicitly documented as lossy (foundational REPORT sec 1b) on data that is already near-noise; the LSTM sees the same 7 floats losslessly. Lesson: encode fewer, more informative bits (delta/phasic won the ablation).
- Dead/saturated networks were the dominant training failure mode (V4 epoch-5 guard, threshold annealing, 50% mask + weight floor 0.1 all exist to fight it); fast-sigmoid k=25 gives ~zero gradient beyond ~0.1 of threshold so dead stays dead. Every fix was a hyperparameter patch, none was a mechanism.
- Reported 'inhibition has no effect' (L6) is a wiring bug, not a finding: config.inhibition is never passed to snn.Leaky in build_snn_model, and the run reproduces baseline to 4 decimals. Lesson: identical-to-the-decimal results mean a flag did nothing.
- Energy-aware neuron Phase 2 'validated' a tautology: the runner never applies compute_energy_earned, so every neuron is energy-negative by construction, and the architect analysis rationalised it (D10 'universal deficit is expected'). Also thr=1.0 with unit spikes makes the neuron a pass-through (out rate ~= f_in), so beta learning changed nothing measurable while raw beta blew past its own clamp (1.016). Lesson: before claiming a neuron 'learns', show the neuron can do anything but relay.
- Limbic reward module: reward_sensitivity/cost_sensitivity are nn.Parameters with zero gradient because energy is detached - a 'learnable' knob that was a constant. Lesson: assert .grad is not None in tests for every parameter you claim is learned.
- Single seed, 1 epoch, flat landscape: MNIST differences of <0.5pp (most of the fixed-parameter table) are inside noise; the report admits it. Fashion-MNIST spread (74.8-84.7) is the only discriminating signal in the corpus. Lesson: pick a task hard enough to separate hypotheses before sweeping.
- Documentation drift: report says learnable Leaky = 5 epochs (JSON: 3), total compute ~26 h (JSON sum: 5.0 h), 17 PNGs (16), 57 Leaky files (18). Multi-agent pipeline (architect/builder/critic) produced polished prose over unverified numbers; critics caught counts but not the wiring bugs. Lesson: verify claims against the JSON, not the markdown.
- Phase 4 (N-MNIST, native spikes) and 'Part II Rhythm Neuron' were promised and never built; the only spike-native dataset test never ran, so the central SNN claim (advantage on event data) is untested.
- Simple-Spiking-Net-Demo clamps membrane at 0 while the Python neurons allow negative membrane; the visual intuition and the trained models disagree on hyperpolarisation.

SEEDS:
- CLAIM A byte-neuron (state = uint8 of the last outputs of 8 subscribed upstream neurons; fires when popcount(byte & mask) >= k or when byte matches a learned 256-entry LUT; emits 1 bit/tick) can reach >= 95% on rate-coded MNIST with a 784->1000->10 topology, i.e. within 1pp of the Leaky baseline 96.10%, at strictly integer CPU cost. // FALSIFIER Best byte-neuron config stays below 90% after the same 1-epoch/25-step budget, or needs float surrogates that cost more than the LIF forward pass. // EXP Fork EnergyAwareLIF.forward into ByteNeuron(x_bits, byte)->(bit, byte); add neuron_model='Byte' to build_snn_model; train with a soft-popcount surrogate (sum of sigmoid bit-probs) via the existing train_and_evaluate; compare to experiments/beta0.99_steps25_Leaky_MNIST_*.json. Fan-in 8 forces a sparse random or learned subscription map (784 inputs -> each hidden byte subscribes to 8). // COST ~3-6 CPU-hours on the 4-core N1 for 5-10 configs at 142 s/epoch-equivalent
- CLAIM Ordered-firing (which bits set in what order) is representable as a shift register of one upstream's last 8 outputs, and a network of such neurons is a strictly cheaper LIF: an 8-tap binary FIR with a LUT threshold equals LIF with beta in {0.5..0.99} on rate-coded inputs up to quantisation. // FALSIFIER For fixed input spike trains, the byte shift-register neuron cannot reproduce the LIF spike train of the spikyNN two-neuron sim (beta 0.8, thr 1.0, w 0.8) with any 256-entry LUT; spike-train edit distance stays > 20% of spikes. // EXP Rewrite spikyNN/snn_neuron.py with the byte neuron; sweep all 256 LUT masks for N1 and N2 against the recorded LIF spike trains (200 steps); report best-match distance. Seconds of compute. // COST < 1 CPU-minute
- CLAIM The dead/saturated guard (rate <0.02 or >0.98) reformulated as a per-neuron coherence score (correlation of a neuron's bit stream with its subscribed inputs) prunes byte-neurons with no accuracy loss and yields the coupled-oscillator map the owner wants: neurons whose bit streams phase-lock form the coupling graph. // FALSIFIER Pruning the 30% lowest-coherence neurons after epoch 1 drops MNIST accuracy by > 1pp, or the phase-lock graph is indistinguishable from random subscription. // EXP Log per-neuron spike trains from train_and_evaluate (already stacked as [T,B,hidden]), compute pairwise phase-lock / correlation, prune, re-evaluate on the test set only (no retrain, ~30 s). // COST ~10 CPU-minutes per config
- CLAIM Learnable timescales, not neuron complexity, are what moved accuracy (97.69% learnable Synaptic vs 96.1% any fixed model); for a byte neuron the analogue is a learnable shift length / bit-mask, and it will recover the same +1.5pp over fixed masks. // FALSIFIER Learnable mask/shift byte-neuron gains < 0.5pp over its best fixed mask on MNIST and Fashion-MNIST (baselines 84.73% Leaky b0.8). // EXP Two runs of the byte model (fixed mask vs learnable soft mask), 5 epochs each, on both datasets, using experiment_plan.py's skip-done sweep. // COST ~4 x 12 min = ~1 CPU-hour
- CLAIM Re-running the two artefact experiments with the bugs fixed reverses the recorded findings: wiring inhibition=True into snn.Leaky changes accuracy or spike density measurably (not identical), and applying compute_energy_earned in run_p2_experiments.py makes low-rate neurons energy-positive while a threshold > input amplitude makes beta learning finally affect spike rate. // FALSIFIER Inhibition still reproduces 96.10%/4.34% to 4 decimals; or energy remains negative at f_in=0.1 with earning applied and thr=2.0. // EXP Patch a copy of build_snn_model (pass inhibition=config.inhibition), 1 epoch (~142 s); patch a copy of run_p2_experiments.py to add earned energy and sweep thr in {1.0, 1.5, 2.0} (seconds). // COST < 5 CPU-minutes
- CLAIM On the finance task the SNN failed for lack of signal, not lack of model: a byte-neuron or any SNN fed the same 79 features will not beat LGBM 0.643, but a byte-neuron fed LGBM's own leaf indices (as bits) as a cheap streaming post-filter will match LGBM lift at ~zero incremental compute. // FALSIFIER Byte-neuron post-filter on LGBM leaf bits changes lift by < 0.05 or degrades bear F1. // EXP Requires the SOL 1h labelled parquet (absent here) - first regenerate from ccxt or substitute a public OHLCV; then reuse run_ann_topology_comparison.py folds. Blocked until data exists. // COST ~1 CPU-hour once data is present
- CLAIM The spiking transformer's T-step repetition is a depth-recurrent loop; with a byte-state (1 bit/tick/neuron) instead of float membranes, the KV-cache for the spiking attention path is 8x smaller than fp8 and streaming cost per token is O(popcount). // FALSIFIER Replacing LIFLayer's float mem with a uint8 shift register in SpikingAttentionBlock drops the model below the dense transformer's 0.372 on the same folds, or spike density collapses to < 2% (dead) at every beta. // EXP Swap LIFLayer in notebook cell 26 for a ByteLIF, run the 3-fold harness with the toy-size config (dm=32, 8 epochs) - the original ran on CPU at that size. // COST ~30-60 CPU-minutes per fold set

OPEN:
- Where are the V1-V4, equilibrium and limbic notebooks and their measured directional accuracies? This checkout only carries two notebooks (outputs stripped) and prose descriptions; the 'phasic-only ablation EQ-ABL-02 won' has no number attached.
- Did the finance SNNs ever beat the majority-class / logistic-regression baselines the limbic notebook 'tested alongside'? No number survives in this corpus.
- Is the microcap SOL 1h labelled parquet (79 features, triple-barrier) recoverable? run_ann_topology_comparison.py hard-codes /home/ubuntu/.openclaw/workspace-builder/machinelearning/microcap_swing/data/labels/sol/sol_1h_labeled.parquet.
- What was the 'Rhythm Neuron / Living Frequency Network' Part II design (frequency-resonance neuron) referenced in the progress report? No design file exists here; it may be the ancestor of the coupled-oscillator idea.
- Byte-neuron semantics to pin down before coding: is the byte (a) a snapshot of 8 different upstream neurons' current bits (spatial, fan-in 8), (b) a shift register of one upstream's last 8 bits (temporal, order-sensitive), or (c) both via 2 bytes? 'In what order' suggests (b) or a LUT over (a); the two are different models with different costs.
- Training rule for a non-differentiable bit-threshold: soft-popcount surrogate (keeps the existing BPTT harness), differentiable-LUT relaxation, or local Hebbian/energy rules (would finally give the energy ledger a job)? Decide which one the first hypothesis set commits to.
- Which snnTorch surrogate did the standard-model sweeps actually use? Code passes no spike_grad, so snntorch 0.9.4 default ATan applies; finance code uses fast-sigmoid k=25 - the two halves of the corpus are not directly comparable.
- Should the Simple-Spiking-Net-Demo's Gemini call be stubbed so the sandbox is paid-API-free, given the town's CPU-only, no-paid-API rule?
- Was the report's '~26 hours' total compute counting failed/rerun jobs not saved as JSON (measured sum is 5.0 h)? If so, ~80% of the compute produced no artefact - worth knowing before repeating the pattern.

CRITIC (confidence 0.82):
unsupported:
- 'Learnable timescales are the only thing that moves the needle' (key_facts, summary, seed 4). MEASURED: the 4 learnable runs are the ONLY multi-epoch runs in the corpus (Counter: 88 runs at 1 epoch fixed, 3 at 5 epochs learnable, 1 at 3 epochs learnable; all seed 42). Their accuracy_curve after epoch 1: learnable Leaky 0.9547 (< fixed beta0.99 0.9610), learnable Synaptic a0.5b0.5 0.9599 (< best fixed Synaptic 0.9634, syn_alpha_zero_a0.01_b0.8). The reported +1.26pp/+1.59pp is fully explained by epochs; there is no fixed-parameter 3- or 5-epoch control anywhere. The reader repeated the report's headline finding and built seed 4 on it.
- Seed 1: 'at strictly integer CPU cost'. Training with a soft-popcount surrogate is float BPTT through 25 steps, same as the LIF; only inference is integer. On a CPU-only box training cost dominates the budget the reader quotes (142 s/epoch).
- Seed 7: 'KV-cache 8x smaller than fp8, streaming cost O(popcount)'. Nothing in the corpus has a KV-cache: SpikingAttentionBlock runs over fixed 16-24 candle windows, non-autoregressive. The claim is imported, not sourced. Also the harness in this checkout (run_ann_topology_comparison.py: SEQ=24, T_STEPS=4, DM=32) does NOT match the JSON it supposedly produced (seq_len 16), and the reader itself says T=8 in the summary and T=3 in failures - the run config is unrecoverable, so 'same folds' parity is not available.
- Seed 3: 'yields the coupled-oscillator map the owner wants'. The reader's coherence = correlation of a neuron's bit stream with its inputs. The owner's dead-head equation (scratchpad/dead-head/README.md, MEASURED) is tau_death(d) = 0.96/sqrt(d_model) on mean coupling to the residual stream, derived from BKT criticality. These are different quantities; the dead-head repo sits in the same scratchpad with data files (base_rate_analysis.json, qwen25_05b_*, open_llama_7b_*) and was never opened.
- Seed 6: 'byte-neuron on LGBM leaf bits will match LGBM lift at ~zero incremental compute' - no evidence anywhere in the corpus; and LGBM's 0.643 acc has no base-rate comparator in the JSON (bear_f1 0.745 suggests bear is the majority class; LGBM lift is 1.76 +- 0.38 on 3 folds).
- 'Fashion-MNIST spread is the only discriminating signal' - Leaky 84.73 vs Synaptic 83.69 is 1.0pp on one seed, one epoch; the reader's own noise caveat (<0.5pp within noise, seed 42 only) does not establish that 1pp is outside noise either.
- 'hidden membrane variance explodes 0.39->86.8' framed as instability. MEASURED csv: mem_mean_hidden goes -0.09 -> -7.36 at beta 0.99/100 steps, i.e. the variance is sub-threshold negative drift of silent neurons under subtract reset with negative net current, not oscillation; a hyperpolarisation-clamp (as the TS demo does) would remove most of it.
- ESTIMATE '2-4 min/epoch on this N1' - report says CPU-only but never names the CPU; no basis for 'similar order'.
errors:
- Epoch confound missed (see above) - the single largest error; it invalidates the corpus' central 'finding' and the reader's seed 4 as written.
- Reader is internally inconsistent on the spiking transformer's T (8 in summary, 3 in failures, script says T_STEPS=4) and did not notice the checkout script's SEQ=24 contradicts the JSON's seq_len 16.
- The finance comparison numbers are means over degenerate folds: Spiking Transformer acc 0.361 +- 0.193 and Dense Transformer lift 0.528 +- 0.528 (std == mean => two of three folds at zero) - '0.361 vs 0.372' is not a comparison, it is noise over collapsed folds. Reader presented them as ranked results.
- Verified correct: 92 JSONs with the stated breakdown; wall_clock sum 17,989.97 s = 5.00 h vs report's '~26 hours' (L753); inhibition never passed to snn.Leaky (common_kw lacks it) and inhibition run == baseline (0.96104, hidden 0.04336) to full precision; learn_beta JSON num_epochs=3, wall 421 s vs report '5 epochs, ~710 s' (L432, L497); loss is CE on summed output membrane (L293-294); compute_energy_earned imported L14 and never called; EnergyAwareLIF thr=1.0 fixed, beta clamp [0.5,0.99], raw _beta reaches 1.016; Exp A rates 0.039@0.05..0.490@0.50; TS demo clamps V at 0 (L43); snntorch 0.9.4 default spike_grad=None => ATan.
missing:
- The readout: CE is on SUMMED OUTPUT MEMBRANE, so output-layer spikes never touch the loss or the prediction. Every 'output spike density' number in the corpus is decoration, and - more important for the byte-neuron - no result in the corpus used a spike-count readout. A 1-bit-out byte neuron cannot expose a float membrane sum, so the 96.10% baseline is not the right comparator for seeds 1 and 4; a spike-count-readout LIF baseline has to be run first (1 epoch, ~142 s) or the byte-neuron comparison is confounded from the start.
- snnTorch 0.9.4 snn.Leaky already has a `state_quant` argument (MEASURED signature). The cheapest byte-neuron pilot is not a new class: run the existing harness with membrane quantised to 8 levels / 256 levels and see what accuracy survives. That is one config flag and ~142 s, and it bounds what any uint8-state neuron can do before writing any code.
- The dead-head repo (scratchpad/dead-head) is the owner's stated thread and carries the real equation and measured base-rate data; the SNN reading should have mapped tau_death = 0.96/sqrt(d) onto something measurable in an SNN (e.g. mean synaptic coupling of a hidden neuron to the next layer's membrane, normalised by hidden width) rather than inventing a correlation score.
- 88 of 92 runs are 1 epoch, so the 'flat landscape' is a 1-epoch landscape; whether beta/threshold/reset differences survive convergence is untested. The reset result (96.10/95.24/93.26) is the only >1pp fixed-parameter effect and is the one worth re-checking at 3 epochs.
- No majority-class / base-rate number for the SOL task anywhere; without it none of the finance accuracies (including LGBM's 0.643) mean anything.
- Seed 2's falsifier threshold (20% spike edit distance) sits next to the leak floor: at beta=0.8, 0.8^8 = 0.17 of membrane memory lies beyond an 8-tick window, so a shift register of 8 bits structurally loses ~17% of the LIF's state - the test should report the distance as a function of beta, not against a single 20% bar.
- The p2 pass-through finding implies a cheaper and more decisive fix than the reader's thr sweep: with unit Bernoulli inputs and thr=1.0 every input spike fires the neuron regardless of beta; scaling input weight below 1 (e.g. 0.4) is the minimal change that makes beta observable, and it tests the neuron rather than the runner.
- Training cost is the whole CPU budget: every proposed byte-neuron experiment still does float BPTT over 25 steps; a local/Hebbian or STDP rule was not costed even though it is the only path that makes the 'integer CPU' claim true end-to-end and the only path that gives the energy ledger a job.
strongest seed: Seed 2 (byte shift-register vs recorded LIF spike train, exhaustive 256-LUT sweep on spikyNN, <1 CPU-minute) - it decides the owner's semantic question (spatial byte vs temporal byte) with a deterministic, seed-free, training-free measurement before any MNIST run, and its failure is as informative as its success. Pair it with the free `state_quant` control and the spike-count-readout baseline (two flags on the existing harness, ~5 CPU-min total) and the town learns more per token than from any of the training seeds. Seed 5 (bug-fix re-runs, <5 CPU-min) is a close second: it converts two published artefacts into real findings at near-zero cost.
weakest seed: Seed 6 (byte-neuron post-filter on LGBM leaf bits) - blocked on data that is not in the checkout, built on a finance task whose every sequence-model result is a mean over collapsed folds with no base rate, and its claim ('match LGBM lift at ~zero compute') has no source. Seed 7 is nearly as weak (KV-cache claim has no anchor in the corpus; the harness on disk does not match the JSON), and seed 4 is invalid as formulated because its premise - learnable timescales gave +1.5pp - is an epoch-count artefact (learnable runs trail fixed runs at equal epochs: 95.47 vs 96.10, 95.99 vs 96.34).

### looped-transformer
SOURCE: Primary: "Recurrent Looped Transformer" (RLT), Yifan Zhang, technical report dated 2026-09-12, read in full via pdftotext from https://yifanzhang-pro.github.io/recurrent-looped-tranformer/Recurrent_Looped_Transformer.pdf (alphaXiv page https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2609.recurrent-looped-transformer loaded and agrees; repo https://github.com/yifanzhang-pro/recurrent-looped-tranformer, Apache-2.0, paper+figures only). Related: Ouro arXiv 2510.25741 (+ HF ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B card), Huginn HF tomg-group-umd/huginn-0125, Jacobian Lens arXiv 2609.01924, Recurrent Transformer arXiv 2604.21215, T2MLR arXiv 2607.15178, Full-bandwidth Transformer arXiv 2608.08888, Latent Recurrent Transformer arXiv 2605.26797, LoopFormer arXiv 2602.11451, LoopCoder-v2 arXiv 2606.18023, MoDr (ICLR 2026 note), Saunshi et al. arXiv 2502.17416, Qwen HF API (author=Qwen). All fetched 2026-09-13/14.

The alphaXiv id 2609.recurrent-looped-transformer resolves to Yifan Zhang's 15-page technical report "Recurrent Looped Transformer" (2026-09-12). It is a DESIGN SPEC WITH ZERO EXPERIMENTS: the text says it "does not report measured efficiency or scaling results", no parameter count, no benchmarks, no weights, no code (repo = PDF + figures). Architecture: a causal encoder E builds prefix KV memory M<=t; a recurrent decoder D (L_D blocks, each = causal sliding-window self-attn over decoder activations + cross-attn to encoder memory + FFN) is applied ONCE per token, and its final hidden state s_t is gated-merged into the next token's encoder embedding (u_t = e_t + a*g_t*W_s*RMSNorm(s_{t-1})). "Looped" means the tied reference config L_E=L_D=48 shares attention/FFN weights between encoder and decoder = two logical passes through one backbone, 96 block-evals/token; "infinite depth" means the TEMPORAL path grows 48t blocks over t tokens (across prompt AND response, no reset at the prompt boundary), not extra loops per token. There is NO adaptive per-token loop count. Training = full BPTT through the sequential decoder recurrence (checkpointing, optional TBPTT); RL = exact current-policy replay rebuilding all state under current weights. Cost: prefill is inherently sequential over T decoder transitions (author concedes utilization loss); per token L_E+L_D blocks plus attention over context; KV = O((L_E+G) t d_KV + L_D min(t,W-1) d_KV^D). This is the Feedback-Transformer / Full-bandwidth-Transformer / LRT lineage (cross-token latent feedback), NOT the Ouro/Huginn lineage (intra-token depth looping) — the task's framing "how many loops" does not apply.

The measured evidence lives in the related work: Ouro-2.6B (48 layers x 4 loops, shared weights, 7.7T tokens) scores MMLU 74.6 / BBH 80.5 / MATH500 90.9 vs Qwen3-8B 76.6 / 77.7 / 62.3; performance peaks at trained loop count, degrades 1-3%/extra loop; last-step KV reuse gives 4x KV reduction at <0.15% loss; adaptive exit averages 2.5 loops for 66% MMLU. Huginn-3.5B (2 prelude + 4 recurrent + 2 coda blocks, r=4..64 at inference, 800B tokens) is the open adaptive-depth model. The Jacobian-lens paper shows Ouro re-derives its workspace every loop (no linear transport across loops) while in Huginn only the last ~2 recurrences causally matter. T2MLR finds recurrence over ~20% middle layers beats full-network recurrence and retrofits a pretrained 1.7B model; LRT/FBT get ~1.5x-token-equivalent gains at 9% / negligible decode overhead; LoopCoder-v2 finds 2 loops optimal, 3+ regress. Ouro-1.4B (24L x 4 loops, HF ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B, loops set by config total_ut_steps) is the only sub-2B open looped model and fits this box in bf16 (~2.8 GB), but transformers is not installed here. "qwen3.8 50b" does not exist: HF lists exactly Qwen3.8-27B, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (180B-A6B), Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B; nearest sensible candidates are Qwen3.8-27B (dense) or Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (MoE).

KEY FACTS:
- MEASURED (rlt.txt p.1-2, scratchpad copy): RLT is a technical report by a single author, dated 2026-09-12; abstract/intro state 'The report develops these mechanisms; it does not report measured efficiency or scaling results.' No benchmarks, no throughput, no parameter count, no weights.
- MEASURED (rlt.txt §2.6): tied reference config L_E=L_D=48; encoder self-attn and decoder SWA share Q/K/V/O and FFN; cross-attn projections, merge, norms, readout are separate; '96 logical block evaluations per token'; temporal state path = 48t decoder blocks after t tokens.
- MEASURED (rlt.txt eqs 2.9-2.11): merge is r=RMSNorm(s_{t-1}); g=sigmoid(W_g[e_t; r]+b_g); u_t = e_t + alpha * g (.) W_s r, with W_g in R^{d x 2d}, W_s in R^{d x d}; author notes scalar gate or low-rank W_s reduces overhead and that alpha init is 'a candidate, not an established stability prescription'.
- MEASURED (rlt.txt §2.4, §3.2): decoder is applied exactly once per token; no per-token loop count and no adaptive compute mechanism; prefill is a sequential path of T transitions x L_D blocks ('Encoder parallelism therefore does not imply fully parallel model prefill'); cost estimate eq 3.2: O((L_E+L_D)(T d^2 + T^2 d) + G T d^2 + L_D T min(W,T) d).
- MEASURED (rlt.txt §5): pretraining = full BPTT through recurrent outputs, decoder KV and encoder memory, with activation checkpointing; TBPTT optional; RL replay must rebuild all state under current parameters after every optimizer step (stale rollout states forbidden); behavior log-probs must include temperature/truncation.
- MEASURED (github.com/yifanzhang-pro/recurrent-looped-tranformer via WebFetch 2026-09-13): repo holds LICENSE (Apache-2.0), README, two PDFs (EN/ZH), figure, index.html; 535 stars, 62 forks; no model or training code.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2510.25741v2): Ouro-1.4B = 24 layers, d=2048; Ouro-2.6B = 48 layers; 4 recurrent steps trained, full weight sharing across steps; 7.7T tokens (6T pretrain + 1.4T anneal + 20B long-context + 300B mid-train). Base MMLU/BBH/MATH500/GSM8K: Ouro-1.4B 67.35/71.02/82.40/78.92; Ouro-2.6B 74.60/80.46/90.85/81.58; Qwen3-4B 73.19/71.14/59.60/72.86; Qwen3-8B 76.63/77.65/62.30/83.09.
- MEASURED (same page): Ouro loop scaling: T=1 ~40% MMLU, peak at T=4, T=5-8 degrade ~1-3%/step; last-step KV reuse during decoding = 4x KV memory reduction at <0.15% loss; adaptive exit averaging 2.5 steps reaches 66% MMLU with ~4x compute saving (note: MMLU here is the 1.4B run per that page's framing).
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B): trust_remote_code=True; loop count controlled by config 'total_ut_steps' (default 4); 'early_exit_threshold' (default 1.0); Apache-2.0; stored as ~1B-class bf16 safetensors (ESTIMATE ~2.8 GB on disk).
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/tomg-group-umd/huginn-0125): 3.5B params, ~1.5B prelude + ~1.5B recurrent block + ~0.5B embeddings; num_steps variable at inference, <4 'very coarse', gains up to ~64; 800B tokens on 4096 MI250X (Frontier); Apache-2.0; supports per-token exit criteria (entropy-diff, latent-diff, KL, argmax-stability) and KV-cache sharing across recurrences.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2609.01924, Jacobian lens): Ouro-2.6B: 92% of tokens match final output by loop 3; cumulative exit prob [0.03,0.14,0.34,0.49] over loops 1-4; semantic content does NOT linearly transport across loop boundaries (bulk cosine 0.27-0.46, concept ~0.1 of target); Huginn-0125: content computed by recurrence 2-3 readable through all 16, but causal read/write window is ~2 recurrences; last-window-only ablation = all-windows (17/28 answers kept); input re-injection attenuated to alpha<=0.25 destroys output (0/8 prompts).
- MEASURED (arxiv 2604.21215 abstract): Recurrent Transformer (Oncescu, Kakade et al.) — each layer attends to KV computed from its own previous outputs; exact tiling reduces HBM traffic Theta(N^2)->Theta(N log N); 150M and 300M C4 pretraining beat param-matched baselines with fewer layers.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2607.15178 abstract): T2MLR — recurrence on ~20% middle layers often beats full-network recurrence; retrofit of a pretrained 1.7B model + brief finetune improved math reasoning; parallel training via fixed Jacobi iterations.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2608.08888 abstract): Full-bandwidth Transformer — top-layer hidden state gated (GLU) into next token embedding; 1B models to 400B tokens; matches standard transformers trained with ~1.5x more tokens; negligible decode overhead; scheduled multi-pass training keeps teacher forcing.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2605.26797 abstract): Latent Recurrent Transformer — 1.3B/2.1B; interleaved parallel training at ~2x ideal token compute; matches a three-loop looped transformer in BPB with one forward per token and 9% decode latency overhead.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2606.18023 abstract): LoopCoder-v2 7B/18T tokens, parallel-loop with cross-loop position offsets: 2 loops broadly beat non-looped (SWE-bench Verified 43.0->64.4); 3+ loops regress ('only loop once').
- MEASURED (arxiv 2502.17416 abstract): a k-layer transformer looped L times nearly matches a kL-layer model on reasoning (addition, p-hop, math) but not on memorization; looped models simulate T CoT steps with T loops.
- MEASURED (papernotes MoDr, ICLR 2026): on Huginn-3.5B, 4 LoRA (rank-16 q/k/v/o) branches + hard router over a frozen backbone, <0.2% trainable params, avg recursion 32, TBPTT 8: +7.2% avg math accuracy (62.02->69.22).
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/api/models?author=Qwen&search=Qwen3.8): Qwen3.8 open models are exactly Qwen3.8-27B, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B (+FP8 variants). No 50B. Secondary source (codersera blog) says Flash-Next = 180B total / 6B active and 'no 4B/8B/14B in this generation'. Nearest 'about 50B' open Qwen: Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (MoE) or Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B.
- MEASURED (local shell 2026-09-13): torch 2.10.0+cpu, 4 threads, numpy 2.4.3, onnxruntime 1.24.4, tokenizers 0.22.2 present; transformers NOT installed; free -g shows 23 GB total, ~6 GB available (not 7); 35 GB disk free; no llama.cpp/ollama on PATH; HF cache holds only faster-whisper small/tiny.
- ESTIMATE: Ouro-1.4B at 4 loops = 96 layer-evals of d=2048 per token ~ compute of a ~5.6B dense model; on 4 arm64-N1 cores in torch bf16/fp32 expect ~0.5-2 tok/s decode. Huginn-3.5B in bf16 (~7 GB) does not fit the ~6 GB available without int8.

REUSABLE:
- /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/scratchpad/rlt.txt — full pdftotext of the RLT report (865 lines) and rlt.pdf beside it; sections 2.1-2.6 hold every equation, Appendix A the execution schedules. Copy into the graph if the digest node should cite line numbers.
- RLT merge equations (rlt.txt eqs 2.9-2.11) and decoder block order (2.12-2.16): directly implementable in ~40 lines of torch on top of any nanoGPT-style tiny model as a cross-token feedback path.
- RLT cost model eq 3.2 (prefill FLOPs) and eq 3.3 (KV storage) — reusable as the accounting formula for any encoder-memory + recurrent-decoder design.
- Open looped checkpoints: ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B (24L x 4 loops, config keys total_ut_steps, early_exit_threshold, trust_remote_code) and ByteDance/Ouro-2.6B(+ -Thinking); tomg-group-umd/huginn-0125 (num_steps at inference, per-token exit criteria, KV-sharing). Both Apache-2.0.
- LoopFormer code: https://github.com/armenjeddi/loopformer (elastic-depth training: random loop counts + step-size conditioning + shortcut-consistency loss) — the recipe for H2 below.
- Jacobian-lens companion code: https://github.com/anthropics/jacobian-lens (virtual-unrolling adapter for looped/recurrent models) — the measurement tool for H5's cross-loop 'coherence' pruning.
- Curated list: https://github.com/huskydoge/Awesome-Loop-Models.
- Local: torch 2.10 CPU + numpy already installed; existing graph nodes goal:g14, goal:g14.2, goal:g14.3, vision:local-maxxing already carry the owner's verbatim brief for this thread (no need to re-quote).

FAILURES/LESSONS:
- The alphaXiv abs/overview pages load, but they are AI summaries; the actual content is a 15-page PDF. WebFetch returns PDF bytes as binary — the fix is to save the file and run pdftotext (available at /usr/bin/pdftotext). Lesson: for any paper digest, go straight to the PDF + pdftotext and cite line ranges.
- The task premise ('how many loops, adaptive compute, benchmark vs same-param baselines') does not match the paper: RLT has no loops-per-token, no experiments and no baselines. Lesson: verify a paper's genre (spec vs empirical) before assigning extraction fields; do not fill missing fields from the Ouro/Huginn literature as if they were RLT's.
- 'qwen3.8 50b' does not resolve to any released model (HF API, 2026-09-13). Lesson: the owner's candidate must be re-stated as Qwen3.8-27B (dense, 4-bit ~15 GB, fits the 24 GB XS) or Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (MoE, 3B active — the only one plausibly CPU-runnable on this box); flag to owner before any Camber spend.
- Local box has ~6 GB available RAM (measured), not 7, and transformers is not installed; Ouro-1.4B needs transformers + trust_remote_code. Any run here needs an owner-approved install step first (this session was read-only).
- Two of the 'related' papers found are dated in the future relative to some fetch tools' priors (WebFetch called 2604.21215 'future-dated'); ignore that — today is 2026-09-13 and the arXiv ids are real.

SEEDS:
- CLAIM H1 (depth via looping at toy scale): a 4-layer tied transformer looped 4 times (~12M params) reaches >=95% of the accuracy of a 16-layer untied model (~45M params) on p-hop induction and multi-digit addition, and <80% on a pure memorization task (key-value recall) — reproducing Saunshi et al. 2502.17416 on CPU. // FALSIFIER Looped model scores <90% of the untied-16L model on both reasoning tasks, OR beats it on memorization by a similar margin (which would mean the gain is capacity, not depth). // EXP nanoGPT-style char-level model in plain torch (no transformers needed), d=256, 4 heads; three synthetic generators (p-hop, addition, kv-recall) with 200k examples each; train 4L-untied, 16L-untied, 4L-x4-looped for the same token budget on 4 threads; report accuracy vs loop count 1..8 at eval. // COST ~3-6 CPU-hours per task (ESTIMATE); $0; no GPU.
- CLAIM H2 (elastic depth): training H1's looped model with a random loop count in [1,8] plus step-size conditioning and a shortcut-consistency loss (LoopFormer recipe) yields a single checkpoint whose per-token latent-diff early exit uses <=50% of the max loop-evals at <=1% accuracy loss, versus the fixed-4-loop model which loses >10% at 2 loops (Ouro's T=1..4 curve). // FALSIFIER The elastic model at its exit-averaged budget is worse than the fixed-loop model at the same average budget, or the exit criterion fires uniformly (no per-token variance), or Ouro-style collapse at fewer loops persists. // EXP Reuse H1 code; add time/step conditioning (FiLM on the loop index) and a consistency term between the 2-step and 4-step trajectories; evaluate accuracy at forced loops 1,2,4,8 and under latent-diff and argmax-stability exits (Huginn criteria); log loop histogram per token. // COST ~4-8 CPU-hours (ESTIMATE); optional 1 GPU-hour on Camber XS to repeat at 100M scale.
- CLAIM H3 (cross-token feedback, the RLT/FBT/LRT axis): adding RLT's gated merge of the previous token's final hidden state into the next token's input (eqs 2.9-2.11, scalar gate, low-rank W_s) to a NON-looped 4L tiny model recovers >=50% of the p-hop gain that 4 loops give, at <=1.15x decode cost per token — i.e. temporal depth substitutes for intra-token depth on multi-hop tasks. // FALSIFIER Feedback model shows no improvement over the 1-loop baseline at matched tokens, or training is unstable for every alpha init in {0.05,0.1,0.3} (RLT itself flags alpha as unproven). // EXP Implement the merge on H1's model; train two ways: (a) exact sequential BPTT with TBPTT horizon 32 on short sequences (len 64) — feasible on CPU at toy scale; (b) FBT-style scheduled two-pass teacher forcing. Compare BPB and p-hop accuracy vs 1-loop and 4-loop models; measure ms/token decode on 4 threads. // COST ~6-10 CPU-hours (sequential BPTT is the slow part; ESTIMATE); $0.
- CLAIM H4 (where to recur — T2MLR vs RLT full-decoder): with H3's feedback path, feeding the previous token's MIDDLE-layer state (layers 2-3 of 4, ~50% of the stack; T2MLR's ~20% at scale) into layer 1 beats feeding the final layer's state into the input by >=2 points on p-hop at equal cost, and a retrofit of a frozen pretrained <=1B model (SmolLM2-135M or Qwen3-0.6B) with only the merge + LoRA trained on CPU improves GSM8K-style few-shot by a measurable margin. // FALSIFIER Full-decoder feedback (RLT's choice) matches or beats middle-layer feedback, or the retrofit gives no gain within the CPU budget (then the retrofit claim of T2MLR does not transfer below 1B). // EXP Toy: sweep source layer {2,3,4} x target layer {1,2} on H3's model. Retrofit: needs transformers installed (owner approval); SmolLM2-135M (~270 MB bf16) with rank-8 LoRA on q/v + a trainable merge, 2k steps of short-sequence TBPTT on 4 threads; eval on 200 GSM8K items with 4-shot. // COST Toy ~4 CPU-hours; retrofit ~12-24 CPU-hours or 1 Camber GPU-hour (ESTIMATE).
- CLAIM H5 (coherence pruning across loops — the owner's dead-head equation applied to loops/heads): in H2's elastic looped model, heads whose cross-loop Jacobian transport cosine is below a threshold (Jacobian-lens: Ouro loops re-derive, no linear transport; Huginn only last ~2 recurrences causally matter) can be pruned in all but the last 2 loops with <1% accuracy loss, and last-loop-only KV retention (Ouro: 4x KV cut, <0.15% loss) holds at toy scale; the same per-head transport matrix is a valid coupled-oscillator coupling map (phase-locking strength = transport cosine) for the SNN import. // FALSIFIER Pruning by transport-cosine is no better than random head pruning at equal sparsity, or accuracy drops >3% when early-loop KV is discarded, or the coupling map derived from Jacobians has no stable phase-locked clusters when simulated as Kuramoto oscillators. // EXP Use anthropics/jacobian-lens style virtual unrolling (or a hand-rolled torch.func.jacrev on d=256) to compute per-head, per-loop Jacobian cosines on 200 prompts; prune by threshold vs random; export the head x head cosine matrix, run a 100-node Kuramoto simulation in numpy, measure order parameter r. // COST ~2-4 CPU-hours (ESTIMATE); $0.
- CLAIM H6 (cooperation = self-speculation; vision 3): because loop count is a runtime knob, the SAME looped weights at 1 loop can draft and at 4 loops verify (self-speculative decoding); on CPU this gives >=1.5x tokens/s over plain 4-loop decoding with an identical output distribution, and a pair of tiny models (1-loop drafter A + 4-loop verifier B with shared KV, Ouro last-step KV reuse) beats either alone on accuracy-per-second. // FALSIFIER Draft acceptance rate <50% (Ouro's T=1 ~40% MMLU suggests weak drafts; the Jacobian-lens 92%-by-loop-3 number suggests a 3-loop draft instead), or memory for two KV sets exceeds the 6 GB available, or verification overhead eats the speedup on 4 cores. // EXP On H2's elastic toy model first (measure acceptance rate for draft loops 1,2,3 vs verify loops 4/8); then, once transformers is approved, on ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B in bf16 (~2.8 GB) with total_ut_steps=1 vs 4 using a hand-written speculative loop; report tok/s and acceptance on 100 GSM8K prompts. // COST Toy ~2 CPU-hours; Ouro-1.4B run ~4-8 CPU-hours at ~1 tok/s (ESTIMATE); $0.

OPEN:
- Which model did the owner mean by 'qwen3.8 50b'? No such release exists (HF API 2026-09-13). Candidates to confirm with the owner: Qwen3.8-27B dense (4-bit ~15 GB fits Camber XS 24 GB; too big for this box's ~6 GB free), Qwen3.6-35B-A3B MoE (3B active, plausibly CPU-runnable via llama.cpp offload), or Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B.
- Has anyone trained RLT at any scale? The report has no experiments and the repo has no code; a follow-up empirical paper by the same author may appear — worth a monthly re-search on 'Recurrent Looped Transformer' + 'yifanzhang-pro'.
- Does ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B's remote code run on torch-CPU (no CUDA kernels, no flash-attn hard dependency)? Unverified — requires installing transformers, which this read-only session could not do.
- What GPU is the Camber Cloud XS instance (24 GB VRAM: L4? A10G? RTX 4090?) and its price/hour — goal:g14.3(a) is still unresolved and gates any 3-GPU-hour/month plan.
- LoopFormer's actual model sizes, loop range and results table were not extracted (abstract only); the repo README should be read before H2 is briefed.
- Sequential BPTT for RLT-style feedback is the CPU bottleneck: at what toy scale does the FBT scheduled-multipass or T2MLR Jacobi approximation stop matching exact BPTT? (Needed before scaling H3/H4 past ~10M params.)
- Jacobian-lens shows per-loop supervision (Ouro) destroys cross-loop linear transport while implicit pressure (Huginn) preserves it — which regime does the owner's coherence/oscillator model want? Transport-preserving (Huginn-like) is the natural fit for coupled oscillators; this should be decided before H5 is briefed.

CRITIC (confidence 0.82):
unsupported:
- 'anthropics/jacobian-lens = virtual-unrolling adapter for looped/recurrent models' (reusable_assets, H5 tool). MEASURED: the repo exists but is the companion to Anthropic's 'Verbalizable Representations Form a Global Workspace' paper — a lens for ordinary decoders. The virtual-unrolling adapter is Wang & Reid's (Fin AI Research, arXiv 2609.01924), and that paper links NO code. H5's measurement tool does not exist off the shelf; someone must write it.
- 'Ouro last-step KV reuse: 4x KV reduction at <0.15% loss' (key_facts, H5). MEASURED on arxiv.org/html/2510.25741v2: GSM8K 78.85 vs 78.92 (0.07 pt) but MATH-500 80.40 vs 82.40 (2.0 pt). The <0.15% figure is the favourable benchmark only; H5's 'holds at toy scale' premise inherits the cherry-pick.
- 'Huginn last-window-only ablation = all-windows (17/28 answers kept)'. My re-read of 2609.01924 shows the ten-direction ablation as '0/28 vs 1/28 kept' and a separate '17/21' dual-task figure; 17/28 does not appear as cited. Direction of the claim (last window carries the causal effect) holds; the number is likely conflated.
- 'T2MLR ... parallel training via fixed Jacobi iterations' — cited to the arXiv abstract, which does not contain it (MEASURED via WebFetch of arxiv.org/abs/2607.15178). May be in the body; unverified from the cited source.
- 'MoDr (ICLR 2026) ... +7.2% avg math accuracy (62.02->69.22)' — sourced to a third-party 'papernotes' page, not the paper; not re-verified here. Low provenance.
- H1 parameter counts: '4-layer tied looped (~12M params)' and '16-layer untied (~45M params)' at d=256. MEASURED arithmetic: 16 x 12 x 256^2 = 12.6M non-embedding; 4 layers = 3.1M. The stated counts are ~3.5x too high for the stated width, or the width is wrong.
- H1/H2/H3 CPU-hour estimates ('3-6 CPU-hours per task' for 200k examples x 3 models). MEASURED here: fp32 matmul peaks at 50 GFLOPS on the 4 N1 cores; 6 x 12.6M params x 200k x 64 tokens ~ 1e15 FLOPs -> ~18 h per 16-layer run at a realistic 15 GFLOPS sustained (ESTIMATE). The seeds are ~3-5x optimistic unless examples or sequence length shrink.
- H6 'identical output distribution' + 'pair of tiny models with shared KV'. Identical distribution is true only under rejection-sampling speculative decoding, and KV cannot be shared between two different models — only between loop counts of the same weights. The two halves of the claim are conflated.
- ESTIMATE 'Ouro-1.4B in bf16 ~2.8 GB, 0.5-2 tok/s'. MEASURED here: torch bf16 matmul runs at 2.0 GFLOPS on this ARM box (fp32: 50). Ouro-1.4B at 4 loops ~11 GFLOP/token -> ~5 s/token in bf16 (ESTIMATE), and fp32 weights (5.6 GB) do not fit the 6 GB available. The plan needs int8 dynamic quant, which nobody has tested on a looped model.
errors:
- 'performance ... degrades 1-3%/extra loop' — MEASURED (2510.25741v2): Ouro-1.4B MMLU 67.45 (T=4) -> 64.49 (T=8) = ~0.75 pt/loop; Ouro-2.6B 74.60 -> 72.24 = ~0.6 pt/loop. Overstated 2-4x (the 1-3% is the total over four extra loops).
- 'stored as ~1B-class bf16 safetensors' for Ouro-1.4B is fine, but the card's hard requirement 'transformers<4.56.0 (recommend 4.54.1)' was omitted — a naive pip install of current transformers will not load the remote code. Also omitted: trained at 4K context; vLLM path ignores early exit.
- Huginn HF card lists F32 tensors ('4B params'), so the download is ~14 GB, not the ~7 GB bf16 figure; the 'does not fit 6 GB' conclusion stands but the disk/RAM plan doubles.
- The Ouro-vs-Qwen3-8B comparison is presented as a win for a smaller model, but at 4 loops Ouro-2.6B executes 192 layer-evals at d=2048 per token — roughly the per-token compute of a ~10B dense model (ESTIMATE). Looping saves parameters/RAM, not FLOPs; for vision 1 (lighter hardware) this is a footprint win only, which the summary's framing hides.
missing:
- CPU cache economics of looping (the one genuinely CPU-specific advantage): a looped tiny model whose shared weights fit in the arm-cloud's 32 MB system-level cache re-reads them from cache on every loop, so loops cost compute but not DRAM bandwidth — unlike a dense kL-layer model that streams k times more weights. This is testable in an afternoon (tok/s vs loop count for models at 8/16/32/64 MB of weights) and is the strongest reason looping matters for THIS town; the reader never mentions cache or bandwidth.
- Precision on this box: MEASURED bf16 matmul = 2 GFLOPS vs fp32 = 50 GFLOPS. Every 'bf16 on CPU' plan in the reading (Ouro-1.4B, Huginn, SmolLM2 retrofit) should be fp32 or int8-dynamic-quant. Open question the reader missed: does quantization error compound across loops (weights reused 4x) more than in a dense model? Nobody has measured it and it gates both the 8 GB box and Camber.
- The source's actual novelty — cross-token state feedback — is the closest thing in this reading to the owner's neuron-as-byte / SNN thread (state carried across ticks, one output per tick, gated merge = synapse). The reader treats RLT as 'the wrong lineage' rather than as the bridge to the owner's own thread; only H5 touches SNN and via an unrelated Kuramoto bolt-on.
- KV-cache compression thread: RLT eq 3.3 bounds decoder KV by window W (SWA) and Huginn's 'compress-s16' overwrite strategy is a concrete looped-KV compressor; neither was folded into the owner's KV-compression thread beyond Ouro's last-step reuse.
- Prior art for H6: LayerSkip (Elhoushi et al. 2024, arXiv 2404.16710) and Draft&Verify (Zhang et al. 2023) already do self-speculative decoding with early exit and shared KV; H6 is a port to loop count, not a new idea, and Ouro's T=1 output (41.2% MMLU) suggests a 3-loop draft (92% top-1 agreement per the Jacobian-lens paper) is the only viable drafter — which erases most of the speedup.
- The second box (8 GB unified RAM, Intel HD iGPU) is never addressed; Ouro-1.4B int8 (~1.4 GB) would fit it comfortably and it is idle.
- Compute-matched vs parameter-matched comparison: the reading (and H1) compares looped vs untied at matched tokens, but the looped model burns kL layer-evals per token. A knowledge-per-token-honest H1 needs a third arm: untied 4-layer trained for 4x the tokens (matched FLOPs).
- LoopFormer (H2's recipe) results and sizes were never extracted — the reader admits it; H2 is briefed on an abstract.
- Ouro's per-loop deep supervision vs Huginn's implicit training is the actual design decision behind whether cross-loop transport survives (Jacobian-lens finding); the reader lists it as an open question but no seed tests it, though it is the cheapest discriminating experiment in the whole set (two loss functions, same toy model).
strongest seed: H3 (cross-token gated feedback, RLT eqs 2.9-2.11, on a non-looped 4-layer toy model vs 1-loop and 4-loop baselines). It is the only seed that tests the SOURCE's untested premise (RLT has zero experiments), it discriminates the two lineages (temporal vs intra-token depth) with one ~40-line torch change, it is the natural bridge to the owner's SNN/neuron-as-byte thread (state across ticks), and its failure mode (alpha instability, which RLT itself flags) is knowledge either way. Caveat: run it fp32, shrink to ~50k examples x len 32 (sequential BPTT is the cost), and add a FLOPs-matched dense arm. H1 is a prerequisite harness, not the strongest seed on its own — it reproduces a published result.
weakest seed: H5 (coherence pruning across loops + Kuramoto coupling map). Three claims bundled into one, resting on a cherry-picked number (<0.15% KV loss; MATH-500 loses 2.0 pts), a tool that does not exist (the looped-model Jacobian adapter has no code; anthropics/jacobian-lens is the base lens), and an unargued leap from Jacobian transport cosines to oscillator coupling strengths with no stated mechanism. Its falsifier ('no stable phase-locked clusters') cannot fail informatively because nothing predicts clusters should exist. H6 is second-weakest: prior art (LayerSkip) already answers most of it and a 41%-MMLU 1-loop drafter will not get 50% acceptance.

### kv-cache-streaming
SOURCE: Literature sweep (WebSearch/WebFetch, 2026-09-13) for goal:g14 local-maxxing town, thread kv-cache-streaming. Primary sources: github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp discussions #23470 (asymmetric KV), #20969 (TurboQuant), #28170 (attention-aware eviction proposal, 2026-09-01), #3443 (StreamingLLM), tools/server/README.md (flags); github.com/AmesianX/TurboQuant (archived fork); arXiv 2309.17453 (StreamingLLM), 2306.14048 (H2O), 2404.14469 (SnapKV), 2406.02069 (PyramidKV), 2402.02750 (KIVI), 2401.18079 (KVQuant), 2405.05254 (YOCO), 2410.14442 / NAACL-2025-short-34 (cross-layer KV sharing study), 2604.04921 (TriAttention), 2609.03430 (Random Attention, 2026-09-03), 2603.04428 (persistent Q4 KV for multi-agent edge), 2605.03884 (QKVShare), 2407.08454 (KVMerger); kaitchup.substack.com KV-cache-of-small-MoEs (2026-03-18); HF config.json for Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-4B, Qwen3.5-0.8B; docs.vllm.ai CPU + quantized_kvcache; codersera.com Qwen3.8 lineup; local box: lscpu/free on /home/ubuntu (arm64-N1, 4 cores).

Bottom line for a 4-core ARM arm64-N1 with ~6-7 GB free and a 0.5B-4B model: the KV cache, not the weights, is the memory that kills long context on tiny dense models. Qwen3-0.6B stores 112 KB/token at f16 (28 layers x 8 KV heads x 128 dim); at 32K context that is 3.5 GiB, ~3x the Q8 weights. Every technique below is a way to shrink or bound that.

RANKED TABLE (worth testing here, best first; engine status is the real ranking key):
1. KV quantization q8_0 K + q8_0 V, upstream llama.cpp (`-ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0 -fa on`). Measured ~47% KV memory cut, KLD 0.0064 at 65K ctx, 0-4/500 ARC answer flips across three 7-27B models. V-cache quant requires flash attention; on CPU asymmetric pairs may fall to a slow path. Cost: minutes.
2. Asymmetric q8_0 K / q4_0 V, upstream. 24% more saving over q8/q8 (34 -> 26 KiB/tok on Qwen3.8-27B), KLD 0.0123; K at q4_0 alone collapses quality, V at q4_0 alone flips 1/500. Untested below 7B — that is the gap to fill.
3. Architecture choice = cross-layer/hybrid for free: Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B (hybrid Gated-DeltaNet, only 6 of 24 layers hold KV, 2 KV heads x 256) = 12 KB/token, 9.3x less than Qwen3-0.6B; GDN state is fixed (~18 MiB est). Supported in llama.cpp (day-1 GGUF). Cost of this: no context-shift on recurrent layers; use `--ctx-checkpoints`.
4. StreamingLLM/attention sinks = `--keep N --context-shift` in llama.cpp (community consensus in #3443: "a more graceful way to do --keep"). Paper: 4 sink tokens + window, 22.2x over recompute, 4M tokens stable. Measure PPL vs pure sliding window on this box.
5. Prompt-cache/KV persistence for cooperating tiny models (vision 3): `--cache-reuse N`, `--slot-save-path`, `--cache-ram`. Papers show TTFT 22-136x lower with Q4-persisted KV, +2.8% PPL (Llama 8B, M4 Pro); llama.cpp already has the machinery.
6. Ngram speculative decoding (`--spec-type ngram-simple|ngram-map-k|ngram-mod|ngram-cache`), zero extra memory, no draft model — streaming throughput lever; no CPU numbers found.
7. SWA models (Gemma 3 1B/4B): default SWA cache vs `--swa-full`; Gemma 3 report claims KV to ~1/6.
8. TurboQuant 3.25-4.25 bpw (ICLR 2026): 4.4-5.2x KV cut, +0.3% PPL on 35B, but forks only, CPU kernels are fallbacks, gen speed 3-8x slower on Metal, head_dim 80 unsupported, main fork archived. Build cost too high for this box; watch upstream.
9. Eviction (H2O 20% budget, SnapKV 8.2x mem, PyramidKV 12%, TriAttention 10.7x, Random Attention matches all at 4x with +32-43% throughput): NOT in llama.cpp or vLLM-CPU; only HF-Python. Random-eviction is the cheap CPU probe.
10. KIVI/KVQuant 2-3 bit (pre-RoPE per-channel K): research code only; llama.cpp's iq4_nl KV type is the nearest built-in.
11. CLA/YOCO/MLA/token-merging: need training or new weights — not testable on fixed GGUFs; capture as architecture notes.

Engines: vLLM CPU (aarch64) = fp32/fp16/bf16 only, no KV quant on CPU. candle/mlc: no evidence of KV quant on CPU found. llama.cpp is the only stack with all levers on ARM.

Owner's "qwen3.8 50b" does not exist: Qwen3.8 = 27B dense, Flash-Next 180B-A6B, 2.4T-A95B (no 4B/8B/14B); the tiny line is Qwen3.5 0.8B/2B/4B/9B (Feb 2026) and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B. None of the 3.8s fit 7 GB.

KEY FACTS:
- MEASURED (lscpu/free, this box 2026-09-13): arm64-N1, 4 cores, flags include asimddp (int8 dot product) but NOT i8mm, NOT sve, NOT bf16; 23 GB total, ~6 GB available; no llama.cpp binary, no llama_cpp python module, no .gguf files found under depth 4.
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B config.json): 28 layers, 16 heads, 8 KV heads, head_dim 128, max_pos 40960. ESTIMATE: 112 KB/token f16 KV -> 896 MiB @8K, 3584 MiB @32K; q8_0 59.5 KB/tok; q4_0 31.5 KB/tok.
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-4B config.json): 36 layers, 32 heads, 8 KV heads, head_dim 128. ESTIMATE: 144 KB/token f16 -> 4608 MiB @32K.
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B config.json): 24 layers, full_attention_interval 4 (6 full-attn layers), 2 KV heads, head_dim 256, linear layers 16 heads x 128x128, max_pos 262144. ESTIMATE: 12 KB/token KV (9.3x less than Qwen3-0.6B) + ~18 MiB fixed GDN state.
- MEASURED (github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/discussions/23470): Qwen3.8-27B @65K ctx KV per token q8/q8 34 KiB, q8/q4 26 KiB, q8/q5_1 29 KiB; wikitext-2 KLD q8/q8 0.006425, q8/q4_0 0.012340, q5_1/q4_0 0.017557; 500-question ARC answer flips q8/q8: 0/4/1 (Qwen2.5-7B/Mistral-7B/Qwen3.6-27B), q8/q4_0: 1/10/1; 'q4_0 on K alone reproduces the full collapse, while q4_0 on V alone changes 1/500'; asymmetric pairs fall to CPU without flash attention.
- MEASURED (WebSearch summary, llama.cpp KV quant benchmarks, Nemotron-3-Nano-30B @128K): q8_0 KV 768->408 MiB (-47%), q4_0 -> 216 MiB (-72%); q8_0 PPL delta 0.002-0.05.
- MEASURED (github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/tools/server/README.md): -ctk/-ctv values f32,f16,bf16,q8_0,q4_0,q4_1,iq4_nl,q5_0,q5_1 (default f16); -fa on|off|auto; --keep N; --context-shift/--no-context-shift (default disabled); --cache-reuse N; --swa-full; --cache-ram N (default 8192 MiB); -ctxcp/--ctx-checkpoints N (default 32); --slot-save-path; -kvu/--kv-unified; --spec-type none,draft-simple,draft-eagle3,draft-mtp,draft-dflash,draft-dspark,ngram-simple,ngram-map-k,ngram-map-k4v,ngram-mod,ngram-cache.
- MEASURED (github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/discussions/20969, 2026-03-25): TurboQuant TQ3 3.25 bpw 4.9x, TQ4 4.25 bpw 3.8x; Qwen3.5-35B PPL 6.20 vs 6.19; Qwen2.5-1.5B mixed precision +2.1% PPL; M5 Max gen 3.3-8x slower with turbo3; 'CPU 4-thread Qwen35B Q4_K_M tq3_0: 20.1 t/s prompt, 11.4 t/s gen' (no baseline given); not in upstream; Aaryan-Kapoor CPU branch.
- MEASURED (github.com/AmesianX/TurboQuant README): ARCHIVED; Qwen3.5-35B 8K: 5120->990 MiB (5.2x), PPL 6.829->6.850 (+0.3%); Gemma-4-26B gen 56.4->41.1 t/s (-27%); CPU = fallback kernels, no NEON; Qwen3-4B head_dim 80 unsupported -> q8_0 fallback (note: HF config says Qwen3-4B head_dim 128; discrepancy unresolved).
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/abs/2309.17453 StreamingLLM): 4 initial sink tokens + sliding window; up to 22.2x speedup vs sliding-window-with-recompute; stable to 4M tokens on Llama-2/MPT/Falcon/Pythia. llama.cpp #3443: 'basically a more graceful way to do --keep' (2023-10-02, no maintainer benchmark).
- MEASURED (arxiv 2306.14048 H2O): 20% heavy hitters -> up to 29x throughput vs DeepSpeed/Accelerate on OPT-6.7B/30B, up to 1.9x lower latency. (arxiv 2404.14469 SnapKV): 3.6x gen speed, 8.2x memory at 16K input; 131K vs 16K max input at batch 2. (arxiv 2406.02069 PyramidKV): matches full cache with 12% KV on LongBench.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2609.03430 Random Attention, 2026-09-03): keep prompt + uniform random per-head eviction 'matches the strongest prior evictor while serving 32-43% higher throughput'; Qwen3-4B @~4x compression: MATH500 0.874, AIME 0.610, GPQA-D 0.530, LCB 0.744; ahead in 31/60 comparisons vs SnapKV, R-KV, VaSE, TriAttention; no model <4B tested.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2604.04921 TriAttention, 2026-04-06): pre-RoPE Q/K concentration -> trig-series score, no attention matrix; AIME25 10.7x KV reduction or 2.5x throughput at 40.8% full-attn accuracy; MATH500 68.4 vs 69.6 at 1024/32K budget; needs offline calibration (stable 50k-960k tokens); models 7B-20B only.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2402.02750 KIVI): 2-bit, per-channel K / per-token V, 2.6x peak memory, 2.35-3.47x throughput. (arxiv 2401.18079 KVQuant): pre-RoPE per-channel K, <0.1 PPL degradation at 3-bit on LLaMA/Mistral; 0.82 PPL gain from pre-RoPE at 3-bit LLaMA-7B.
- MEASURED (kaitchup.substack.com KV-cache-of-small-MoEs, 2026-03-18): KV/token bf16: Qwen3-30B-A3B 96 KB, GLM-4.7-Flash 52.8 KB (MLA 512+64), Nemotron-3-Nano 6 KB (6 attn layers), Qwen3.5-35B-A3B 20 KB (10 attn layers); @32K: 3.22 / 1.77 / 0.20 / 0.67 GB.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2603.04428, 2026-02-17): Q4-persisted per-agent KV on M4 Pro fits 4x more agent contexts than FP16; TTFT 22-136x lower (Gemma 3 12B), PPL delta -0.7% / +2.8% / +3.0% (Gemma/Llama-8B/DeepSeek-Coder-V2-Lite). (arxiv 2605.03884 QKVShare, 2026-05-05): TTFT 397 ms vs 1030 ms @8K with KV handoff vs re-prefill, Llama-3.1-8B, HF stack.
- MEASURED (docs.vllm.ai CPU install + quantized_kvcache): aarch64 CPU backend = NEON, FP32/FP16/BF16 only; kv_cache_dtype fp8 documented for CUDA/ROCm only. No candle or mlc CPU KV-quant evidence found (ESTIMATE: absent).
- MEASURED (codersera.com Qwen3.8 lineup; datacamp): Qwen3.8 = 27B dense (2026-08-14), Flash-Next 180B-A6B (2026-08-26), 2.4T-A95B (2026-08-12), Max hosted; 'no 4B, 8B or 14B in this generation'; no 50B exists. Qwen3.5 small line: 0.8B/2B/4B/9B, 262K ctx, ~75% GDN layers.
- MEASURED (github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/discussions/28170, 2026-09-01): attention-aware eviction with recoverable evicted context is a design proposal only — no code, no numbers, no maintainer reply. No H2O/SnapKV PR exists in llama.cpp; SGLang has a draft H2O-style PR (#20450, batch 1 only).
- MEASURED (ik_llama.cpp, via search): Q8_KV cache type added 2025-02-19 (PR 208); Gemma 3 + quantized KV has a known severe perf regression report in llama.cpp.

REUSABLE:
- KV-size formula and calculator (verified against Kaitchup's numbers): bytes/token = n_kv_layers x n_kv_heads x head_dim x 2 (K+V) x bytes_per_elem, with bytes_per_elem f16=2.0, q8_0=1.0625, q4_0=0.5625 (block-32 GGUF). Python one-liner used this session: for (L,kv,hd) compute L*kv*hd*2*b; results table in summary.
- llama.cpp flag set for every seed below (tools/server/README.md): -ctk/-ctv {f16,q8_0,q4_0,q5_1,iq4_nl} -fa on; --keep N --context-shift; --cache-reuse N; --slot-save-path DIR; --swa-full; --ctx-checkpoints N; --spec-type ngram-*; plus llama-perplexity for wikitext-2 PPL/KLD (KLD via --kl-divergence-base on an f16 reference logits file) and llama-bench for t/s.
- Benchmark protocol reused from llama.cpp #23470: (a) wikitext-2 KLD vs f16-KV baseline at fixed ctx, (b) N=500 multiple-choice answer-flip count vs baseline — cheaper and more sensitive than PPL for 'did the answer change'.
- Model shortlist that fits 6-7 GB free with room for KV: Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B/4B Q8_0 or Q4_K_M (Qwen3ForCausalLM, head_dim 128 — all KV types apply), Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B GGUF (hybrid GDN; 12 KB/tok), Gemma 3 1B/4B (SWA, tests --swa-full). Qwen3.8 has nothing under 27B.
- Paper set to fold into the graph as doc nodes: StreamingLLM 2309.17453, H2O 2306.14048, SnapKV 2404.14469, PyramidKV 2406.02069, KIVI 2402.02750, KVQuant 2401.18079, TurboQuant (ICLR 2026, research.google blog), YOCO 2405.05254, cross-layer study 2410.14442, TriAttention 2604.04921, Random Attention 2609.03430, persistent Q4 KV multi-agent 2603.04428, QKVShare 2605.03884, KVMerger 2407.08454, RDKV joint eviction+quant 2605.08317. PDFs of 2410.14442 and NAACL-2025-short-34 were saved by WebFetch under /home/ubuntu/.claude/projects/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/tool-results/ (webfetch-1789350347395-n5bppq.pdf, webfetch-1789350366439-ka7qv7.pdf) for a later pdftotext pass.

FAILURES/LESSONS:
- WebFetch cannot read arXiv/ACL PDFs (returned 'compressed PDF stream'); the cross-layer KV sharing study's numbers (2410.14442) are therefore unextracted — use the arxiv.org/html/ URL or pdftotext on the saved file next time.
- Kaitchup's Qwen3.5 0.8B-9B requirements post is paywalled; I reconstructed the KV numbers from the HF config.json instead — always go to config.json first, it is free and exact.
- The owner's candidate 'qwen3.8 50b' does not resolve: no 50B in Qwen3.8 (27B / 180B-A6B / 2.4T-A95B) and nothing in that generation fits 7 GB. Lesson: resolve model ids against the vendor lineup before spending any budget on them; the tiny line is Qwen3.5 (0.8B-9B) or Qwen3.6-35B-A3B.
- Two sources disagree on Qwen3-4B head_dim (TurboQuant README says 80 and falls back; HF config.json says 128). Trust the config; treat fork READMEs as ESTIMATE.
- Almost every KV-eviction paper (H2O, SnapKV, PyramidKV, TriAttention, Random Attention) benchmarks on GPUs at 4B-32B with HF/vLLM; none report CPU or sub-4B numbers. The literature has no measured data for exactly this box — every seed below must generate its own baseline first.
- The TurboQuant llama.cpp effort produced 5+ forks and one archived main fork in six months with CPU as an afterthought; chasing it on this box would spend tokens on build engineering, not knowledge. Ranked 8th for that reason despite the best paper numbers.
- Read-only constraint: llama.cpp is not installed and no GGUF is present, so nothing was measured locally beyond lscpu/free; the first experiment in this thread is necessarily a build + download (banked as an open question, not done).

SEEDS:
- CLAIM On Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B (Q8_0 weights), q8_0 K + q8_0 V with -fa on costs <0.01 wikitext-2 KLD and <=1% answer flips on 200 ARC-style questions while halving KV memory, and on the N1 CPU decode t/s at 8K ctx does not drop more than 10% vs f16 KV. // FALSIFIER KLD > 0.02 or > 3% answer flips, or decode t/s falls > 10% (the CPU FA path with quantized V being slower than the f16 non-FA path is the specific unknown). // EXP llama-perplexity -m Qwen3-0.6B-Q8_0.gguf -f wiki.test.raw -c 2048 --kl-divergence-base ref.kld (ref from -ctk f16) for -ctk/-ctv in {f16/f16, q8_0/q8_0}; llama-bench -p 512 -n 128 -ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0 -fa 1 vs f16; record RSS via /usr/bin/time -v. // COST ~25 CPU-min (0.6B); ~50 CPU-min (1.7B)
- CLAIM The K-vs-V asymmetry measured on 7-27B (q4_0 on K collapses, q4_0 on V is ~free) holds on a 0.6B model: q8_0 K / q4_0 V is within 2x the KLD of q8/q8, while q4_0 K / q8_0 V is >5x worse. // FALSIFIER q8/q4_0 KLD exceeds 2x q8/q8, or q4_0/q8 is not clearly worse than q8/q4_0 (tiny models may have a different K sensitivity because 8 KV heads at 128 dim carry more redundancy per head than a 27B). // EXP Same llama-perplexity KLD run over the 4 pairs {q8,q4_0} x {q8,q4_0} plus iq4_nl on K; 512-token ctx to keep it under budget; plot KLD per pair. // COST ~40 CPU-min
- CLAIM Choosing the hybrid architecture beats every runtime trick: Qwen3.5-0.8B at 32K ctx uses <450 MiB of KV+state (vs ~3.5 GiB for Qwen3-0.6B f16), and its prompt-processing t/s on the N1 at 4K prompt is at least 0.5x Qwen3-0.6B's despite the GDN CPU kernels. // FALSIFIER Measured KV+state at 32K > 600 MiB, or GDN prompt processing is < 0.3x the dense model (which would make the 9x memory win unusable for streaming). // EXP llama-bench -m Qwen3.5-0.8B-Q8_0.gguf -p 4096 -n 64 -c 32768 vs Qwen3-0.6B same; read 'KV self size' / 'recurrent state' lines from llama.cpp load log; confirm --context-shift is refused and --ctx-checkpoints works. // COST ~30 CPU-min
- CLAIM StreamingLLM-equivalent (--keep 4 --context-shift, ctx 2048) on a 0.6B model streaming a 32K-token text keeps wikitext PPL within 15% of the full-context PPL, while --keep 0 sliding-window degrades by >50% — reproducing the 4-sink-token effect at tiny scale. // FALSIFIER --keep 4 and --keep 0 PPL differ by < 5% (sinks do not matter at 0.6B), or --keep 4 PPL is > 30% above full-context. // EXP llama-cli / llama-perplexity with -c 2048 --keep {0,4,64} on a 32K concatenated wiki text with context shift on; compare to -c 32768 -ctk q8_0 (fits in ~2 GiB) as the 'full' reference. // COST ~45 CPU-min
- CLAIM For two cooperating tiny models sharing a system prompt (vision 3), llama-server --cache-reuse 256 plus --slot-save-path cuts time-to-first-token on the second agent's turn by >5x at a 4K shared prefix on the N1 (papers show 11-136x on M4 Pro at 8B), with byte-identical outputs at temperature 0. // FALSIFIER TTFT gain < 2x, or outputs differ (KV shifting/reuse changing logits). // EXP llama-server -m Qwen3-0.6B-Q8_0.gguf -np 2 --cache-reuse 256 --slot-save-path /tmp/slots; send the same 4K system prompt + two different user turns; time /completion with and without cache; also save slot, restart, restore, re-time. // COST ~20 CPU-min
- CLAIM Ngram speculative decoding (--spec-type ngram-map-k) on a 0.6B-1.7B model gives >=1.3x decode t/s on repetitive/agentic outputs (code, JSON, tool calls) on the N1 with zero extra memory, and <1.05x on free prose. // FALSIFIER < 1.1x on the repetitive workload (verification overhead on a 4-core CPU eating the gain), or any output divergence at temperature 0. // EXP llama-server --spec-type ngram-map-k vs none; 20 prompts asking to re-emit/transform a 1K-token JSON; measure tokens/s from server timings; diff outputs. // COST ~30 CPU-min
- CLAIM Random per-head KV eviction (Random Attention, 2609.03430) keeping the prompt and 25% of generated KV reproduces on Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B in HF transformers on CPU: GSM8K accuracy on 100 problems stays within 3 points of full cache, and a SnapKV-style attention-score evictor at the same budget is not better by more than 2 points. // FALSIFIER Random eviction drops >5 points vs full cache at 25% budget on the 0.6B (tiny models may lack the redundancy that makes random work at 4B+), or scored eviction beats random by >3 points. // EXP Python + transformers on CPU (bf16 unsupported on N1 -> fp32), Qwen3-0.6B, custom DynamicCache subclass that randomly drops keys per head after each 256 generated tokens; 100 GSM8K, max_new_tokens 512; 4 threads. // COST ~60 CPU-min for 0.6B (ESTIMATE; fp32 CPU decode ~5-8 t/s); 1.7B exceeds the 1-hour cap
- CLAIM On Gemma-3-1B (SWA 1024, 1:5 local:global), llama.cpp's default SWA cache uses <25% of the KV memory of --swa-full at 16K ctx with identical logits for prompts shorter than the window, so SWA-native tiny models are the second-best 'free' KV win after hybrid GDN. // FALSIFIER Memory ratio > 40%, or logits differ for prompts < 1024 tokens. // EXP llama-bench / llama-perplexity -m gemma-3-1b-it-Q8_0.gguf -c 16384 with and without --swa-full; read KV size from the load log; compare top-1 logits on a 512-token prompt via llama-cli --logits-all or the server's /completion n_probs. // COST ~20 CPU-min
- CLAIM For the head-pruning / coherence thread: the pre-RoPE Q/K concentration that TriAttention relies on (Q,K clustering around fixed centers per head) is measurable on Qwen3-0.6B and its per-head Mean Resultant Length ranks heads the same way a dead-head coherence score does — i.e. one statistic serves both KV eviction and head pruning. // FALSIFIER Spearman correlation between per-head MRL and the town's coherence score < 0.3 across the 28x16 heads. // EXP HF Qwen3-0.6B fp32 on CPU, hook q_proj/k_proj outputs pre-RoPE over 50k tokens of wikitext, compute per-head mean direction and MRL; compare to the coherence-pruning score once the sibling thread defines it. // COST ~15 CPU-min

OPEN:
- Does llama.cpp's CPU flash-attention path with quantized V (required for -ctv q8_0/q4_0) run faster or slower than non-FA f16 on arm64-N1 (asimddp, no i8mm/sve)? No published ARM number; seed 1 measures it.
- Qwen3-4B head_dim: HF config says 128, the TurboQuant fork README says 80 — which is right, and does it change the KV-type applicability?
- How fast are llama.cpp's Gated-DeltaNet CPU kernels on ARM for Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B prompt processing? (Ollama issue #14579 reports Qwen3.5 slower than llama.cpp; no ARM t/s found.)
- Is there any measured CPU (not GPU) number for ngram speculative decoding in llama.cpp? None found.
- Random Attention (2609.03430) was tested only at 4B+; does per-head random eviction survive at 0.6B where head redundancy is lower?
- What is the cross-layer KV-sharing study's (2410.14442) exact PPL/throughput at 2x, and did any config work by fine-tuning alone (needed for a Camber 3-GPU-hour budget) rather than pretraining? PDF saved but unextracted.
- Which GGUF weight quant to pair with KV quant on this box: Q8_0 vs Q4_K_M for 0.6B-4B — does weight quant error compound with KV quant error (KLD additive or worse)?
- Prerequisite banked for the director: this task was read-only; llama.cpp is not built on the box and no GGUF is downloaded. A build (cmake, ~10 CPU-min) and ~1-3 GB of GGUF downloads (Qwen3-0.6B Q8_0, Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0, Gemma-3-1B Q8_0) precede every seed above.
- Owner's 'qwen3.8 50b' — was a different model meant (Qwen3.8-27B, Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, or Qwen3.5-9B)? None of the 3.8 line fits 7 GB free RAM even at Q4; needs owner confirmation.

CRITIC (confidence 0.78):
unsupported:
- 'On CPU asymmetric pairs may fall to a slow path' (summary item 1-2) — #23470's actual line is that mixed K/V pairs 'all go to CPU and PP tanks' on a CUDA build without GGML_CUDA_FA_ALL_QUANTS. It is a GPU-offload caveat and says nothing about a CPU-only box; the reader transplanted it.
- 'M5 Max gen 3.3-8x slower with turbo3' used to rank TurboQuant 8th — #20969 states the 8x degradation (85.5->10.7 t/s) was an OLDER build, fixed by dequant optimisation; TQ3 now holds 98.7-99.5% of q8_0 speed to 32K. The ranking rationale cites a superseded number from the same source.
- 'CPU 4-thread Qwen35B Q4_K_M tq3_0: 20.1 t/s prompt, 11.4 t/s gen (no baseline given)' — the discussion DOES give the baseline: 19.3 / 10.6 t/s. TQ3 on CPU was not slower; the 'CPU kernels are fallbacks' framing is not supported by the one CPU datapoint the source contains.
- 'Qwen3.5-35B PPL 6.20 vs 6.19' attributed to #20969 — the discussion I fetched reports TQ3 5.471 vs 5.414 (+1.1%) for Qwen3.5-35B-A3B; the 6.20/6.19 pair may come from the archived fork README (6.829->6.850 there) or another post — provenance is muddled.
- 'q5_1/q4_0 KLD 0.017557' — not found in #23470 on re-read; the pair the thread reports is q8_0/q5_1 = 0.007628. Treat 0.017557 as unverified.
- Seed 7's framing that Random Attention 'matches' at ~4x — the paper's own Qwen3-4B full-cache column is MATH500 0.939 vs 0.874, LCB 0.807 vs 0.744, i.e. 6.3-6.5 point drops. It matches prior EVICTORS, not full cache. The reader omitted the full-cache column entirely.
- 'Gemma 3 report claims KV to ~1/6' — no URL, no page; unverified.
- 'Qwen3.8-27B dense' — sources describe it as dense with HYBRID attention; if hybrid, its KV/token in #23470 (34 KiB @ q8/q8) is not a plain-GQA number and should not be scaled to a dense 0.6B by analogy.
- 'GDN state ~18 MiB est' is labelled ESTIMATE correctly, but it assumes f32 state; llama.cpp stores recurrent state in f32 so this is plausible — recomputed at 18 x 1 MiB, consistent.
errors:
- Failure log says 'WebFetch cannot read PDFs ... numbers unextracted' for 2410.14442 — /usr/bin/pdftotext is installed on this box and extracts the saved file in one command. The banked open question ('did any config work by fine-tuning alone?') is answered inside that PDF: Appendix D initialises the nine 1.1B configs from the TinyLlama 2.5T checkpoint (MLKV uptraining scheme) with 'the same training details as Section 4.2' = 100B SlimPajama tokens. That is orders of magnitude beyond a 3-GPU-hour Camber budget, so the cross-layer thread is NOT testable on this town's compute. The paper also trains 110M models — a genuinely tiny-scale data point the reader never mentions.
- TurboQuant CPU baseline misreported as absent (it is 19.3/10.6 t/s) and the Metal slowdown misreported as current (it was fixed). Both errors point the same direction: TurboQuant is less costly than the reader's rank-8 justification claims. The remaining true blocker is only that it is unmerged and the main fork is archived.
- #23470 caveat about asymmetric pairs misattributed to CPU; it is a CUDA-offload caveat.
- Seed 7 cost estimate '~60 CPU-min' for 100 GSM8K x 512 tokens in fp32 HF transformers on a 4-core N1 is off by ~3-5x: 51K generated tokens at an ESTIMATE 3-6 t/s fp32 decode = 2.4-4.7 h, and the reader's own note that bf16 is unavailable makes fp32 mandatory.
- Seed 7's claim is already contradicted at 4B by its own cited paper (6.5-point MATH500 drop at 4x), so the falsifier threshold 'within 3 points of full cache' is set below the published result before the experiment starts.
missing:
- Bandwidth, not bytes: on a 4-core N1 decode is memory-bandwidth bound, and every decode step re-reads the whole KV. At 32K ctx a 0.6B f16 KV is 3.5 GiB per token; at an ESTIMATE ~20-25 GB/s DDR4 that is ~150+ ms/token from KV traffic alone, dwarfing the 0.6 GB of Q8 weights. KV quant is therefore a SPEED lever on CPU, not only a memory lever — this makes seed 1 stronger than the reader argues and should be its primary metric (t/s vs ctx length), not RSS.
- Prefill wall-clock: at ESTIMATE 100-300 t/s prompt processing on the N1, a 32K prompt takes 2-5 minutes. The 'kills long context' framing assumes contexts the box can barely reach in wall-clock; for the owner's multi-agent use (2-8K shared prefixes) KV is <1 GiB even at f16 and weights dominate. The reader never states at what context length KV overtakes weights on THIS box (~5K tokens f16 for 0.6B Q8: 5K x 112 KiB = 560 MiB ~ weights).
- #20969's K/V norm-disparity finding (K/V magnitude ratio up to 182x in Qwen2.5-1.5B; uniform K=6/V=3 gives +78.1% PPL while outlier-aware mixed gives +2.1%) is direct small-model evidence for seed 2's K-vs-V asymmetry and was missed despite being in a cited source.
- The second box (8 GB, old Intel HD iGPU) is never mentioned; llama.cpp Vulkan/SYCL on Intel iGPU shares system RAM and would let KV quant be compared across two CPU-class hosts. Also the Camber 24 GB GPU (3 h/month) is unused in every seed — KLD reference-logit generation for seed 1/2 could run there in minutes and save CPU-hours.
- Qwen3.5-0.8B is a VLM with a vision tower; GGUF conversions may strip it, but weight-size and llama.cpp support claims ('day-1 GGUF') should name the specific GGUF repo and its text-only status.
- asimddp (sdot) matters for which KV types are fast: q8_0 K/V dot products use int8 sdot on N1, whereas q4_0/iq4_nl decode paths without i8mm rely on repacking that may not help FA. This is a concrete reason to expect q8_0 KV to be FASTER than f16 on this box and q4_0 V to be no faster — testable in seed 1 and unpredicted by the reader.
- No mention of llama.cpp's `--cache-type` interaction with `--kv-unified` for the multi-agent seed (shared KV buffer across sequences is exactly vision 3's substrate) or of prompt-cache hit accounting via `/metrics`; seed 5 measures TTFT only and could also measure bytes shared.
- Cross-layer study's 110M-parameter results are the only sub-1B KV-sharing numbers in the literature set and directly bound how much redundancy a tiny model's KV carries — relevant prior to seeds 2 and 7 but never extracted.
- No seed measures accuracy on the owner's stated axis (benchmarks over time, vision 2); everything is PPL/KLD/flip-count. A cheap 200-question MC harness reused across all seeds would make results comparable — the reader lists the #23470 protocol as reusable but no seed except 1 actually uses answer-flips.
strongest seed: Seed 3 (hybrid GDN Qwen3.5-0.8B vs dense Qwen3-0.6B: KV+state at 32K and GDN prompt-processing t/s on ARM). ~30 CPU-min answers a question with zero published data (Gated-DeltaNet CPU kernel speed on arm64-N1), the memory delta is 9x by construction from verified configs so the experiment cannot be uninformative, and its outcome decides which model line every other seed should run on. Seed 1 (q8_0 KV) is the cheapest and must run first as calibration, but it mostly re-confirms a result already established at 7-27B; its only new knowledge is the CPU-FA speed question, which the bandwidth angle above predicts.
weakest seed: Seed 7 (Random Attention eviction replicated in HF transformers fp32 on CPU). Its claim ('within 3 points of full cache at 25% budget') is already falsified at 4B by the cited paper (MATH500 0.939 -> 0.874 at 4x); the reader hid that by omitting the full-cache column. Its cost is under-estimated 3-5x (fp32 decode on 4 cores over 51K tokens is hours, not 60 min), it requires a custom DynamicCache subclass (build engineering), it cannot transfer to llama.cpp where every other seed lives, and the paper's own mechanism (redundancy across heads and restated reasoning) is exactly what a 0.6B model lacks. Seed 9 is a close second for weakness: it depends on a coherence score the sibling thread has not defined, so it cannot be scored yet.

### tiny-models-cpu
SOURCE: tiny-models-cpu sweep, 2026-09-13. Sources: HF model cards (Qwen/Qwen3.5-{0.8B,2B,4B,9B}, Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B, Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM3-3B, microsoft/Phi-4-mini-instruct, meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct, unsloth/gemma-4-E2B-it-GGUF, unsloth/gemma-4-E4B-it-GGUF), github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3.8, ai.google.dev/gemma/docs/core/model_card_4, simonwillison.net/2026/Aug/16/qwen-38-27b/, artificialanalysis.ai/articles/qwen3-5-small-models, liquid.ai LFM2 blog + arxiv 2511.23404, thomasvanlaere.com Cobalt-100 post, chipsandcheese.com/p/deep-diving-neoverse-n1, cambercloud.com/pricing, openrouter.ai/api/v1/models (fetched live), plus local lscpu/free/ps and a numpy bandwidth microbench on this box.

(1) Tier winners (model-card numbers, 2026): 0.8B: Qwen3.5-0.8B (Apache-2.0; MMLU-Pro 29.7, MMLU-Redux 48.5, IFEval 52.1 non-thinking). 1-2B: Qwen3.5-2B (MMLU-Pro 55.3 / 66.5 thinking, IFEval 61.2 / 78.6, GPQA 51.6 thinking); LFM2-1.2B (IFEval 74.89, GSM8K 58.30, Liquid claims 2x Qwen3 CPU decode); Llama-3.2-1B (MMLU 49.3, IFEval 59.5, GSM8K 44.4). 2-3B: Gemma 4 E2B (2.3B effective / 5.1B total; MMLU-Pro 60.0, GPQA 43.4, LCB v6 44.0; Q4_K_M 3.11 GB), SmolLM3-3B (IFEval 76.7, GSM-Plus 72.8), Llama-3.2-3B (MMLU 63.4, IFEval 77.4, GSM8K 77.7). 3-4B: Qwen3.5-4B (MMLU-Pro 79.1, GPQA 76.2, IFEval 89.8, LCB 55.8), Gemma 4 E4B (MMLU-Pro 69.4, GPQA 58.6; Q4_K_M 4.98 GB), Phi-4-mini 3.8B MIT (MMLU 67.3, GSM8K 88.6, MATH 64.0). 7-9B: Qwen3.5-9B (MMLU-Pro 82.5, GPQA 81.7, IFEval 91.5, LCB 65.6, TAU2 79.1). Nobody publishes HumanEval/GSM8K on 2026 cards any more — LCB v6 / GSM-Plus / IFEval are the comparable columns.
(2) "qwen3.8 50b" does not exist. Open Qwen3.8 today = Qwen3.8-27B (dense, Apache-2.0, 262k ctx, Q4_K_M 17 GB, released 2026-08-14), Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B (MoE, 2026-08-12), Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (125B-A6B + 51B n-gram emb + 4B MTP, qwen-community-1.0). Most plausible referent: Qwen3.8-27B — its bf16 checkpoint is ~54 GB, i.e. "50 GB" not "50B"; it is the model the owner's "quantize a little, parallelize, get off OpenRouter" describes, and Q4 (17 GB) fits a 24 GB XS GPU. Runner-up: Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (Apache-2.0, 3B active, UD-Q4_K_M 22.1 GB). goal:g14.3's "dense 50B at 4-bit is 25+ GB" premise is therefore false.
(3) This box MEASURED: 4x arm64-N1, flags include asimddp (dotprod) but NO i8mm / SVE / bf16; numpy copy bandwidth 20.1 GB/s single-process, 65.7 GB/s aggregate over 4 processes; RAM 23.4 GB total but only 6.6 GB available, 4 GB swap fully used (firefox 5.3 GB, ffmpeg 4.8 GB, Xtigervnc 4.5 GB RSS); 34 GB disk free; no llama.cpp, no GGUFs, no ollama daemon. CPU tok/s ESTIMATE (Q4_K_M, 4 threads, tg): 0.8B 30-60, 2B 15-30, 4B 7-14, 8-9B 4-8, A3B MoE 8-15 IF 22 GB were free (it is not). Blog-reported A1 4-OCPU: 7B Q4_K_M 5-8 and 8-12 tok/s; Qwen2.5-3B 10.57 tok/s (core count unstated); Pi 5 (4x A76, N1's sibling core) Qwen3-1.7B Q4_K_M ~9 tok/s.
(4) 8 GB box fits ≤~5 GB Q4 weights: Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B, Gemma 4 E2B/E4B, SmolLM3, Phi-4-mini, LFM2; Qwen3.5-9B borderline; nothing A3B/27B.
(5) OpenRouter live: deepseek-v4-flash $0.0899/$0.1797 per 1M in/out (1M ctx); qwen3.5-9b $0.10/$0.15; gemma-3-4b-it $0.05/$0.10; llama-3.2-1b $0.027/$0.201; qwen3.8-27b $0.214/$2.55; qwen3.6-35b-a3b $0.10/$0.90; gemma-4-26b-a4b:free $0. Camber XSMALL = 1 GPU (type unlisted), 5 CPU, 50 GB RAM, 3 credits/h.
