- CLAIM Hybrid Gated-DeltaNet models (Qwen3.5-2B/4B) decode at ≤0.7x the tok/s of plain-transformer peers of equal Q4 byte size (Llama-3.2-3B, SmolLM3-3B, LFM2-2.6B) on llama.cpp CPU today, so the 'best benchmark model' and the 'fastest model' at ≤4B are different models on this hardware. // FALSIFIER Qwen3.5-2B and Qwen3.5-4B tg128 within 10% of a same-byte-size plain transformer; or LFM2 does not beat Qwen3 by Liquid's claimed ~2x. // EXP Same llama-bench harness; pair models by GGUF bytes (Qwen3.5-2B vs Llama-3.2-3B-ish vs LFM2-1.2B/2.6B; Qwen3.5-4B vs SmolLM3-3B vs Phi-4-mini); report tok/s per GB and pp512 too; also test at -c 8192 with a real 6k-token prompt to expose linear-attention prefill behaviour. // COST ~1 h after seed 1's downloads
- CLAIM For parent/kid agent use the binding constraint on this box is prefill, not decode: a 4B Q4 model processes an 8k-token prompt in >2 minutes on 4 N1 cores, so KV-cache reuse (prompt caching / --cache-prompt, slot save) is worth more than any decode optimization. // FALSIFIER pp measured ≥100 tok/s for a 4B model (8k prompt < 80 s), or llama-server prompt-cache hit reduces a repeated 8k-prefix turn by <50%. // EXP llama-server with Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M, -c 16384, --cache-prompt; send the same 8k-token system prefix with 5 different 200-token tails; time first-token latency for cold vs warm prefix; repeat with KV cache type q8_0 vs f16 and measure RSS. // COST ~30 min, 0 $
- CLAIM Knowledge-per-CPU-second (accuracy x tok/s) at ≤4B is maximized by Qwen3.5-4B with thinking OFF; turning thinking ON raises GSM8K/IFEval accuracy but multiplies tokens 5-20x so accuracy-per-second falls below Gemma-4-E4B or Phi-4-mini non-thinking. // FALSIFIER Thinking-on Qwen3.5-4B (or 2B) achieves higher accuracy-per-CPU-second than any non-thinking model, or Phi-4-mini/Gemma-4-E4B beats Qwen3.5-4B non-thinking on the GSM8K+IFEval slice outright. // EXP 100-item stratified slices of GSM8K and IFEval via llama-server OpenAI API (temperature 0, max 512 / 4096 tokens for off/on); for each of Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B (x thinking off/on), Gemma-4-E2B/E4B, SmolLM3-3B, Phi-4-mini, LFM2-1.2B record accuracy, total generated tokens, wall seconds; rank by accuracy / CPU-second and by accuracy / token (the town moral). // COST ~4-8 h wall unattended (serial, 4 threads), 0 $; owner's 'knowledge per token' metric materializes here
- CLAIM Memory contention on this box (6.6 GB free, swap exhausted) costs >30% tok/s for any model whose Q4 file exceeds ~3 GB, and makes Qwen3.5-9B / Qwen3.6-35B-A3B / Qwen3.8-27B unrunnable here regardless of theory; the 8 GB [region] box, if it has nothing else resident, will out-run this box on ≤4B models despite older silicon. // FALSIFIER Qwen3.5-9B Q4_K_M (~5.5 GB) runs at ≥4 tok/s under current load with no page-thrash (major faults flat), or tok/s with firefox/ffmpeg/VNC stopped differs by <10%. // EXP Run llama-bench Qwen3.5-4B and Qwen3.5-9B under current load while sampling /proc/vmstat pgmajfault and MemAvailable each second; then, only if the owner permits stopping the desktop tenants, repeat; separately run the identical ladder on the [region] box (record its lscpu, flags, DDR type) and compare tok/s per model. // COST ~1 h per box, 0 $; needs owner say-so before touching firefox/ffmpeg/VNC

OPEN:
- Which model did the owner mean by 'qwen3.8 50b'? Best guess Qwen3.8-27B (bf16 ≈ 54 GB) — needs one owner confirmation; the alternative Qwen3.6-35B-A3B changes every GPU-offload plan (3B active vs 27B dense).
- What GPU is Camber's XSMALL (1 GPU, 5 CPU, 50 GB RAM, 3 credits/h)? 24 GB VRAM suggests L4 / A10 / RTX A5000 — bandwidth differs 3x between them and sets 27B-Q4 tok/s; also what does 1 credit cost and does '3 GPU-hours/month' mean 9 credits?
- Are firefox / ffmpeg / Xtigervnc permanent tenants on this box? If yes the practical envelope is ≤4B Q4 models (~3 GB), not the 23 GB nominal; if they can be paused, Qwen3.5-9B and even Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (22 GB, mmap) become measurable.
- What exactly is the [region] box CPU (model, AVX2/AVX-512, DDR type)? 'Intel HD iGPU, very old' could be anything from a 2013 Haswell to a 2019 Comet Lake; tok/s spread is 3x.
- Does llama.cpp's KleidiAI/repack path give N1 (dotprod only, no i8mm) any Q4_0 speedup at all, or is the 'Q4_0 is fastest on ARM' folklore i8mm-specific?
- Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B cards omit GSM8K/HumanEval/AIME; is the 0.8B's thinking-loop tendency (reported by artificialanalysis) a disqualifier for unattended kid use, or fixable with a token cap?
- Gemma 4 E2B is 5.1B real params (PLE embeddings) at 3.11 GB Q4 — does its decode speed track 2.3B or 5.1B on CPU (embeddings are lookup, not matmul)? Decides whether it belongs in the 2B or 4B speed tier.
- Is the owner's 'get off OpenRouter in bursts' economically real: deepseek-v4-flash output is $0.18/1M; a 4B model at ~10 tok/s produces 36k tok/h — what is the owner's implied $/h for this box, and does 'knowledge per token' rather than $ per token decide it?

CRITIC (confidence 0.7):
unsupported:
- Tier table mixes inference modes without saying so. MEASURED: Qwen3.5-2B card has explicit non-thinking (MMLU-Pro 55.3, IFEval 61.2) vs 'Knowledge & STEM (Thinking)' (66.5, IFEval 78.6, GPQA 51.6 thinking-only) tables; Qwen3.5-4B and 9B cards publish ONE unlabelled table and state 'Qwen3.5 models operate in thinking mode by default'. So 4B MMLU-Pro 79.1 / GPQA 76.2 and 9B 82.5 / 81.7 are very likely thinking-mode, while Gemma 4 E4B 69.4, Phi-4-mini MMLU-Pro 52.8, SmolLM3 no-think are non-thinking. The '3-4B winner: Qwen3.5-4B' ranking and seed 4's premise ('thinking OFF Qwen3.5-4B maximizes accuracy x tok/s') rest on numbers that may be thinking-ON.
- 'Camber XSMALL = 1 GPU' — MEASURED: the pricing page as fetched lists XSMALL as 5 CPU / 50 GB RAM / 3 credits/h with no GPU count or type in the on-demand table (only reserved XLARGE/XXLARGE name A100/H100). The '1 GPU' is inferred, not read.
- LFM2 '2x faster decode and prefill than Qwen3 on CPU' — MEASURED arxiv 2511.23404 abstract says 'up to 2x faster prefill and decode on CPUs compared to similarly sized models'; 'up to' and 'similarly sized' — the Qwen3-specific 2x is Liquid's blog marketing, not the paper. Seed 2's falsifier ('LFM2 does not beat Qwen3 by ~2x') treats a marketing ceiling as a prediction.
- Bandwidth microbench: '20.1 GB/s copy, 65.7 GB/s aggregate over 4 processes' — my re-run: copy 16.5 GB/s if read+write are both counted, sum 10.4 GB/s. The reader never states whether copy counts bytes once or twice, and the 65.7 aggregate (near-perfect 4x scaling) was not reproduced. Seed 1's whole premise (compute-bound, not bandwidth-bound at 4 threads) flips depending on that factor of two: at 65 GB/s a 2.5 GB Q4 4B model has a ~26 tok/s ceiling (compute-bound if 7-14 measured); at ~33 GB/s the ceiling is ~13 tok/s (bandwidth-bound).
- 'KleidiAI/i8mm Q4_0 fast path not available; dotprod path only' — llama.cpp's ARM repack has a dotprod-only layout (the historical Q4_0_4_4 / GGML_CPU_AARCH64 path) distinct from the i8mm (4x8) and SVE (8x8) ones; the reader presents the dotprod path as absence of a fast path. Seed 1 therefore tests something the ggml design already intends, which lowers its surprise value, and the open question 'does repack give N1 anything' is answerable by reading ggml-cpu source before spending 2 h of downloads.
- ollama/ollama#14579 and ggml-org/llama.cpp#19366 numbers (15-20 vs 100 tok/s; 24 vs 60 t/s) — tagged MEASURED but not re-opened by me; also they are GPU/Apple-silicon reports being used to support a CPU-kernel-lag claim.
- 'Qwen3.8-27B released 2026-08-14' — card as fetched says only 'August 2026'; the exact day comes from secondary sources.
- All tok/s numbers for this box are ESTIMATE, correctly tagged, but the summary sentence 'CPU tok/s ESTIMATE ... 0.8B 30-60' has no derivation shown — neither from the bandwidth ceiling nor from the Cobalt-100 per-thread scaling — so it cannot be checked.
errors:
- Open question 'what does 1 credit cost' is answered on the cited page: MEASURED cambercloud.com/pricing: '1 Camber Credit = 1 U.S. dollar'. XSMALL = $3/h; the owner's 3 GPU-hours/month cap = $9/month; Pro plan includes 20 GPU hours, Team 50, Free 0.
- The reader's own OpenRouter snapshot (or_models.json, 445 rows) contains '~deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-latest' at $0.0352 in / $0.1056 out per 1M (ctx 1,310,720) — 60% cheaper on input and 40% on output than the 'deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash' $0.0899/$0.1797 the reader quotes as the OpenRouter baseline for the get-off-OpenRouter economics. qwen3.8-27b context in the snapshot is 1,000,000, not noted.
- Mode-mixing across the tier table (see unsupported_claims #1) is a factual comparison error, not just a caveat: Qwen3.5-4B 79.1 vs Gemma-4-E4B 69.4 MMLU-Pro is not the same measurement. The one consistent cross-model column the reader already has is artificialanalysis' Intelligence Index (9B 32, 4B 27, 2B 16, 0.8B 9) and should have been used for the ladder.
- Local state numbers reproduce (flags asimddp yes / no i8mm / no sve / no bf16; MemTotal 24,550,508 kB; MemAvailable now 6.5-6.8 GB; SwapFree ~1.2 MB; firefox 5.7 GB, ffmpeg 5.0 GB, Xtigervnc 4.7 GB RSS; 35 GB free on /) — no error, but pgmajfault is already 2.01M, so the box is actively paging; the reader's microbench itself ran under that contention and should have been tagged as such.
missing:
- Alternative referent for 'qwen3.8 50b': the reader's own key_fact lists Qwen3.8-Flash-Next as '125B-A6B + 51B n-gram embedding' — a 51B component inside the current open Qwen3.8 release is at least as plausible an origin of the owner's '50b' as a 54 GB bf16 checkpoint, and it changes the answer (6B active MoE with a huge lookup table is exactly a CPU/RAM-offload candidate). Not raised.
- Gated DeltaNet's constant-size recurrent state is the owner's KV-cache-compression thread made concrete: at 262k ctx a 3:1 GDN hybrid keeps roughly a quarter of the KV a plain transformer needs. The reader treats GDN only as a speed liability (seed 2) and never connects it to the KV/streaming thread or to the 8 GB box's memory envelope, where KV, not weights, is the binding term for agent prompts.
- No seed tests vision 3 (cooperating tiny models). Speculative decoding with Qwen3.5-0.8B drafting for 4B/9B on llama.cpp CPU, or a 2-model verifier/proposer on GSM8K slices, is a same-harness, $0 experiment that directly measures 'two tiny models > sum' and was not proposed.
- Depth-recurrence / looped-transformer angle (owner's explicit candidate) is absent: none of the candidate models are looped, and no seed asks whether a 0.8B model with N-times loop matches a 2B on GSM8K per CPU-second — which is the cheapest test of vision 1+2 on this hardware.
- Gemma 4 E-series is the only family here with per-layer embeddings and KV sharing (why 5.1B total is 3.11 GB but 2.3B compute); the reader asks the right question in open_questions but does not carry it into a seed, though it is a one-line llama-bench comparison against Qwen3.5-2B.
- The Camber page names A100/H100 only for reserved tiers; the owner's '24 GB VRAM' for XS is the owner's word. A single 5-minute `nvidia-smi` on one XS ($0.25) resolves GPU type, bandwidth and therefore 27B-Q4 tok/s — cheaper than any estimate, and inside the 3 h/month budget.
- The 8 GB Intel box's iGPU is dismissed ('unlikely to beat CPU') with no evidence; OpenVINO / SYCL on old Intel HD parts is a documented llama.cpp path and worth one measurement given that box is idle and this one is not.
- Reader never checks that current llama.cpp/ollama CPU builds actually run Gemma 4 and Qwen3.5-GDN at all on aarch64 without i8mm (the cited issues are GPU/Apple); a failed load would void seeds 1-4 after the 12 GB download.
- No llama.cpp commit pinning: GDN CPU kernels changed materially through 2026, so every tok/s in seeds 1-3 must carry the build hash to be reusable — the reader's cpu_experiment for seed 1 says 'record commit' but seeds 2-5 do not.
- Owner's Doppler/secrets note (g14.md): the [region] box is the secrets hub; running benchmarks there has an operational constraint the reader did not surface.
strongest seed: Seed 2 (hybrid Gated-DeltaNet models decode at ≤0.7x the tok/s of plain-transformer peers of equal Q4 bytes on this dotprod-only N1). It is the only seed whose answer is genuinely unknown for this ISA, is decision-changing (whether the best-benchmark ≤4B model and the fastest ≤4B model are different models on the owner's actual hardware), costs ~1 h after downloads with $0, and its by-product — tok/s per GB per architecture — feeds both the KV/streaming thread and every later estimate. Seed 4 is the most on-mission (it literally instantiates the owner's knowledge-per-token metric) but is 4-8 h, currently contaminated by the thinking/non-thinking labelling error, and should run after seed 2 with modes explicitly controlled. Seed 1 should shrink to reading ggml-cpu's repack source plus one llama-bench pair before any download.
weakest seed: Seed 5 (memory contention costs >30% tok/s; the 8 GB box will out-run this one). It is an operational fact about who is logged into a desktop, not a research hypothesis: the falsifier depends on whether firefox/ffmpeg/VNC are running, needs owner permission to touch them, and its result generalizes to nothing once the tenants change. The pgmajfault counter (2.01M already) plus MemAvailable already tells the story for free; the only reusable part — 'record free RAM alongside every tok/s' — is a logging rule, not a seed. The [region]-box comparison inside it is worth keeping but belongs as a control row in seeds 1/2, not as its own claim.

### ensembles
SOURCE: Literature sweep 2023-2026 via WebSearch/WebFetch on 2026-09-13; primary URLs: arxiv.org/abs/2406.04692 (MoA), arxiv.org/abs/2502.00674 (Self-MoA), arxiv.org/html/2402.05120 (Agent Forest), arxiv.org/html/2408.03314 (Snell), arxiv.org/html/2502.06703v1 (Liu, 1B vs 405B), arxiv.org/abs/2408.00724 (Wu, inference scaling laws), arxiv.org/abs/2407.21787 (Large Language Monkeys), arxiv.org/html/2605.01566 (Wunderlich 2026, Pareto TTS), arxiv.org/html/2508.17536v1 (Debate or Vote), arxiv.org/html/2510.20963v2 (ColMAD), arxiv.org/abs/2506.07962 (Correlated Errors, ICML25), arxiv.org/html/2605.29800v1 (Nine Judges), arxiv.org/html/2608.11403 (Self-consistency backfires), arxiv.org/html/2406.12585v1 (GaC), arxiv.org/abs/2506.18203 (Weaver), arxiv.org/pdf/2605.27559 (Detection w/o Correction), huggingface.co/Skywork/Skywork-o1-Open-PRM-Qwen-2.5-1.5B, github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/discussions/10466, runaihome.com speculative-decoding 2026 post, lmsys.org RouteLLM blog, arxiv.org/abs/2305.05176 (FrugalGPT), github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3.8; local box: /home/ubuntu/work/agi/.agi/nodes/goal/g14.md, vision/local-maxxing.md

Vision 3 ("two or more tiny models are exponentially smarter than the sum") is PARTLY supported and PARTLY refuted by 2023-2026 evidence, and the split is clean.

SUPPORTED (small beats big): the wins are almost all *homogeneous sampling + an external verifier on a verifiable task*. Agent Forest: Llama2-13B GSM8K 35%→59% at 40 samples, beating 70B single (54%) — but 70B at 40 samples reaches 74%, so sampling lifts everyone. Snell 2024: compute-optimal search beats best-of-N with ~4x less compute and beats a ~14x larger model in FLOPs-matched tests, only on easy/medium questions and low inference load; on the hardest bin nothing helps. Liu 2025: Qwen2.5-0.5B 76.4% MATH-500 > GPT-4o 74.6%; Llama-3.2-3B 78.2% > Llama-3.1-405B 71.4%, with 100-1000x fewer FLOPs — but strictly dependent on a matching 7B PRM (Math-Shepherd/RLHFlow PRMs failed out-of-distribution). Wu 2024: Llemma-7B+REBASE > Llemma-34B at every FLOPs budget on MATH. Large Language Monkeys: coverage is log-linear in samples (exponentiated power law), and majority vote/reward models plateau after a few hundred samples without an automatic verifier.

REFUTED (diversity/debate/"exponential"): Self-MoA (single best model, repeated) beats heterogeneous MoA by 6.6 on AlpacaEval; quality dominates diversity. Wunderlich 2026 (ACL SRW): an 8B scaled 20x reaches only 53% MMLU-Pro vs 70B plain CoT 64.3%; an all-8B MoA scores 51.2%; small aggregators wreck MoA. Debate is a martingale (Debate-or-Vote 2025): majority vote alone explains the gains; ColMAD gives ≤4 pts only for cross-family pairs on non-saturated tasks. Errors are correlated: models agree 60% of the time when both err (ICML 2025); a 9-judge, 7-family panel has ~2.0-2.5 effective votes (Kish neff). Majority vote *hurts* 57-66% of GPQA problems for 7-8B models while aggregate moves only +3-4 pts. Verifiers detect errors that small generators cannot correct (2605.27559).

Honest verdict on "exponential": the only exponential in this literature is Condorcet-style vote convergence, which requires per-model accuracy >0.5 and INDEPENDENT errors; measured error correlation caps neff at ~2-3 regardless of ensemble size, and coverage grows log-linearly in compute. Super-additivity beyond the best member is real but small (GaC +1.4-1.9 pts; MoA +7.6 LC-win-rate pts with 70B-class members) and needs either a verifier or genuinely different reasoning processes. The lever for this town is therefore: tiny homogeneous samplers + a cheap external verifier (program execution, 1.5B PRM, Weaver-style weak-verifier ensemble distilled to 400M) + routing/cascades (RouteLLM: 85% cost cut at 95% GPT-4 quality; FrugalGPT: up to 98%), NOT debate or diverse-model mixing. Speculative decoding with 0.5B drafts gives 1.4-2.9x on GPU; CPU numbers are unmeasured and likely smaller (compute-bound). "qwen3.8 50b" does not exist: Qwen3.8 = 27B dense (Apache-2.0) and 2.4T-A95B; smallest current is Qwen3.5-0.8B.

KEY FACTS:
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2402.05120, Agent Forest/TMLR 2024): GSM8K Llama2-13B 0.35→0.59 at ensemble 40; Llama2-70B 0.54→0.74; GPT-3.5 0.73→0.85. MATH 13B 0.03→0.09, 70B 0.05→0.11. 13B ≈ 70B-single at ensemble size 15. Debate+Llama2 on code = 0 accuracy (cross-agent noise).
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2502.06703v1, Liu 2025 ICLR): compute-optimal TTS with 7B PRM: Llama-3.2-3B 78.2% MATH-500 vs Llama-3.1-405B 71.4%; Qwen2.5-0.5B 76.4% vs GPT-4o 74.6%; R1-Distill-7B 95.2% vs o1 94.8%; total FLOPs reduced 100-1000x. Search beats sampling for 0.5B-3B; best-of-N best for 32B-72B. Math-Shepherd and RLHFlow PRMs fail on out-of-distribution policies; PRMs show over-criticism, error neglect, localization bias, length bias.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2408.03314, Snell 2024): PaLM 2-S* on MATH; compute-optimal beats best-of-N with up to 4x less compute (16 vs 64 gens); beam search over-optimizes on easy bins, wins on medium, nothing wins on hardest bin 5; test-time compute beats 14x-larger pretraining only at low inference-to-pretraining ratio and on easy/medium questions.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/abs/2408.00724, Wu 2024 ICLR25): Llemma-7B + REBASE tree search outperforms Llemma-34B with majority voting on MATH at every FLOPs budget; 7B typically the compute-optimal size.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/abs/2407.21787, Large Language Monkeys): SWE-bench Lite 15.9%→56% at 250 samples; coverage vs samples log-linear (exponentiated power law) over 4 orders of magnitude; majority vote and reward models plateau beyond a few hundred samples when no automatic verifier exists.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/abs/2406.04692 + github togethercomputer/moa): MoA of six 70B-110B open models = 65.1% AlpacaEval 2.0 LC vs GPT-4o 57.5%; MoA-Lite (2 layers, Qwen1.5-72B aggregator) +1.8% over GPT-4o.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/abs/2502.00674, Self-MoA 2025): repeating the single best model beats heterogeneous MoA by +6.6 on AlpacaEval 2.0 and +3.8 avg on MMLU/CRUX/MATH; MoA is quality-sensitive and mixing lowers average member quality.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2605.01566, Wunderlich et al. ACL-SRW 2026): Llama-3.1-70B and 8B (4-bit) on MMLU-Pro/BBH; at equal compute debate +1.3 and MoA +2.7 pts over self-consistency (SC@10 = 68.7%); max +7.1 over CoT at 20x budget; 8B scaled heavily = 53% vs 70B CoT 64.3%; 8B aggregator over 70B proposers drops to 69.6%; all-8B MoA = 51.2%; self-refinement below CoT; extra debate rounds degrade. Guideline: #parallel generations should exceed #sequential aggregation layers (3 models/2 layers, 4/3, 5/4).
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2508.17536v1, Debate or Vote 2025): Qwen2.5-7B Arithmetics single 0.814 / majority 0.990 / best MAD 0.840; GSM8K 0.871 / 0.940 / 0.903; MMLU-FormalLogic 0.491 / 0.540 / 0.556. Theorem: belief sequence under debate is a martingale (E[p_t|past]=p_{t-1}); majority voting explains nearly all MAD gain; holds at 32B too.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2510.20963v2, ColMAD): competitive and consensus MAD underperform single-agent by up to 15 pts on error detection; collaborative ColMAD gains ≤4 pts only with cross-family pairs (GPT-4 + Llama-3.1) and only on non-saturated tasks; ~0 on GSM8K.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/abs/2506.07962, Correlated Errors, ICML 2025): 350+ LLMs; on one leaderboard models agree 60% of the time when both err; larger/more accurate models are MORE correlated even across providers and architectures.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2605.29800v1, Nine Judges 2026): Kish neff = k/(1+(k-1)·φ̄); 9 judges from 7 families → neff ≈ 2.0-2.5; first 5 judges give 90% of achievable independence (1.96 vs 2.18); judges 6-9 add +0.22 effective votes.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2608.11403, 2026): GPQA Diamond, 64 samples, T=0.7: majority vote lowers per-problem accuracy on 56.6% (Qwen2.5-7B) and 65.7% (Llama-3-8B) of problems while aggregate moves 34.2→36.9 and 27.3→31.3; dissenting samples commit at median 20.5 nats margin; neither plurality agreement nor token entropy predicts when voting helps.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2406.12585v1, GaC token-level ensemble): OpenChat-3.5+SOLAR-10.7B avg 58.24→59.66 (+1.42); Mixtral-8x7B+Yi-34B 59.69→61.56 (+1.87); ensembling only tokens where gate confidence <0.5 touches 6-10% of tokens at near-identical accuracy (60.96 vs 60.55) and cuts latency 102→78 ms/token; pairing models with a large accuracy gap DEGRADES the stronger one.
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/Skywork/Skywork-o1-Open-PRM-Qwen-2.5-1.5B): Best-of-N@64 with Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct policy: MATH greedy 75.2 / maj@64 78.4 / PRM-1.5B 79.8 / PRM-7B 81.9; AMC23 45.0 / 62.5 / 67.5 / 65.0; AIME24 13.3 / 13.3 / 10.0 / 13.3 — a 1.5B PRM adds +1.4 over majority on MATH and can be WORSE than majority on AIME.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/abs/2506.18203, Weaver 2025): weighted ensemble of ≤70B weak verifiers reaches 87.7% avg on reasoning/math with Llama-3.3-70B generator (vs GPT-4o 69.0, o3-mini 86.7); distilled to a 400M cross-encoder to cut cost.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/pdf/2605.27559, Detection Without Correction 2026): verifiers detect 40-60% of errors from Llama-3.1 8B/70B on GPQA/MATH/GSM8K but correction rates run 10-20 pts below detection; small generators cannot fix errors a verifier finds.
- MEASURED (lmsys.org/blog/2024-07-01-routellm): matrix-factorization router keeps 95% of GPT-4 quality on MT-Bench with 85% cost cut, sending 14% of queries to the strong model; 45% on MMLU, 35% on GSM8K. FrugalGPT (arxiv 2305.05176): up to 98% cost cut matching GPT-4, or +4% accuracy at equal cost.
- MEASURED (github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/discussions/10466, RTX 4070): Qwen2.5-Coder 0.5B draft → 7B 1.75x, 14B 2.36x, 32B 2.9x on HumanEval; general text 1.41x/1.67x/1.84x; 3B draft on 14B only 1.33x; Gemma-2 2B→9B no speedup; CPU offload noted as 10x+ slowdown (bandwidth). runaihome 2026 post: below ~60-70% acceptance you lose (41% match → 60→45 tok/s). No credible CPU-only ARM measurement found; llama.cpp issue #21453 is a proposal with no data.
- MEASURED (github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3.8, 2026-08): Qwen3.8 ships as Qwen3.8-27B dense (Apache-2.0, 2026-08-14) and Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B MoE (2026-08-12); NO ~50B variant; smallest referenced tiny model is Qwen3.5-0.8B. Owner's 'qwen3.8 50b' most plausibly = Qwen3.8-27B (or an unconfirmed 35B-A3B rumour).
- MEASURED (this box, 2026-09-13): 4 cores arm64-N1 (asimd, asimddp, no SVE), 23 GB RAM with ~6 GB available, 34 GB disk free; torch 2.10.0+cpu installed; NO transformers, NO llama_cpp, NO ollama, NO llama.cpp binaries; only faster-whisper tiny/small in HF cache. Any ensemble experiment must first build llama.cpp from source (free, ARM NEON).
- ESTIMATE (this box): llama.cpp Q4_K_M on 4 N1 cores ≈ 20-40 tok/s for a 0.6B model, 8-15 tok/s for 1.7B, 3-6 tok/s for 4B; a 200-question × 4-model × ~300-token GSM8K sweep ≈ 240k tokens ≈ 3-4 CPU-hours at 0.6B-1.7B scale. Unmeasured; first experiment should measure it.

REUSABLE:
- Kish effective-vote formula for correlated ensembles: neff = k / (1 + (k-1)·φ̄), φ̄ = mean pairwise correlation of error indicators (arxiv.org/html/2605.29800v1) — direct super-additivity meter, computable from per-question correctness vectors.
- Coverage scaling law: pass@k ≈ exp(a·k^{-b})-style exponentiated power law, log-linear in k (arxiv.org/abs/2407.21787) — fit it to tiny-model samples to test 'exponential' vs 'log-linear'.
- Martingale theorem for debate (arxiv.org/html/2508.17536v1 Theorem 2): E[p_t | p_{t-1..0}] = p_{t-1} — predicts zero expected gain from extra debate rounds; a falsifiable null for any debate experiment.
- Agent Forest code (sampling-and-voting, similarity-based voting for open-ended answers): github.com/MoreAgentsIsAllYouNeed/AgentForest
- HF search-and-learn (best-of-N, weighted BoN, beam, DVTS with PRM; vLLM-based, adaptable): github.com/huggingface/search-and-learn
- Compute-optimal TTS code + Qwen2.5-Math eval harness: github.com/RyanLiu112/compute-optimal-tts
- Small PRM runnable on CPU: Skywork/Skywork-o1-Open-PRM-Qwen-2.5-1.5B (HF); 7B variant for reference numbers.
- RouteLLM router framework + trained routers: github.com/lm-sys/routellm
- GaC token-level ensembling with union vocabulary + confidence-gated ensembling (arxiv.org/html/2406.12585v1) — only 6-10% of tokens need the ensemble.
- llama.cpp speculative docs and CLI (llama-speculative, --draft-max, --draft-p-min): github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/docs/speculative.md
- Awesome-LLM-Ensemble survey list: github.com/junchenzhi/Awesome-LLM-Ensemble (arxiv 2502.18036)
- Local PDFs saved by WebFetch: /home/ubuntu/.claude/projects/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/tool-results/webfetch-1789350746225-2wx8g7.pdf (Snell 2408.03314) and webfetch-1789350806224-qm71j8.pdf (2605.27559).
- Town context nodes: /home/ubuntu/work/agi/.agi/nodes/goal/g14.md (owner verbatim incl. 3 visions, 3 GPU-h/month), /home/ubuntu/work/agi/.agi/nodes/vision/local-maxxing.md

FAILURES/LESSONS:
- HF 'Scaling test-time compute' blog Space (huggingface.co/spaces/HuggingFaceH4/blogpost-scaling-test-time-compute) returns only Docker metadata to WebFetch; the cookbook page and README carry no numbers. The 1B>8B / 3B>70B MATH-500 claims are therefore cited only via VentureBeat + lewtun's post (Llama-3.1-8B PRM, DVTS, compute-optimal); exact N and accuracies remain unfetched — treat as reported-not-verified.
- arXiv /pdf/ URLs come back as binary and are useless to WebFetch; /html/<id> versions work. Abstract pages (/abs/) rarely contain numbers — go to /html/ first.
- Search-result summaries misattribute hardware: the '180+ tok/s on CPU with 1B draft + 8B' snippet was actually an RTX 4090 figure in the source post. Never take a hardware claim from a search summary without fetching the page.
- llama.cpp issue #21453 ('Speculative decoding for low-latency CPU inference') is a proposal template with zero measurements — searching for CPU speculative numbers yielded no credible ARM/CPU-only data anywhere; this is a genuine gap the town can fill by measuring.
- The owner's 'qwen3.8 50b' has no matching checkpoint; do not mint a node around it until the owner confirms Qwen3.8-27B (or another id). Recording a nonexistent model id as a candidate would propagate an error.
- The local box has no LLM inference stack at all (only torch-cpu and whisper); any 'CPU-runnable experiment' below has an unbudgeted step 0: build llama.cpp from source and download 2-5 GGUF files (~0.4-3 GB each) into the 34 GB free.

SEEDS:
- CLAIM H-ENS-1 (error correlation ceiling): four tiny models from different families (Qwen3-0.6B, Llama-3.2-1B, SmolLM2-1.7B, Gemma-3-1B) have mean pairwise error correlation φ̄ ≥ 0.3 on GSM8K, so their Kish effective vote count neff ≤ 2.0 and no majority-vote ensemble of them can behave super-additively. // FALSIFIER neff ≥ 3.0 (φ̄ ≤ 0.11) on ≥200 GSM8K items, or a 4-model plurality vote exceeding the best single member by more than the Condorcet-independent prediction. // EXP Build llama.cpp; run each model greedy on 200 GSM8K test items (same prompt, same extraction regex); store per-item correctness vectors; compute pairwise φ on error indicators, neff, and plurality-vote accuracy vs best single vs independent-Condorcet prediction. // COST ≈240k generated tokens; 3-5 CPU-hours on this box; 0 GPU; 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-2 (log-linear, not exponential): for Qwen3-0.6B and 1.7B on GSM8K/MATH-500-easy, coverage pass@k fits an exponentiated power law (log-linear in k) with no super-linear region; majority-vote accuracy saturates by k≈8-16 (Agent Forest/Wunderlich plateau), so accuracy-per-CPU-second is concave in ensemble size. // FALSIFIER A convex (accelerating) segment in accuracy vs log(k), or maj@k still rising at k=32 by more than 1 pt per doubling. // EXP Sample k=32 answers (T=0.7) per item for 200 items per model; compute pass@k and maj@k for k in {1,2,4,8,16,32}; fit c(k)=exp(a·k^-b) and report residuals; plot accuracy vs measured wall-clock. // COST ≈2M tokens per model (200×32×300); ≈20-30 CPU-hours per model at 0.6B, 2-3× at 1.7B — run in background over days; 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-3 (tiny sampler beats bigger greedy only with a verifier): Qwen3-0.6B maj@16 does NOT beat Qwen3-4B greedy at matched CPU-seconds on GSM8K, but 0.6B best-of-16 selected by a free program-execution verifier (extract Python, run it) DOES. // FALSIFIER maj@16 of 0.6B ≥ 4B greedy at equal wall-clock (refutes the verifier necessity), or program-verified BoN ≤ maj@16 (refutes the verifier lever). // EXP Time 4B Q4 greedy on 200 items; give 0.6B the same wall-clock budget of samples; compare maj@k vs program-verified BoN vs 4B greedy; log the per-item backfire set (items where voting turns a correct k=1 into wrong). // COST ≈1.5M tokens total; 15-25 CPU-hours; 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-4 (tiny PRM helps less than majority on the hard tail): Skywork-o1-Open-PRM-1.5B used as a BoN selector over Qwen3-1.7B samples adds ≥+1 pt over maj@16 on MATH-500 levels 1-3 but is ≤ maj@16 on levels 4-5 (mirrors its AIME24 10.0 vs 13.3 result). // FALSIFIER PRM-1.5B BoN beats majority on the level 4-5 subset by ≥2 pts. // EXP Run PRM-1.5B in torch-cpu (fp32/bf16, ~6 GB RAM — check against the ~6 GB available) over stored samples from H-ENS-2; compare last-step, min, product aggregation; stratify by difficulty level. // COST Scoring only: ~3200 responses × ~400 tokens through a 1.5B encoder ≈ 1-3 CPU-hours; RAM is the risk, not time; 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-5 (small aggregator kills MoA): a 2-layer MoA with three tiny proposers and a 1.7B aggregator scores BELOW plurality vote of the same three proposers on GSM8K (Wunderlich 2026: all-8B MoA 51.2% < SC). // FALSIFIER MoA-tiny ≥ plurality vote + 2 pts. // EXP Reuse H-ENS-1 proposer outputs; feed the three answers to Qwen3-1.7B with the MoA aggregation prompt; measure accuracy and whether the aggregator merely copies the plurality answer (agreement rate with vote). // COST ≈200×600 tokens of aggregation ≈ 1-2 CPU-hours; 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-6 (debate is a martingale at tiny scale): two rounds of debate between two Qwen3-1.7B instances change GSM8K accuracy by |Δ| < 1 pt versus round-0 majority, with flip rate correct→wrong ≈ wrong→correct. // FALSIFIER Debate improves accuracy by ≥3 pts over round-0 voting, or the correct→wrong flip rate is less than half the wrong→correct rate. // EXP 200 items, 2 agents, 2 rounds, log every belief flip; compare with 4-sample majority at the same token budget. // COST ≈200×4×400 tokens ≈ 3-5 CPU-hours; 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-7 (speculative decoding on this CPU is ≤1.3x): with llama.cpp on 4 N1 cores, Qwen3-0.6B drafting for Qwen3-4B Q4 yields ≤1.3x tokens/s on prose and ≤1.6x on code, because CPU decode here is closer to compute-bound than the bandwidth-bound GPU case that gives 1.4-2.9x. // FALSIFIER Measured ≥1.5x on prose or ≥2.0x on code at any --draft-max in {2,4,8}. // EXP llama-speculative vs llama-cli on 5 prompts × 256 tokens each, prose and code; record tok/s, acceptance rate, draft length; also test prompt-lookup (draft-free n-gram) as the zero-model baseline. // COST <1 CPU-hour; 0 $; the first genuinely missing number in the public literature
- CLAIM H-ENS-8 (confidence-gated cascade dominates): a 0.6B→4B cascade gated on answer-token margin (escalate when top-2 logit margin < τ) reaches ≥95% of 4B accuracy on GSM8K while spending ≤50% of 4B's CPU-seconds (RouteLLM/FrugalGPT pattern); majority-vote agreement as the gate does NOT work (2608.11403 says agreement/entropy do not predict harm). // FALSIFIER No τ gives ≥95% of 4B accuracy under 60% of its cost, or agreement-gating matches margin-gating. // EXP Store 0.6B logits at the answer token (llama.cpp --logits or torch); sweep τ; plot accuracy vs CPU-seconds Pareto; compare to 4B alone and to 0.6B maj@k. // COST Reuses H-ENS-3 outputs plus 4B on escalated items; 2-6 CPU-hours; 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-9 (token-level fusion of same-tokenizer tinies): GaC-style logit averaging of Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3-1.7B, applied only where the 1.7B's top-token probability < 0.5 (6-10% of tokens), matches or beats 1.7B alone by +1 pt at ≤1.2x its latency; mixing across a large quality gap (0.6B into 4B) degrades the 4B. // FALSIFIER Fused 0.6B+1.7B ≤ 1.7B − 0.5 pt, or 0.6B+4B fusion ≥ 4B + 0.5 pt. // EXP Implement in torch-cpu with HF checkpoints (shared Qwen3 tokenizer so no vocabulary union needed); 100 GSM8K items; compare greedy 1.7B, gated fusion, and always-fusion. // COST torch-cpu fp32 is slow (est. 2-5 tok/s for 1.7B): 100 items ≈ 4-8 CPU-hours; needs transformers installed (free); 0 $
- CLAIM H-ENS-10 (super-additivity is only reachable by making errors uncorrelated): tiny models trained/prompted to reason via DIFFERENT processes (e.g. program-of-thought vs natural-language CoT vs equation-only) have lower φ̄ than same-process samples of one model, and their 3-way vote gains more per member than 3 samples of the best member (the 'genuinely different reasoning' recommendation of 2605.29800). // FALSIFIER φ̄ across processes ≥ φ̄ within one process, or cross-process vote ≤ self-consistency@3 of the best process. // EXP Same Qwen3-1.7B, three prompt regimes, 200 items; compute φ̄ within vs across; vote accuracies; this isolates process diversity from model diversity at zero extra model cost. // COST ≈200×3×400 tokens ≈ 3-5 CPU-hours; 0 $

OPEN:
- Exact numbers behind the HF 'Llama-3.2-1B beats 8B, 3B beats 70B on MATH-500' result (N, method, PRM=Llama-3.1-8B) are unverified here because the Space would not render; someone should read the HF blog HTML directly or the search-and-learn results tables.
- Does the Liu 2025 '0.5B > GPT-4o' result survive without a 7B PRM (which alone is 12x the policy)? No paper found that uses a sub-2B PRM to lift a sub-1B policy past a 30B+ model; the town's CPU budget makes this the decisive question.
- Are tiny models of the SAME family (Qwen3 0.6B/1.7B/4B) more error-correlated than cross-family tinies? ICML 2025 found larger models more correlated; the tiny end of the curve is unmeasured.
- What does 'exponentially smarter than the sum' mean operationally for the owner — coverage (pass@any), voted accuracy, or knowledge extracted per token? Condorcet convergence is exponential in k only under independence; the owner's claim should be restated as a measurable target before any MVP.
- CPU speculative-decoding gains on ARM arm64-N1 are absent from the literature; H-ENS-7 fills that gap cheaply and decides whether tiny drafts matter for this town's throughput at all.
- Owner's 'qwen3.8 50b': no such checkpoint exists (Qwen3.8 = 27B dense, 2.4T-A95B MoE); needs owner confirmation of the intended id before it enters any node as a candidate. Note the 27B dense at Q4 (~16 GB) does not fit the ~6 GB currently available on this box nor the 8 GB [region] box.
- Weaver's weak-verifier ensemble was distilled to a 400M cross-encoder — is that checkpoint public and does it generalize to tiny generators? Not checked.
- 2026 papers (TMAS 2605.10344, Share-More-Search-Less 2605.27030, ReM-MoA 2606.24437, Minority Sentinel 2606.29270) report Pareto gains but their abstracts hide model sizes; none was confirmed to test ≤4B models against 30-70B.

CRITIC (confidence 0.75):
unsupported:
- KEY_FACT on 2605.27559 is not in the paper: it claims 'verifiers detect 40-60% of errors from Llama-3.1 8B/70B on GPQA/MATH/GSM8K but correction rates run 10-20 pts below detection'. MEASURED from the saved PDF: the models are gpt-4.1-nano, gpt-4.1, gpt-oss-120b, Gemini-2.5-flash and Llama-3.2-1B (no 8B/70B anywhere); the paper is about a generator's own detection in debate/self-correction, not external verifiers; the headline numbers are conditional miscorrection 53-94% (Table 3) and detection rate varying by >10x (Table 4). The summary sentence 'Verifiers detect errors that small generators cannot correct (2605.27559)' is therefore also unsupported by that source.
- SUMMARY claim 'small aggregators wreck MoA' contradicts Wunderlich Table 2 (MEASURED): 70B proposers + 8B aggregator = 69.6% (still above SC@10 68.7% and CoT 64.3%; only 1.8 pts under best MoA 71.4%), whereas 8B proposers + 70B aggregator = 52.9%. It is small PROPOSERS that wreck MoA, not the aggregator. H-ENS-5's rationale ('small aggregator kills MoA') is built on this misreading.
- 'strictly dependent on a matching 7B PRM' (Liu 2025): the paper evaluates PRMs from 1.5B to 72B and the html page does not state which PRM or which budget N in {4,16,64,256} produced the 78.2/76.4 headline; 'strictly' and '7B' are the reader's inference. The paper does show PRM quality matters (Y=7.66 log(X)+44.31) and that Math-Shepherd/RLHFlow fail OOD — that part is MEASURED.
- 'Wunderlich et al. ACL-SRW 2026' — the arXiv html shows v1, May 2 2026, University of Göttingen; no venue string was visible. Venue unverified.
- 'Debate+Llama2 on code = 0 accuracy (cross-agent noise)' — Table 3 shows 0 in BOTH standalone and enhanced debate for Llama2-13B and 70B on HumanEval; that pattern is more consistent with a pipeline/format failure than with 'cross-agent noise'. The parenthetical is interpretation presented as a fact.
- 'unconfirmed 35B-A3B rumour' — the Qwen3.8 README lists Qwen3.5-35B-A3B (2026-02-24) and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (2026-04-16) as released models. Not a rumour. It is the most plausible referent of the owner's 'qwen3.8 50b' after 27B, and a 3B-active MoE is the one 'big' model line that could ever run on this CPU (Q4 ≈ 20 GB; would need most of the 23 GB box, not the 6 GB currently free).
- H-ENS-7 premise 'CPU decode here is closer to compute-bound than the bandwidth-bound GPU case' is an unmeasured ESTIMATE stated as a mechanism. Single-stream Q4 decode on a 4-core N1 is plausibly still memory-bandwidth-bound (matrix-vector); the experiment is good precisely because it discriminates the regime, but the hypothesis should not presume the answer.
- Applying Nine Judges' neff ≈ 2-2.5 to tiny-model GSM8K voting is an extrapolation: that paper measured frontier models (Claude Sonnet 4.5, GPT-4o, Gemini 2.5 Pro, Llama 4, Qwen3-32B, Mistral Large 3, DeepSeek-V3) on ChaosNLI classification and RewardBench preference judging — not reasoning, not sub-2B models.
- H-ENS-6's claim (|Δ| < 1 pt, flips symmetric) is inconsistent with the reader's own cited 2605.27559: MEASURED Table 5, Llama-3.2-1B on GSM8K debate R0 42.00 → Rlast 33.00 (Δ = −9.00 pp); DM exceeds DC by 14.8x; miscorrection 93.66% (E1) / 88.68% (E2); self-correction analog Δ = −10 pp. For tiny models the literature predicts debate HURTS, not zero-mean drift. The martingale is over beliefs; realised accuracy falls when miscorrection dominates.
- All ESTIMATE lines about tokens per answer (~300) ignore that Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B/4B are hybrid thinking models; in thinking mode GSM8K answers run 1-5k tokens, which multiplies every CPU-hour estimate 3-15x. No seed states whether /no_think is used.
errors:
- 2605.27559 misattributed (Llama-3.1 8B/70B, '40-60% detection, correction 10-20 pts below') — the paper's actual tiny-model result (Llama-3.2-1B, GSM8K debate −9 pp, 94% miscorrection) was missed even though it is the most on-point measurement in the whole sweep for Vision 3.
- Wunderlich Table 2 inverted: small proposers (52.9%) not small aggregators (69.6%) wreck MoA; H-ENS-5 is mis-motivated as a result.
- Qwen 35B-A3B called an unconfirmed rumour; Qwen3.5-35B-A3B and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B are listed releases in the same README the reader cites.
- H-ENS-6 predicts |Δ|<1 for tiny-model debate while the cited 2605.27559 measured Δ=−9 pp for a 1B model on GSM8K; the seed's claim and falsifier need re-signing (predict a NEGATIVE Δ driven by miscorrection, falsify if Δ ≥ 0).
missing:
- Statistical power: 200 items gives SE ≈ 3.5 pts at p≈0.5; most falsifiers are 1-2 pts and H-ENS-9 uses 100 items (SE ≈ 5). Every seed needs paired per-item tests (McNemar / bootstrap over items) and a power statement, or the falsifiers are unfalsifiable at this N.
- Difficulty confound in φ̄/neff (H-ENS-1, H-ENS-10): error-indicator correlation is inflated by item-difficulty heterogeneity even for statistically independent models. Compute φ within difficulty strata (or fit a Rasch-style item model) or the 'correlation ceiling' will be found trivially and mean nothing.
- Cost of replicating the SUPPORTED evidence: Liu 2025 uses budgets up to N=256 with a PRM on MATH-500 (≈500 items × 256 × ~500 tokens ≈ 64M generated tokens); at the reader's own 20-40 tok/s that is 450-900 CPU-hours. The 'small beats big' half of the verdict is not reproducible on this box; only the N≤16 regime is, where the same paper's own scaling curves show far smaller lifts. The reading should state this gap explicitly.
- The Camber GPU budget (3 GPU-h/month) appears in no seed. PRM-7B scoring of a few thousand stored samples (H-ENS-4) fits comfortably in 1 GPU-hour and would replicate the Skywork 1.5B-vs-7B gap on the town's own tiny generators.
- Adaptive / early-stopping self-consistency (Aggarwal et al. 2023 'Let's Sample Step by Step'; Li et al. 2024 ESC) cuts sample budgets 3-7x at equal accuracy — the single most CPU-relevant ensemble technique and absent from the sweep.
- Thinking mode: Qwen3 tinies have a /think toggle and a thinking budget; a 1-sample thinking answer vs k no-think samples at matched tokens is the first comparison a CPU town should make, and it may make self-consistency moot for 1.7B/4B.
- Speculative decoding on CPU: besides draft models, llama.cpp's prompt-lookup (n-gram) and self-speculative/early-exit variants cost zero extra RAM — H-ENS-7 mentions lookup only as a baseline; on a 6-GB-free box it may be the only affordable variant.
- Memory, not time, is the binding constraint: 6 GB available means 4B Q4 (~2.5 GB) + 1.7B (~1.1 GB) + 0.6B (~0.5 GB) + KV cache barely fit simultaneously; H-ENS-9 (torch-cpu fp32 1.7B ≈ 7 GB) does not fit at all as written. No seed budgets RAM.
- Owner's Vision 3 restated operationally (the reader raises it as an open question but does not answer it): the only measurable 'exponential' available is coverage pass@k, and Large Language Monkeys already shows it is log-linear; the reading should recommend the owner adopt 'knowledge per token' = marginal accuracy per CPU-second as the metric, and drop 'exponential' as a claim to test.
- The tiny-model debate reversal (Llama-3.2-1B −9 pp) directly supports the town moral 'failures included': the backfire/miscorrection set is itself extractable knowledge (which items a tiny model wrongly 'detects'), and H-ENS-3/6 should log DM vs DC counts per the 2605.27559 four-regime scheme rather than only accuracy.
strongest seed: H-ENS-7 (speculative decoding on this ARM CPU): <1 CPU-hour, 0 RAM beyond two GGUFs, zero credible public data on CPU-only ARM (confirmed gap), and it calibrates the bandwidth-vs-compute regime that every other seed's token/time estimates silently assume. Highest knowledge per token by a wide margin. Close second: H-ENS-1 merged with H-ENS-10 as one 200-item corpus (4 models × greedy plus 3 prompt regimes on one model), because the per-item correctness vectors it produces are the raw input for H-ENS-5, H-ENS-8 and the difficulty-stratified φ analysis — provided thinking mode is fixed off and φ is computed within difficulty strata.
weakest seed: H-ENS-2 (log-linear vs exponential coverage): 20-30 CPU-hours per model to re-fit a scaling law Large Language Monkeys already established over four orders of magnitude, with a foreordained answer and a falsifier ('convex segment in accuracy vs log k') that 200 items cannot detect at the noise level. Runner-up for weakest: H-ENS-9 (GaC-style fusion) — 4-8 CPU-hours in torch-cpu fp32, needs transformers installed, does not fit the 6 GB available, and hunts a +1 pt effect with n=100 (SE ≈ 5 pts). H-ENS-5 is also mis-motivated (see Wunderlich inversion) though cheap.

### oscillators-bitneurons
SOURCE: Literature sweep (WebSearch/WebFetch, 2026-09-13) + owner sources: github.com/project-89/coherence-guided-dead-head-identification; github.com/CodexOperator/machinelearning (spikyNN, snn_standard_model, snn_applied_finance); huggingface.co/api/models?author=Qwen&search=Qwen3.8; arxiv 2410.16144 (bitnet.cpp), 2407.00088 (T-MAC), 2501.00032, 1603.05279 (XNOR-Net), 2011.09398 (Larq), 2606.11585v2 (Kuramoto Attention), 2606.12059 (Attention by Synchronization), 2410.13821 (AKOrN), 2410.11112 (DWN), 2210.08277 + 2508.06097 (difflogic, recurrent), 2510.15653 (Tsetlin CPU), 2302.13939v5 (SpikeGPT), NeurIPS 2023 Spike-driven Transformer, 2008.02217 (modern Hopfield), Scholarpedia binding-by-synchrony, Frontiers Brian2Loihi, KIVI/StreamingLLM/SnapKV, alphaxiv 2609.recurrent-looped-transformer; plus three microbenchmarks run on this box (scripts in scratchpad).

Prior art is dense on every piece of the owner's proposal; the novelty is only in the specific composition. (1) Coherence law: tau_death = 0.96/sqrt(d_model) on a head's mean cosine alignment between its write-back and the receiver's pre-head residual; derived from the BKT critical coupling of coupled oscillators (cos=0.679, /sqrt2 -> chi_c=0.96, rescaled by 1/sqrt(d)). Precision 95.2-100% over six models (GPT-2 to OpenLLaMA-7B); NO recall, NO post-pruning perplexity, and the repo says naive deletion does not yield a working model. So the equation already IS an oscillator-order-parameter proxy - the owner's "generate coupled-oscillator maps from it" is a direct extension, not a leap. (2) Oscillators-as-attention exists and works at tiny scale: Kuramoto Attention (2606.11585) proves the value update is exactly Kuramoto coupling a_tj = sum_u A_tu sin(theta_uj - theta_tj), matches a parameter-matched transformer within 0.01 BPC at 1M-5M params on enwik8/CodeParrot, at ~50% throughput and 2.4x memory; AKOrN (2410.13821) gives the sphere-Kuramoto update and shows test-time extra steps = reasoning (Sudoku 100% ID, 89.5% OOD). Attention-by-Synchronization (2606.12059): causal LM still favours softmax, gap decays as power law in oscillator dim. (3) Neuron-as-byte: closest prior art is weightless/LUT neurons - DWN (ICML 2024, n=6 LUTs, MNIST 98.31% at 23.4 KiB, runs on a 2 KB-SRAM ATmega328P, Learnable Mapping solves the hand-wiring problem), difflogic (>1M MNIST img/s on one CPU core), Tsetlin machines (bitwise clause learning, no floats), rank-order coding/polychronization (bit ORDER as code), and AER pub/sub. Recurrent logic nets reach only 4.39 BLEU on WMT14 (GRU 5.41) - the gradient/scale gap for bit-neurons on language is the biggest risk. (4) 1.58-bit CPU LLMs are mature: bitnet.cpp 700M 194 tok/s M2 Ultra, 7B 52 tok/s, 100B 6.6 tok/s, 55-82% energy cut, 100% lossless vs llama.cpp; T-MAC BitNet-3B 30 tok/s on 1 M2 core, 11 tok/s on RPi5. (5) Spiking transformers: Spike-driven Transformer 77.1% ImageNet, ~87x MAC energy cut; SpikeGPT-216M 39.75 ppl WikiText-103 (Spike-F3 23.41). (6) Measured here (arm64-N1, 4 cores, NEON+asimddp, no I8MM/SVE): a 40-line NEON XNOR-popcount GEMM hits 242 binary-GOPS on ONE core vs 54 f32 GOPS for 4-core OpenBLAS; naive numpy popcount is slower than f32; mean-field Kuramoto step for 4096 oscillators = 154 us (all-to-all = 250 ms). "qwen3.8 50b" does not exist: HF lists Qwen3.8-27B, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (125B total, 6B active, includes a 51B n-gram embedding - likely the "50b"), Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B; only Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B fit the 6 GB free on this box. The recurrent looped transformer paper (alphaxiv, 2026-09-13) reports no experiments at all.

KEY FACTS:
- MEASURED (github.com/project-89/coherence-guided-dead-head-identification README): tau_death(d) = 0.96/sqrt(d_model); coherence = head's mean cosine alignment between incoming write-back signal and receiver pre-head residual; dead-head precision GPT-2 95.2%, GPT-2M 98.2%, Qwen2.5-0.5B 95.5%, SmolLM2-360M 99.6%, OpenLLaMA-7B 100%, Gemma3-4B 100%; ground truth = individual head ablation; NO recall, NO pruned-model perplexity reported; README: 'does NOT claim naively deleting all dead heads produces a working model'; pruning needs SVD spectral filtering + rotation compensation (follow-on). Entry: scripts/coherence_anatomy_scan.py --model gpt2; deps torch transformers numpy datasets matplotlib.
- MEASURED (same README): the 0.96 constant is derived from the BKT transition of coupled oscillators on S^1 (critical alignment cos=0.679, normalised by 1/sqrt2 fluctuation scale -> chi_c=0.96, rescaled by concentration of measure 1/sqrt(d)). The pruning law is already an oscillator order-parameter proxy.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2606.11585v2, Kuramoto Attention): score s_tu=(tau/sqrt k) sum_j g^q_tj g^k_uj cos(theta_tj-theta_uj+omega_j(t-u)); value update a_tj = -sin(theta_tj)Re G + cos(theta_tj)Im G with G_tj=sum_u A_tu e^{i theta_uj}, proven equal to Kuramoto coupling sum_u A_tu sin(theta_uj-theta_tj) (Lemma 2); one sync step per layer + SwiGLU phase step. 5M params CodeParrot: beats transformer by 0.012 val / 0.010 test bits-per-byte; enwik8 within 0.01 BPC; 1M params lags ~0.02 BPC. Throughput 167k vs 335k tok/s (5M), 460k vs 973k (1M); peak memory 4.62 vs 1.94 GB. k=400 phases (5M), 4 layers, seq 256, 50 epochs AdamW 1e-3. Local order parameter R_t ~1 at every layer; global coherence falls with depth.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2606.12059 Attention by Synchronization): fixed-query oscillator attention via Kuramoto-Lohe dynamics, no softmax normalisation, unique globally attractive fixed point; matches softmax on keyword spotting and subject-verb agreement; on causal LM softmax still wins, gap decays as a power law in oscillator dimension; simulations at oscillator dim = 2; authors' aim is physical substrates, not software replacement.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2410.13821 AKOrN): update x_i' = Omega_i x_i + Proj_{x_i}(c_i + sum_j J_ij x_j), ||x_i||=1, discrete with learned step gamma + renormalise; T=16 steps train, up to 128+ at test; CLEVRTex FG-ARI 88.5, Sudoku 100% ID / 89.5% OOD with energy voting; CIFAR-10 clean 91.23%, AutoAttack eps=8/255 51.56%; no FLOP/param counts given.
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2410.16144 bitnet.cpp tables): Apple M2 Ultra tok/s llama.cpp->bitnet.cpp: 700M 114.47->194.38 (1.70x), 7B 15.61->52.36 (3.35x), 13B 8.79->33.78, 70B 1.71->8.67 (5.07x), 100B 6.58; Intel i7-13700H: 700M 30.73->119.08, 7B 3.30->18.75, 13B 1.78->10.99 (6.17x), 70B 2.44. Energy J/token M2: 700M 0.314->0.140, 7B 3.013->1.068, 70B 28.02->8.42 (55-70% cut); i7 71.9-82.2% cut. 100% exact-match lossless on 1000 WildChat prompts vs llama.cpp 1.4%. I2_S: 2-bit ternary packing; TL1: 2 weights->4-bit index, 9-entry int16 LUT of activations; TL2: 3 weights->5-bit index, model 1/6 smaller than TL1.
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/papers/2407.00088 T-MAC): BitNet-b1.58-3B 30 tok/s single core, 71 tok/s 8 cores on M2 Ultra, 11 tok/s on Raspberry Pi 5; up to 4x throughput and 70% energy cut vs llama.cpp; supports W1.58A8, W2A16, W4A16 via bit-wise table lookup. MEASURED (arxiv 2501.00032, Arm kernels): 3-3.2x prompt processing, 2x decode vs llama.cpp on Arm CPUs with groupwise codebook quant.
- MEASURED (arxiv 1603.05279 XNOR-Net): 32x memory saving, 58x faster convolution, BWN within 2.9% top-1 of full-precision AlexNet. MEASURED (arxiv 2011.09398 Larq CE / daBNN search): binarized conv speedup 8.5-18.5x (LCE), 8-10x (daBNN); Bi-RealNet Pixel 1 41.6 ms (LCE) vs 61.3 ms (daBNN).
- MEASURED (arxiv.org/html/2410.11112 DWN, ICML 2024): LUT neurons n=6 inputs, 1-bit out; MNIST 98.31% @ 23.4 KiB (sm 97.80% @ 11.7 KiB) vs difflogic 97.62% @ 23.4 KiB, FINN 98.40% @ 355 KiB; CIFAR-10 57.42% @ 62.5 KiB; tabular avg rank 2.5 vs XGBoost 3.4; on ATmega328P (2 KB SRAM, $1.52) MNIST 97.9% at 16.5 inf/s vs XGBoost 90.2%; FPGA Z-7045 MNIST 125 ns, 19 nJ/sample, 4.6K LUTs; vs FINN 20.7x latency, 121.6x energy. Training: Extended Finite Difference (weights all 2^n LUT entries by Hamming distance, O(2^n) not O(2^2n)); Learnable Mapping learns which inputs feed each LUT, argmax then discarded (zero inference cost).
- MEASURED (arxiv 2210.08277 difflogic): discretised logic-gate nets exceed 1M MNIST images/s on a single CPU core; convolutional variant (2411.04732) 86.3% CIFAR-10 with 29x fewer gate ops. MEASURED (arxiv 2508.06097 Recurrent DDLGN): WMT14 En-De 4.39 BLEU at inference (5.00 train) vs GRU 5.41 - recurrent bit-logic nets are far below transformers on language.
- MEASURED (arxiv 2510.15653 Tsetlin CPU): bitwise instruction-level TM inference cuts time up to 96.71% vs integer TM on ARM (gem5); TM review cites 30.5x compute, 36.6x memory, 13.5x latency/energy cuts vs NNs on chosen tasks. Learning is by automata games, no gradient, no floats.
- MEASURED (search excerpts, arxiv 2302.13939v5 / NeurIPS 2023 / 2508.07710): SpikeGPT-216M 39.75 ppl WikiText-103 (behind GPT-2); Spike-F3 conversion 23.41 ppl; Spike-driven Transformer 77.1% ImageNet top-1 at 288px, 66.34M params, ~87x MAC-energy cut, 1.98-3.52x lower energy than Spikformer; Spikformer matches ResNet-50 in 4 timesteps.
- MEASURED (Frontiers Brian2Loihi, sPyNNaker PMC6257411): Loihi 2 up to >100x faster than Brian2 CPU at sparse activity; Brian2CUDA up to 1000x over Brian CPU; SpiNNaker sized for 1e13 synaptic events/s at 2e8 MIPS; software AER/event-driven pipelines exist (Brian2, Lava, sPyNNaker).
- MEASURED (search excerpts): KIVI 2-bit KV cache, tuning-free, 2.4x lower peak memory on Llama-2/Falcon at same quality; SnapKV window-voting token selection; StreamingLLM attention sinks for unbounded length; MiniKV 2-bit layer-discriminative; PyramidKV. All plug-and-play, CPU-relevant only for long contexts.
- MEASURED (alphaxiv.org/abs/2609.recurrent-looped-transformer, Yifan Zhang, submitted 2026-09-13): formal proposal only - state transitions, cache semantics, gradient paths, RL replay; NO benchmarks, throughput, memory or baseline comparisons reported.
- MEASURED (huggingface.co/api/models?author=Qwen&search=Qwen3.8, 2026-09-13): the only Qwen3.8 ids are Qwen3.8-27B (+FP8), Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (+FP8; 125B total incl. 51B n-gram embedding + 4B MTP, 6B active, 48 layers, hidden 2560, 512 experts/11 active, Gated DeltaNet + sparse attention, 262k ctx, qwen-community-1.0 licence, Aug 2026), Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B (+FP8). There is NO 50B Qwen3.8. Qwen3.5 sizes 0.8B/2B/4B/35B-A3B exist.
- MEASURED (this box, lscpu/free 2026-09-13): 4x arm64-N1, flags asimd asimddp fphp asimdhp (NEON + int8 dot product; no I8MM, no SVE); 23 GB RAM, ~6 GB available; torch 2.10.0+cpu, snntorch 0.9.4, numpy 2.4.3, numba 0.64.0, gcc present; transformers NOT installed; HF cache holds only faster-whisper; 34 GB disk free.
- MEASURED (scratchpad/pop.c, this box): NEON XNOR-popcount GEMM M=256,N=1024,K=4096 on ONE core: 8.9 ms = 242 binary-GOPS (uint16 accumulators); numpy f32 OpenBLAS 4 cores: 39.4 ms = 54.4 GOPS; torch f32 98.5 ms; torch _int_mm int8 388 ms = 5.5 GOPS (no NEON int8 path used); naive numpy bitwise_count broadcast 93 ms = 23 binary-GOPS. Binary weights 512 KiB vs 16 MiB f32 (32x). Binary-vs-f32 output correlation 0.637 for random data.
- MEASURED (scratchpad bench, this box, numpy 4 threads): mean-field Kuramoto (order-parameter trick, O(N)) N=256 62 us/step, N=1024 95 us, N=4096 154 us; all-to-all O(N^2) N=1024 8.5 ms, N=4096 250 ms/step; K=1.5, sigma_omega=0.1 reaches r=0.998 in 400 steps. Lesson: any coupling map must be sparse or low-rank to be affordable on CPU above ~1k oscillators.
- MEASURED (scratchpad bench, this box): hand-set LIF thresholds did not transfer across N: N=1024 2% binary synapses -> network died (0 spikes), N=8192 -> saturated (100% spiking); event-driven gather 32 ms/tick vs dense 700 ms/tick at N=8192. Threshold/balance must be derived from fan-in, not fixed.
- MEASURED (gh api CodexOperator/machinelearning): spikyNN = snnTorch two-Leaky-neuron demo with sliding-window activation; snn_standard_model = planned Leaky/Synaptic/Alpha sweeps on MNIST (TODO mostly unchecked); snn_applied_finance = spiking-transformer phase-1 on SOL 1h (LGBM 0.643 acc baseline). No bit-neuron or oscillator code exists there yet.
- ESTIMATE: Qwen3.8-27B at Q4_K_M needs ~16 GB RAM (does not fit 6 GB available here); Qwen3.5-4B Q4 ~2.5 GB and Qwen3.5-0.8B ~0.6 GB fit; on this arm64-N1 expect roughly 5-10 tok/s for 4B Q4 and 25-40 tok/s for 0.8B via llama.cpp (extrapolated from RPi5/M2 numbers above, not measured here).

REUSABLE:
- /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/scratchpad/pop.c - 40-line NEON XNOR-popcount binary GEMM (vcntq_u8 + vpadalq_u8), compiled with gcc -O3 -march=armv8.2-a+simd; 242 binary-GOPS/core measured. Drop-in kernel for any bit-neuron or BNN layer.
- /tmp/claude-1001/-home-ubuntu-work-agi/c6a6718c-28e1-4575-b753-b22076d47497/scratchpad/bench_bits.py - numpy/torch f32 vs packbits+bitwise_count vs _int_mm baseline harness.
- Equation: coherence dead-head threshold tau_death(d)=0.96/sqrt(d_model), head coherence = mean cosine(write-back, pre-head residual); precision-only law (project-89 repo).
- Equation: Kuramoto Attention value step a_tj = sum_u A_tu sin(theta_uj - theta_tj) with A = causal softmax over gated cosine score (arxiv 2606.11585v2 Lemma 2); local order parameter R_tj = |sum_u A_tu e^{i theta_uj}| as a per-token coherence diagnostic.
- Equation: AKOrN sphere-Kuramoto x_i' = Omega_i x_i + Proj_{x_i}(c_i + sum_j J_ij x_j), renormalise each step (arxiv 2410.13821).
- Trick: mean-field Kuramoto O(N) via order parameter z=(1/N)sum e^{i theta}, dtheta_i = omega_i + K r sin(psi - theta_i) - measured 154 us/step at N=4096 here.
- Method: DWN Extended Finite Difference + Learnable Mapping (arxiv 2410.11112) - the published way to train LUT/bit neurons and learn their input wiring by gradient, then discard the wiring weights.
- Code: github.com/project-89/coherence-guided-dead-head-identification scripts/coherence_anatomy_scan.py --model gpt2 (needs pip transformers+datasets, not installed here); github.com/microsoft/BitNet (bitnet.cpp, ARM TL1 kernels); github.com/microsoft/T-MAC; github.com/Felix-Petersen/difflogic; github.com/larq/compute-engine.
- Owner code: github.com/CodexOperator/machinelearning/spikyNN/snn_neuron.py (snnTorch Leaky pair, sliding-window activation), snn_standard_model/experiment_infrastructure.py (ExperimentConfig/Result JSON harness) - reusable scaffolding for the town's SNN experiments.

FAILURES/LESSONS:
- WebFetch on arxiv /abs/ pages returns abstracts only; /html/<id> gave the tables (worked for 2410.16144, 2410.11112, 2410.13821, 2606.11585v2). Use /html/ first.
- Naive numpy bit-packed popcount matmul (broadcast XOR + bitwise_count) is SLOWER than f32 OpenBLAS on this box (23 vs 54 GOPS); the 32x memory win is real but the compute win only appears with a compiled NEON kernel (242 GOPS/core). Do not benchmark bit-neurons in pure numpy.
- torch._int_mm on this CPU build runs at 5.5 GOPS - 10x slower than f32; int8 paths in stock torch CPU are not a shortcut here.
- Hand-set LIF thresholds (threshold 4, 2% connectivity) died at N=1024 and saturated at N=8192: every bit-neuron/SNN experiment must set thresholds from expected fan-in and report firing rate, or results are meaningless.
- All-to-all Kuramoto is O(N^2): 250 ms/step at N=4096. Coupling maps derived from attention heads must be sparse (top-k) or low-rank to simulate more than a few thousand oscillators.
- The 'coherence' repo validates PRECISION only (dead heads are dead) and reports no recall and no pruned-model quality; treating it as a pruning recipe would be a misread of its own README.
- The owner's 'qwen3.8 50b' does not resolve to any model id; '50b' most plausibly echoes the 51B n-gram embedding inside Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (125B/6B active), which cannot fit this box either. Needs owner confirmation before any download.
- The recurrent looped transformer link (alphaxiv 2609...) is a proposal with zero experiments; nothing to reproduce, only to implement.
- Bit-logic recurrent nets on language are ~an order of magnitude below transformers (4.39 BLEU WMT14 vs GRU 5.41 vs ~27 for a transformer): the 'no gradient path / scale' risk is measured, not hypothetical.
- transformers is not installed on this box and I did not install it (read-only); the coherence scan and any GPT-2/SmolLM2 experiment gates on the director approving a pip install (~1 GB with model weights).

SEEDS:
- CLAIM A NEON XNOR-popcount kernel makes a fully binary (weights and activations) layer at least 5x faster per core than f32 OpenBLAS on this arm64-N1, so a bit-neuron network is compute-viable here. // FALSIFIER A 4-thread build of pop.c falls below 270 GOPS aggregate, or a real BNN layer (with batchnorm/sign) loses the gain to packing overhead. // EXP Extend scratchpad/pop.c with OpenMP over rows, add sign+pack of activations inline, time a 3-layer 4096-wide binary MLP forward on MNIST-shaped input; compare to torch f32 and to bitnet.cpp TL1 ternary at the same width. // COST 1 hour, 0 tokens beyond a kid, no downloads
- CLAIM The coherence law's per-head alignment, thresholded at 0.96/sqrt(d), reproduces on CPU for GPT-2 small and SmolLM2-360M and recall (not just precision) against single-head ablation is above 80%. // FALSIFIER Recall below 50%, or ablating flagged heads raises WikiText-2 perplexity by more than 5% on either model. // EXP pip install transformers datasets (director approval); run scripts/coherence_anatomy_scan.py --model gpt2 and SmolLM2-360M on CPU; ablate every head individually (144 forward passes on 512 tokens), compute recall/precision and delta-ppl. // COST 2-3 CPU hours, ~1.5 GB download
- CLAIM A head-head coupling matrix J_ij built from pairwise coherence of head write-backs yields Kuramoto clusters that coincide with known GPT-2 circuits (induction heads, previous-token heads), i.e. the pruning equation generates a meaningful oscillator map. // FALSIFIER Kuramoto clusters at the BKT critical coupling are no better than random partitions at grouping documented induction/previous-token heads (ARI below 0.2). // EXP From the scan above, compute cos between each pair of heads' write-back vectors over 512 tokens, threshold at tau_death, integrate mean-field Kuramoto (154 us/step at N=4096; 144 heads is trivial) and cluster by phase; compare against the induction-head list from the GPT-2 literature. // COST 1 CPU hour after seed 2
- CLAIM Kuramoto Attention at 1M params reproduces on this CPU on enwik8 within 0.03 BPC of a matched transformer, establishing an oscillator LM baseline for the town. // FALSIFIER After 2 epochs (seq 256, batch 16) the Kuramoto model is more than 0.1 BPC behind the matched transformer, or it fails to train (R_t collapses). // EXP Implement the 2606.11585v2 layer (score with cos and gated Q/K, value step = Kuramoto coupling, bounded phase update, SwiGLU on phases) in torch CPU, 4 layers, k=176; train 1M params on the 100 MB enwik8 subset for a fixed wall-clock budget of 4 hours vs matched transformer. // COST 8 CPU hours total, 100 MB download
- CLAIM A neuron-as-byte (8-input LUT, 1-bit out per tick, 8-bit state) network trained with DWN's Extended Finite Difference and Learnable Mapping reaches at least 97% MNIST at under 32 KiB, matching DWN n=6, so the owner's byte-neuron is a trainable object rather than a hand-wired one. // FALSIFIER Best accuracy under 95% at any size under 64 KiB after 10 epochs, or Learnable Mapping fails to beat random wiring by more than 1 point. // EXP Re-implement DWN (EFD gradient, n=8 LUTs = 256 entries = 32 bytes/neuron, learnable mapping) in numpy/torch; train on MNIST with 1000 hidden LUTs; export to bit-packed uint8 tables and run inference with a popcount-free table walk; time inferences/s. // COST 4-6 CPU hours, MNIST 12 MB
- CLAIM Bit ORDER carries information cheaply: a byte-neuron whose firing depends on the arrival order of its 8 input bits (rank-order code, 8! states) separates a temporal task (sequential MNIST rows or a delayed-XOR task) that an order-blind popcount neuron cannot. // FALSIFIER The order-sensitive neuron gains less than 2 points over the order-blind neuron on delayed-XOR with delay 4, or costs more than 3x the ticks. // EXP Simulate 1024 byte-neurons with an 8-bit shift-register state and a 256-entry LUT; compare order-sensitive vs popcount-only firing rules trained by EFD on delayed-XOR and sequential MNIST; report accuracy and ms/tick using the event-driven gather (32 ms/tick at N=8192 baseline). // COST 3 CPU hours
- CLAIM Test-time extra synchronization steps (AKOrN style) raise accuracy on a reasoning micro-task (Sudoku 4x4 or parity) monotonically up to 8x the training steps, giving Vision 2 a measured 'smarter inference for more compute' curve on CPU. // FALSIFIER Accuracy plateaus or falls before 2x training steps, or the curve is flat within noise. // EXP Train a small AKOrN-style sphere-Kuramoto block (T=8 train) on 4x4 Sudoku or 16-bit parity; evaluate at T=8,16,32,64; plot accuracy vs steps and wall-clock. // COST 3 CPU hours
- CLAIM Ternary (1.58-bit) inference is the cheapest working LLM path on this box: bitnet.cpp TL1 on BitNet-b1.58-2B-4T reaches at least 8 tok/s on 4 arm64-N1 cores, beating llama.cpp Q4 for Qwen3.5-2B at similar quality. // FALSIFIER Under 4 tok/s, or a 5-point loss on a small MMLU/GSM8K slice vs Qwen3.5-2B Q4. // EXP Build bitnet.cpp (ARM TL1) and llama.cpp; download BitNet-b1.58-2B-4T GGUF (~1.2 GB) and Qwen3.5-2B Q4; measure tok/s, RSS and a 50-question eval slice. // COST 2 hours, ~3 GB download, director approval for builds
- CLAIM For any cooperating pair of tiny models, gains are sub-exponential: a draft/verify pair (speculative decoding) gives at most ~2-3x speed and an ensemble gives at most additive accuracy; the town's vision 3 must be restated as a measurable target. // FALSIFIER A two-model scheme on this CPU shows a super-additive accuracy gain (>1.5x the better single model's gain over chance) on any benchmark slice. // EXP Run Qwen3.5-0.8B as draft for Qwen3.5-4B in llama.cpp speculative mode; measure tok/s; separately majority-vote 0.8B+2B on 100 GSM8K items vs each alone. // COST 3 hours, needs models from seed 8

OPEN:
- Which model did the owner mean by 'qwen3.8 50b'? No such id exists; candidates are Qwen3.8-27B (does not fit 6 GB free), Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (125B total/6B active with a 51B n-gram embedding - the likely '50b'), or Qwen3.5/3.6-35B-A3B. Needs owner confirmation before any download or Camber spend.
- Does the coherence law have usable recall, and what does perplexity do when all dead heads are removed with the follow-on SVD/rotation compensation? Neither the paper nor the README reports it; the follow-on work is not yet located.
- Is the coherence measure computed per token or over a dataset mean, and on which corpus? The README does not say; the scan script must be read to know.
- Can Kuramoto Attention's 2.4x memory and 50% throughput overhead be reduced to make it a net win on CPU, or does it only pay off where softmax exponentials are expensive (physical substrates)?
- Can DWN-style Extended Finite Difference train a bit-neuron whose output depends on input ORDER (a state machine), or does the ordering make the LUT address non-stationary and break EFD? No prior art found that trains order-sensitive LUT neurons by gradient.
- Where is the owner's 'neuron = transistor byte' broadcast/subscribe intended to run: as a software AER event queue (Brian2/Lava-style) or as bit-packed synchronous ticks (popcount kernels)? The two have opposite performance profiles at the measured 2% connectivity.
- Does the town accept a pip install of transformers/datasets (about 1 GB with GPT-2 + SmolLM2-360M) on this box? Every coherence experiment gates on it.
- Is 3 Camber GPU-hours/month worth spending on anything above, or should all seeds stay CPU-only until a hypothesis set has been coalesced? Every seed listed is CPU-feasible.

CRITIC (confidence 0.82):
unsupported:
- 'The equation already IS an oscillator order-parameter proxy' - the BKT derivation (cos 0.679 -> /sqrt2 -> 0.96 -> /sqrt d) is a heuristic analogy in the README; no order parameter is ever measured on a transformer, and the paper itself (paper.tex L536-554, cloned at scratchpad/dead-head/) says 'individual ablation precision alone does not distinguish the derived threshold from random selection' and that precision is 'not the primary evidence'. Treat as hypothesis, not established.
- 'Precision 95.2-100% over six models' is presented as strength without the paper's own base-rate table: random selection from the same non-protected pool gives 93.2/99.4/95.5/97.8/99.9% (MEASURED data/base_rate_analysis.json: lift 1.02, 0.99, 1.00, 1.02, ~1.00). The law's discriminative power under individual ablation is ~zero; its only claimed value is 'zero ablation cost' and transfer.
- All five on-box microbenchmarks (242 binary-GOPS, 54 f32 GOPS, Kuramoto us/step, LIF ms/tick, _int_mm 5.5 GOPS) were taken on a 4-core box at load average 10-12 (MEASURED uptime 2026-09-13 22:19Z: 9.77/10.59/11.68) with swap 100% full; re-run now: pop.c 211 GOPS, f32 1-thread 31.1 GOPS, f32 4-thread 9.9 GOPS. The '1 core binary vs 4-core f32' ratio is a load artefact; the fair per-core ratio is ~5-7x, i.e. XNOR-Net's 2016 result reproduced, not new knowledge.
- Seed 1's dense XNOR GEMM says nothing about the owner's byte-neuron: an 8-input LUT neuron is a gather + 256-entry table walk (memory-latency bound), not a 4096-wide popcount dot product. The benchmark measures a BNN, not the proposed object.
- Seed 4 cost: '2 epochs of enwik8 within 4 CPU hours' - the paper trains 50 epochs at 460k tok/s on GPU; 2 epochs = ~200M tokens, which at a realistic 5-20k tok/s CPU training rate (ESTIMATE) is 3-11 hours of pure throughput before overhead; the falsifier and the budget contradict each other. Code availability for 2606.11585v2 was never checked.
- bitnet.cpp '100% lossless vs llama.cpp 1.4%' is exact-match of the kernel against an FP32 reference of the SAME ternary model (MEASURED arxiv html 2410.16144), not a quality comparison against a Q4 Qwen; and the tok/s tables are 'unlimited threads' on a 24-core M2 Ultra / 20-thread i7 with llama.cpp quantization unspecified - none of it transfers to 4 N1 cores without measurement.
- Seed 9 as written is not falsifiable: '>1.5x the better single model's gain over chance' is an arbitrary bar, and 100 GSM8K items with a 0.8B model has ~+-5 point noise (ESTIMATE), larger than any plausible ensemble effect.
- DWN ATmega328P numbers as quoted (97.9% @ 16.5 inf/s vs XGBoost 90.2%) could not be confirmed from the html; the fetch surfaced 94.5% @ 108 inf/s (unpacked) and '1.2% higher than XGBoost, 15% faster' - the paper has several MCU configurations and the reader's pair is unverified. Same for 'difflogic 97.62% @ 23.4 KiB' (DWN table row found: DiffLogicNet xs 96.87% @ 11.7 KiB).
errors:
- 'NO recall, NO post-pruning perplexity' is FALSE and the data was already on disk in the reader's own clone: scratchpad/dead-head/data/full_gpt2_head144_small_redundancy_v2_dimaware.json has clr_theory.safe_recall = 0.315, dangerous_keep_rate = 0.882, and eval loss 2.739 (baseline) -> 5.579 after removing the 42 dead heads alone (ppl ~15.5 -> ~265); threshold_transfer_summary.json gives combined_delta_loss 1.43-2.12 nats on every model. The paper (paper.tex) documents that simultaneous removal is catastrophic.
- Consequently hypothesis seed 2 is already falsified by existing data on both of its own criteria (recall 31.5% < 50%; ablating flagged heads raises loss +104% >> 5%) - running it would spend 2-3 CPU hours and a 1.5 GB download to re-derive a number sitting in the clone.
- Open question 'per token or dataset mean, which corpus?' is answered in the clone: coupling = E_{batch, position} cos(writeback, pre-head residual) (paper.tex L268-273); calibration = WikiText-2-raw train, seq_len 128, 32 batches default (coherence_anatomy_scan.py L129-140); the artefacts used only 8 calibration + 8 eval sequences of 128 tokens (1024 eval tokens) with a safety threshold of 1% of baseline loss - a very small ground truth the reader did not report.
- The dead set is NOT produced by the threshold alone: boundary protection (first/last layer) and a bridge veto remove 26 heads from the GPT-2 pool before thresholding (24 boundary + 2 bridge). Reader described the law as if tau_death were the whole classifier.
- SmolLM2-360M dead-head count: README table says 234, data/base_rate_analysis.json and threshold_transfer_summary.json say 261 - the repo is internally inconsistent and the reader copied the README figure without noticing.
- Kuramoto Attention 1M-param gap: reader says 'lags ~0.02 BPC'; measured html: enwik8 1.629/1.623 vs 1.616/1.610 (0.013) and CodeParrot 0.015/0.013. Minor overstatement; the 5M numbers (0.0118/0.0099 CodeParrot, within 0.01 enwik8), throughput (167k vs 335k, 460k vs 973k) and memory (4.62 vs 1.94 GB) all verified.
- Qwen3.8 listing verified (6 ids under Qwen/, no 50B: MEASURED HF API 2026-09-13), but the reader missed unsloth/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GGUF and third-party 'Qwen3.8-2B/4B/9B-Distill-GGUF' (empero-ai, unverified quality) that are the only Qwen3.8-labelled things that could fit 6 GB; and did not consider that '50b' may be a mis-hearing of '0.5B'/'0.8B' (Qwen3.5-0.8B exists).
- Microbenchmark reporting omitted box load; the 4-thread OpenBLAS f32 baseline collapses from 54 to 9.9 GOPS depending on contention, so the headline '242 vs 54' ratio is not reproducible as stated.
missing:
- The paper's own base-rate analysis and random-init control (paper.tex Table baserate, Table randinit) - the actual evidence the authors lean on - is absent from the reading; any town hypothesis about the 'law' should start from 'lift over random ~1.0'.
- Tsetlin Machines were catalogued but not turned into a seed, though they are the mature, gradient-free, bit-native CPU trainer closest to 'neuron = byte' (pyTsetlinMachine / TMU libraries exist); a Tsetlin vs DWN-EFD head-to-head on the same bit task is the natural first byte-neuron experiment.
- No seed at all for depth recurrence / looped transformers, which the owner named explicitly; the reader cited only a zero-experiment proposal and missed measured prior art (e.g. Geiping et al. 2025 recurrent-depth 'Huginn-3.5B', and universal/looped transformer results) that could give a CPU test-time-compute curve without training.
- Neuron-as-byte prior art beyond LUTs: Kauffman random Boolean networks, Numenta HTM (binary SDRs with pub/sub-like activation), Kanerva sparse distributed memory, and cellular-automaton reservoirs - all are 'bits in, one bit out, state machine per cell' and have known trainability results.
- The coherence-into-SNN thread was left as an open question rather than mapped: cosine alignment of write-back vs residual has direct spike-train analogues (van Rossum distance, SPIKE-synchrony, polychronous groups) that can be computed on the owner's snnTorch demos today, no LLM install needed.
- The fair CPU cost model for a LUT neuron is gather bandwidth, not popcount: at 2% connectivity the reader's own 32 ms/tick event-driven number at N=8192 is the relevant baseline, and no seed measures LUT-walk inferences/s against it.
- Owner's Vision 3 ('exponentially smarter') is only addressed by a poorly falsifiable null seed; a concrete measurable target (e.g. draft/verify acceptance rate, or debate/verify gain on a fixed 500-item slice with bootstrap CI) is missing.
- Kuramoto Attention's 2.4x memory overhead is GPU training memory; the CPU-relevant question is inference FLOPs per token vs softmax, which neither the paper nor the reader quantifies.
- DWN's CIFAR-10 57% (fully-connected only) and the 4.39-BLEU recurrent logic result together imply LUT/bit nets currently lose badly as soon as the task needs composition; the reading lists both but does not draw the scaling lesson for language, which is the owner's target.
strongest seed: Seed 6 (order-sensitive byte neuron vs order-blind popcount neuron on delayed-XOR / sequential MNIST), run on the shared harness of seed 5 (DWN-style EFD + Learnable Mapping, n=8 LUT). It is the only seed that tests the owner's genuinely novel claim (bit ORDER as code), has no prior art either way, costs ~3 CPU hours with no downloads or installs, and yields knowledge whether it succeeds or fails; seed 5 alone mostly replicates DWN at n=8.
weakest seed: Seed 2 (coherence law recall >= 80% and < 5% perplexity change): already falsified by data in the reader's own clone (safe_recall 0.315, loss 2.74 -> 5.58 on GPT-2), rests on a misreading of the paper's base-rate section, and gates on a pip install plus 1.5 GB download. Runner-up: seed 4, whose 4-hour budget cannot deliver its own 2-epoch falsifier on this CPU.
