CRITIC (confidence 0.82):
unsupported:
- Seed #1's headline premise — '95-100% precision across six model families' — is quoted from the README without the paper's own base-rate table (scratchpad/dead-head/paper.tex L533-566, MEASURED): random selection of the same count from the non-protected pool scores 93.2-99.9% precision; on GPT-2 Medium the threshold (98.2%) is BELOW random (99.4%), on Qwen2.5 0.5B it is exactly equal (95.5% vs 95.5%). The authors write 'We do not claim that precision is the primary evidence for the threshold.' Seed #1's success criterion (ablating marked-dead heads moves PPL <0.1) is therefore nearly guaranteed by base rate and does not test the formula at all; the falsifier is vacuous without an equal-count random-head control.
- Seed #2 asserts a 'dead-head coupling matrix ... yields a sparse coupled-oscillator (Kuramoto) adjacency'. The paper computes a per-head SCALAR z_h = sqrt(d)*mean-coupling (README L55-62, paper.tex L434); the oscillators in the model are TOKEN states on S^(d-1), heads are the couplings between tokens. There is no head-to-head or neuron-to-neuron adjacency anywhere in the repo; thresholding a scalar per head gives a set, not a matrix. The 'map to import into an SNN' the owner asked for is an undefined construction that the reader presented as if it already existed.
- Seed #1's cpu_experiment says 'use the dead-head repo's own scripts/ if they are numpy/torch-only'. MEASURED: 5 of 11 scripts import transformers, including the only ones that matter — 98_coherence_pruning_harness.py, coherence_anatomy_scan.py, 98_random_init_control.py. transformers is not installed (import fails, MEASURED). The seed as written cannot run without a package install the survey forbids and no node authorises.
- Seed #1 proposes to 'reproduce on a tiny model (<=0.5B)' as if novel. MEASURED paper.tex L383-384: Qwen2.5 0.5B (d=896, 157/336 dead) and SmolLM2 360M (d=960, 234/480 dead) are already rows in the paper's main table — this is a re-run, not a reproduction on new ground.

The reader produced zero seeds on depth recurrence or KV sharing, the two threads most directly aimed at 'more compute per token from fewer parameters' on CPU. g14.md L106 says 'the thought master reads and digests it, nobody summarizes it from memory' — the survey should at least name the paper's claimed parameter/compute trade and whether any looped checkpoint small enough for CPU exists.
- Architecture mismatch: HF search results (2026-09-13) describe Qwen3.8-27B as a hybrid Gated DeltaNet LINEAR attention + full attention model with an MTP speculative-decoding head. The dead-head derivation assumes softmax attention heads as couplings on the LayerNorm sphere and was validated only on softmax families (GPT-2, Qwen2, Llama, Gemma). Whether tau_death applies to linear-attention layers is an open, cheap, and genuinely new question — and MTP/speculative decoding is a CPU-relevant efficiency lever nobody mentioned.
- Random-selection control is mandatory for ANY dead-head hypothesis (the paper's own Table baserate). A seed that beats base rate — e.g. 'threshold-selected heads removed TOGETHER (not individually) hurt less than an equal-count random set removed together' — is the only formulation with information content; the paper itself says individual ablation is a weak test (paper.tex L538).
- The random-init control (9-18x learned vs random, paper.tex L502-527) is the cheaper and more falsifiable reproduction target than precision: it needs one forward pass per model, no ablation ground truth, and a 0.36B/0.5B model in fp32 fits in ~2 GB. Still needs transformers, so the runtime decision gates it too — the reader should have listed which seeds are blocked on 'install transformers' vs truly zero-install.
- The [region] box has an old Intel HD iGPU: OpenVINO IR builds exist (OpenVINO/Qwen3.8-27B-int8-ov surfaced in search) and OpenVINO is the only realistic iGPU runtime there; onnxruntime is installed here. Neither runtime is discussed as the CPU/iGPU path; the reader's open question 'which runtime' should have been narrowed to the two that need no new binaries.

strongest seed: Seed #3 (byte-neuron in numpy vs a snntorch LIF raster) — the only seed that runs today with zero installs, zero model downloads, zero spend, in minutes, on the owner's own named thread; either outcome is informative and it sits directly on the owner's prior lesson that spike encoding loses to graded input. It must be sharpened first: as written ('emulate a LIF to >90% bit agreement') is near-tautological — an 8-bit-quantised membrane IS a byte neuron — so the testable claim should be 'what task accuracy is lost at 8-bit state + 1 output bit per tick vs fp32 LIF on the same toy sequence task, 5 seeds', with the continuous-input lesson (snn-architecture.md L26-28) as the prior it is trying to overturn. Seed #5 is trivially cheap but also contentless (already answered by the reader's own search; belongs in ROUND 0 (a) as a one-line owner question, not a hypothesis).

## PANEL (three vision seats, raw)

### VISION 1 — there is always a more efficient way to infer on lighter hardware. Frame: on a 4-core arm64-N1 (dotprod-only NEON, no i8mm/SVE/bf16) every efficiency lever is a bytes-touched-per-token lever until proven otherwise; the box's regime (bandwidth vs compute) is unmeasured, the box is under load 9-11 with swap full, and no LLM runtime exists yet. So the chains go: (A) calibrate the box and the runtime levers that already exist in llama.cpp; (B) push below 4 bits and ask what quality it costs at 0.6B-4B; (C) test the one genuinely CPU-specific case for looped depth — cache residency — with the single Camber burst; (D) kill the coherence statistic as a head-pruning lever and re-test it as a KV-head bit-allocation lever; (E) treat the byte-neuron/oscillator thread as an inference-substrate question with kill-tests first. Camber's 3 GPU-hours are spent in exactly one place (C2). Every number below is tagged MEASURED (from a file/page in the digest or read this session) or ESTIMATE.

**A — Calibrate the box: bytes-touched-per-token as the one ISA constant (llama.cpp levers that exist today)** — Every other chain's cost and speed claim silently assumes a regime (bandwidth-bound vs compute-bound) that nobody has measured on 4x N1 with asimddp only; the literature has zero numbers for this ISA at 0.6B-4B. Worse, every on-box microbenchmark so far was taken at load avg ~10 with swap 100% used (MEASURED 22:21Z: 9.48/10.48/11.49, Swap 3/3 GB, MemAvailable 6 GB, pgmajfault 2,014,172); the critic's re-run showed f32 4-thread OpenBLAS collapsing 54 -> 9.9 GOPS between runs. So the first knowledge is whether any number is reproducible here, and the second is the regime. gcc + cmake are present (MEASURED), so the llama.cpp build is ~10 CPU-min and $0.
- A1: Under current tenancy, repeated llama-bench tg128 on the same GGUF (Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0, ~0.9 GB ESTIMATE) has a coefficient of variation > 25% across 5 runs spaced 10 min apart on this box, and < 5% on the idle 8 GB box — i.e. this box cannot host Vision-1 benchmarks until tenants are paused or the 8 GB box takes them. // FALSIFIER CV < 10% here across 5 runs with load avg > 8 and MemAvailable < 7 GB logged at each run. // EXP cmake -B build -DGGML_NATIVE=ON llama.cpp (record commit hash); download one GGUF; 5x llama-bench -t 4 -p 512 -n 128, each run wrapped with /proc/loadavg, MemAvailable, and pgmajfault delta; same script on the 8 GB box (record its lscpu, flags, DDR type). Output: a protocol file that every later tok/s in the town must carry (commit, load, MemAvailable, faults). // HW this box CPU + 8GB box // COST ~40 min wall, ~1 GB download, $0 // IF-FAILS The box is benchmarkable as-is under load; the 8 GB relay is unnecessary and every later chain saves a second run. Either way the town gets the mandatory logging protocol (the reusable part) for free.
- A2: Single-stream decode on this box is memory-bandwidth-bound: for Qwen3.5-0.8B and Qwen3-0.6B at Q8_0 / Q4_K_M / Q4_0, effective GB/s = tok/s x GGUF bytes lands at 0.5-1.0x the measured copy bandwidth (16-20 GB/s single-process MEASURED, with an unresolved factor-2 counting ambiguity flagged by the critic), so Q4 decodes ~1.6-1.9x faster than Q8 and Q4_0 (dotprod repack) is within +-10% of Q4_K_M. // FALSIFIER Q8_0 within 25% of Q4_K_M tok/s at 4 threads (compute-bound), or Q4_0 >= 20% faster than Q4_K_M (dequant compute dominates, the repack path is the lever). // EXP llama-bench ladder -t 1,2,4 -p 512 -n 128 on the 2 models x 3 quants; compute effective GB/s per row; re-run the numpy copy microbench in the same session with bytes counted explicitly once (read+write stated). Repeat the ladder on the 8 GB box and compare tok/s normalised by that box's measured bandwidth. // HW this box CPU + 8GB box // COST ~1.5 h wall after A1, ~3 GB downloads, $0 // IF-FAILS Compute-bound: bytes stop being the currency; the levers become int8 sdot kernels, Q4_0 repack and ternary LUT kernels (Chain B gets more interesting), and KV quantization becomes a memory-only lever (A3's speed claim is void before it runs).
- A3: KV quantization is a SPEED lever on this CPU, not only memory: on Qwen3-0.6B Q8_0 at 8K context (f16 KV 112 KB/token ESTIMATE from HF config 28 layers x 8 KV heads x 128 dim; q8_0 59.5 KB/token), -ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0 -fa on raises decode tok/s >= 15% over f16 KV because the int8 sdot path is what asimddp gives, with wikitext-2 KLD < 0.01 (7-27B measured 0.0064 in llama.cpp #23470); adding -ctv q4_0 buys < 5% more speed on this ISA. // FALSIFIER q8_0 KV tok/s <= f16 KV tok/s at 8K (the CPU flash-attention path with quantized V is a loss on N1), or KLD > 0.02 at 0.6B. // EXP llama-bench -p 8192 -n 64 with {f16/f16, q8_0/q8_0, q8_0/q4_0} and -fa on/off; llama-perplexity -c 2048 --kl-divergence-base against an f16-KV reference (0.6B f16 weights 1.2 GB + 8K f16 KV 0.9 GB fits in the 6 GB available). Do NOT go to 32K: a 32K f16 reference does not fit here and the box cannot prefill 32K in useful wall-clock (2-5 min ESTIMATE), so a Camber burst for long-context references is explicitly not worth it. // HW this box CPU // COST ~1 h CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS On dotprod-only ARM the FA + quantized-V path is slower: the town chooses KV types by RAM only, and long-context on this box is served by f16 KV + prompt caching. Also tells the 8 GB box (x86, different kernels) to re-measure rather than inherit.
- A4: The hybrid Gated-DeltaNet architecture (Qwen3.5-0.8B: 6 of 24 attention layers, 2 KV heads x 256 = 12 KB/token + ~18 MiB fixed state, MEASURED-from-config ESTIMATE) has a crossover: at ctx <= 512 dense Qwen3-0.6B decodes >= 1.3x faster (GDN CPU kernels without i8mm are immature), but at >= 8K the hybrid overtakes because dense KV traffic dominates; GDN prompt processing at 4K is >= 0.5x the dense model's. // FALSIFIER Hybrid slower than dense at every context up to 16K (kernel overhead never amortised), or GDN pp < 0.3x dense (the 9x KV win is unusable for streaming). // EXP llama-bench -p 4096 -n 64 at -c 512/2048/8192/16384 for both models; read 'KV self size' and recurrent-state lines from the load log; confirm --context-shift is refused on the hybrid and --ctx-checkpoints works. Record the llama.cpp commit (GDN kernels changed through 2026). // HW this box CPU // COST ~40 min CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS On this ISA the architecture-level KV win is not cashable: the town's long-context play becomes dense + q8 KV + prompt caching, and the Qwen3.5 line is chosen for benchmark scores, not speed — which changes the model ladder every other panel runs on.
- A5: For the owner's actual use (2-8K agent prompts), prefill is the binding cost, not decode or KV bytes: pp on 4 N1 cores for 0.8B-4B is 50-200 tok/s (ESTIMATE), so an 8K prompt costs 40 s-3 min, >= 5x the decode time of a 300-token answer; llama-server --cache-reuse 256 + --slot-save-path cuts a repeated-4K-prefix turn's TTFT >= 5x with byte-identical output at temperature 0. // FALSIFIER pp >= 400 tok/s for 0.8B (prefill is not binding), or cache-reuse TTFT gain < 2x, or outputs differ between cold and warm prefix (KV reuse changes logits). // EXP llama-server -np 2 --cache-reuse 256 --slot-save-path; one 4K shared system prefix, 5 different 200-token tails; time /completion cold vs warm; save slot, restart, restore, re-time; diff outputs; read /metrics for cache hit accounting. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30 min CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS If prefill is cheap, streaming/eviction levers (StreamingLLM --keep, context-shift) matter more than caching and get promoted; if reuse changes logits, prompt caching breaks determinism and the ensembles panel's per-item correctness vectors cannot use it.

**B — Below 4 bits on a dotprod-only CPU: does bytes-per-param keep buying tok/s, and what does it cost at 0.6B-4B?** — Weight quantization is THE CPU lever (head pruning removes < 8% of parameters; see D2), and the trove's only quantization artifact (supporting/qwen3_8b_role_aware_comparison.json: uniform GPTQ 4.25-bit +0.339 loss vs role-aware attn4/ffn_read4/ffn_write6 +0.0375 at 4.24 bits, MEASURED but unverifiable, no code) is on an 8B. Ternary (bitnet.cpp: 700M 1.70x, 7B 3.35x over llama.cpp on M2 Ultra MEASURED) has never been measured on N1 without i8mm, and 'bigger model at fewer bits' is folklore below 4B. Everything here reuses Chain A's harness and protocol.
- B1: bitnet.cpp's ARM TL1 kernel on BitNet-b1.58-2B-4T (~1.1 GB I2_S ESTIMATE) decodes >= 1.5x the tok/s of llama.cpp Qwen3.5-2B Q4_K_M (~1.3 GB ESTIMATE) at 4 threads on this box, i.e. ternary LUT kernels survive without i8mm. // FALSIFIER <= 1.1x tok/s at matched thread count and load. // EXP Build bitnet.cpp (ARM TL1) next to llama.cpp; download BitNet-b1.58-2B-4T GGUF and Qwen3.5-2B Q4_K_M; bench both with the A1 protocol; also run on the 8 GB box (x86 TL2) to separate ISA from architecture. // HW this box CPU + 8GB box // COST ~2 h wall, ~3 GB download, $0 // IF-FAILS T-MAC/bitnet gains are i8mm/AVX-specific; on N1 ternary is a RAM lever only (1.1 GB for a 2B), and B2 decides whether that RAM saving is worth its quality cost on the 8 GB box.
- B2: At matched bytes, native ternary loses to post-training Q4 of a stronger pretrain: BitNet-2B-4T scores within 5 pts of Qwen3.5-2B Q4_K_M (non-thinking, explicitly /no_think) on a fixed 200-item harness (100 ARC-style MC + 100 GSM8K, answer-flip protocol from llama.cpp #23470), and beats Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0 (~0.9 GB). // FALSIFIER > 10 pts below Qwen3.5-2B Q4, or below Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8 (then ternary-from-scratch is dominated on both axes at this scale). // EXP llama-server OpenAI API, temperature 0, per-item correctness vectors stored (shared with the ensembles panel); thinking mode fixed off for all Qwen runs; paired McNemar over items (200 items gives SE ~3.5 pts, so the 5/10-pt thresholds are the smallest honest ones). // HW this box CPU // COST ~2 h CPU (600 generations x 3 models at ESTIMATE 10-30 tok/s), $0 // IF-FAILS Ternary loses at 2B: the sub-4-bit path for this town is PTQ of the best available model (B3), and BitNet is dropped from the 8 GB box's ladder.
- B3: The 'larger model at fewer bits beats smaller at more bits' rule holds down to 3 bpw but breaks below it at tiny scale: at matched ~1.5 GB, Qwen3.5-4B IQ3_XXS beats Qwen3.5-2B Q4_K_M by >= 3 pts on the B2 harness, while Qwen3.5-4B IQ2_M does not. // FALSIFIER 4B@IQ3_XXS <= 2B@Q4_K_M (rule fails already at ~3 bpw for 4B), or 4B@IQ2_M > 2B@Q4_K_M (rule survives to 2 bpw; the 8 GB box should always run the biggest IQ2 that fits). // EXP llama-quantize with an imatrix from 50k wikitext tokens (ESTIMATE ~30-60 min CPU for 4B); produce IQ2_M, IQ3_XXS, Q4_K_M; run the B2 harness; also record tok/s per quant (IQ kernels are slower on CPU — the tok/s column is part of the answer). // HW this box CPU // COST ~3-4 h CPU, ~6 GB download (needs a 4B f16 or Q8 source), $0 // IF-FAILS The rule's breaking point at tiny scale is itself the knowledge: it fixes the 8 GB box's model choice (largest that fits at >= 3 bpw vs at Q4) with one number instead of folklore.
- B4: The role-aware result transfers below 1B: on Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3.5-2B, llama-quantize with --tensor-type ffn_down=Q6_K (the 'ffn write' role) and everything else Q4_K recovers >= 50% of the uniform-Q4_K KLD (vs f16) at <= 12% more bytes, and beats the stock Q4_K_M recipe by >= 10% KLD at equal bytes. // FALSIFIER < 20% KLD recovery, or delta vs stock Q4_K_M < 10% (K-quants already bake in the same asymmetry, so the 8B result is not new information). // EXP Three quants per model (uniform Q4_K, custom ffn_down=Q6_K, stock Q4_K_M); llama-perplexity --kl-divergence-base vs f16 on wikitext-2; bytes from file size; tok/s from A2 protocol. // HW this box CPU // COST ~1 h CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS The ffn-write sensitivity is scale-dependent or already captured by K-quants: custom recipes are dropped, and this also bounds what a role-aware recipe could do for any larger model on Camber — no burst needed to learn that.
- B5: Weight-quant and KV-quant errors stack additively, not multiplicatively: on Qwen3-0.6B, KLD(Q4_K_M weights + q8_0 KV) <= 1.2 x [KLD(Q4_K_M, f16 KV) + KLD(f16 weights, q8_0 KV)] at 2K context. // FALSIFIER Joint KLD > 1.5x the sum (levers interact; every combined config needs its own measurement). // EXP Four llama-perplexity runs (f16/f16 reference, Q4/f16, f16/q8, Q4/q8) at -c 2048 with --kl-divergence-base; one number. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30 min CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS Non-additive: the bytes-touched ledger (coalescence 1) gets a quality column that is not separable, and the town cannot pick weight quant and KV quant independently — a real constraint on the 8 GB box's config space.

**C — Looped depth on CPU: the case is cache residency, not FLOPs (and the one Camber burst)** — Every reader treated looped transformers as a parameter/RAM story and missed the only CPU-specific advantage: a shared block whose weights stay cache-resident across loops costs compute but not DRAM bandwidth, unlike a kL-layer dense model that streams k times more weights. RLT itself has zero experiments (rlt.txt MEASURED: 'does not report measured efficiency or scaling results'), Ouro at 4 loops burns ~10B-dense per-token FLOPs (ESTIMATE), and quantization error across reused weights is unmeasured anywhere. Training even a 10-20M toy pair is ~100+ CPU-hours at a realistic 15 GFLOPS sustained (ESTIMATE; critic's arithmetic), which is exactly what a 20-30 min GPU burst does — so this chain is where Camber's minutes go, and nowhere else.
- C1: This N1 VM has an exploitable cache tier: a batch-1 chain of fp32 matvecs whose total weights are <= 8 MB sustains >= 3x the bytes/s-equivalent of one with 128 MB of weights (L2 is 1 MB/core; the Ampere host's shared system cache is ~32 MB but contended by other tenants — size and residency unknown). // FALSIFIER <= 1.3x between 8 MB and 128 MB working sets (no cache tier the VM can exploit at batch 1; looping buys RAM only). // EXP numpy/torch fp32 (or the scratchpad NEON kernel) over weight sets of 4/8/16/32/64/128 MB, 200 layer-evals each, 1 and 4 threads, A1 logging protocol; repeat on the 8 GB box (a desktop CPU with a private L3 should show the knee clearly, which calibrates whether the VM's flatness is real or noisy-neighbour). // HW this box CPU + 8GB box // COST ~30 min, $0, no downloads // IF-FAILS The CPU-specific argument for looping is dead here: looped models are judged on RAM alone (which the 8 GB box cares about) and on accuracy-per-FLOP (Vision 2 panel's question), and C4 is skipped.
- C2: THE CAMBER BURST. A 4-layer tied block looped 4x (d=256, ~3.1M non-embedding params MEASURED arithmetic) trained with elastic loop count in [1,8] (LoopFormer recipe: random loop count + step conditioning + shortcut consistency) reaches >= 90% of a FLOPs-matched 16-layer untied model (~12.6M) on p-hop and multi-digit addition at 4 loops and >= 70% at 2 loops, whereas the same architecture trained at fixed 4 loops loses > 30% at 2 loops (Ouro's T=1 ~40% MMLU pattern, MEASURED 2510.25741v2); on key-value recall (memorisation) the looped model stays < 80% of untied. // FALSIFIER Elastic-looped < 80% of untied at 4 loops on both reasoning tasks, or no exit curve (2-loop and 4-loop accuracy within noise for the elastic model AND the fixed model), or looped >= untied on memorisation (gain is capacity, not depth). // EXP One Camber XS session, 20-30 GPU-minutes total (12.6M params x 100M tokens x 6 FLOPs = 7.6e15 per untied run, ESTIMATE ~2-5 min each on a 24 GB-class GPU): train 4L-untied, 4L-x4-fixed, 4L-x4-elastic, 16L-untied, and one sandwich-top cross-layer-KV 16L variant (2410.14442 recipe; xlayer.txt L561-563 shows the paper needed 1.7B tokens on 110M — out of reach, so this is the toy-scale proxy), each on 100M tokens of three synthetic tasks; first 5 minutes: nvidia-smi to resolve the XS GPU type and bandwidth (goal:g14.3(b), still unknown). Export checkpoints (< 100 MB) for C3/C4 on CPU. Everything after the burst is CPU. // HW Camber XS minutes (20-30) then this box CPU // COST ~0.5 GPU-h of the 3 GPU-h/month (~$1.50 at 3 credits/h = $3/h MEASURED cambercloud.com/pricing), plus ~2 h CPU for eval // IF-FAILS At toy scale looping is not depth: the town drops intra-token looped depth from Vision 1 entirely, keeps only the cross-token feedback variant (RLT eqs 2.9-2.11) for the SNN bridge, and has spent 1/6 of one month's GPU budget to close a whole thread — the cheapest possible closure.
- C3: Quantization error compounds sub-linearly across loops: with the C2 checkpoints, int8 dynamic quantization of the shared block gives KLD(k loops) <= k x KLD(1 loop) for k in {2,4,8}, and 4-bit fake-quant stays <= 2k x KLD(1 loop); i.e. a looped model tolerates the same bit-width as a dense one. // FALSIFIER Super-linear compounding (KLD at 4 loops > 4x KLD at 1 loop for int8, or 4-bit diverges), meaning looped models need more bits and the RAM win is erased. // EXP torch-cpu: quantize the tied block (torch.ao dynamic int8; 4-bit via per-channel fake-quant); measure KLD vs the fp32 looped model on 10k held-out tokens per task at each loop count; also on the 16L untied model at matched bits as the control. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30 min CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS Looping and quantization are anti-synergistic — the 8 GB box's RAM-minimal design must pick one (looped fp16/int8 vs dense Q4), which is a hard, reusable constraint; nobody has published this either way.
- C4: At batch 1 on this box the 4L-x4 looped model decodes >= 1.4x the tok/s of the FLOPs-identical 16L untied model (weights 4x smaller, cache-resident per C1), and the ratio tracks C1's cache curve within 20%. // FALSIFIER < 1.1x tok/s (same FLOPs, no cache benefit at model level). // EXP Hand-rolled torch-cpu decode loop for both checkpoints, 512 tokens x 5 prompts, 1 and 4 threads, A1 protocol; compare to C1's prediction. // HW this box CPU + 8GB box // COST ~20 min, $0 // IF-FAILS Confirms C1's negative at model level; the town records 'looping = RAM only on these boxes' as a verdict with two measurements behind it.
- C5: An off-the-shelf looped model has no elastic depth: ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B run with total_ut_steps=2 gives < 60% of its 4-step accuracy on 100 GSM8K items (collapse), so any CPU looped play needs the C2 elastic recipe at scale — which is beyond 3 GPU-h/month. Requires torch int8 dynamic quant (fp32 weights 5.6 GB do not fit in 6 GB available; bf16 matmul on this box is 2 GFLOPS vs 50 fp32 MEASURED by the critic, so bf16 is dead on arrival). // FALSIFIER 2 steps >= 85% of 4-step accuracy at ~55% wall time (elastic depth exists for free; the C2 recipe is unnecessary and Ouro-1.4B int8 becomes the 8 GB box's candidate). // EXP Gated on a director-approved pip install of transformers<4.56 (Ouro card requirement MEASURED) with trust_remote_code; torch.ao dynamic int8; 100 GSM8K /no-CoT-cap 256 tokens at total_ut_steps 1,2,3,4; log tok/s (ESTIMATE ~1 tok/s fp32-equivalent at 4 loops). // HW this box CPU (or 8GB box if int8 load fits) // COST ~4-8 h CPU unattended, ~2.8 GB download, one install decision, $0 // IF-FAILS Off-the-shelf looped depth IS elastic on CPU: the town gets a 1.4B-class model with a runtime speed/accuracy knob and skips training entirely — the best possible outcome for Vision 1, which is why the kill-test is worth its hours.

**D — The coherence statistic: kill it as a head-pruning lever, re-test it as a KV-head bit-allocation lever** — The trove's own data already says head pruning cannot be a Vision-1 lever: the tau_death law's lift over random selection is 1.00-1.02 (MEASURED data/base_rate_analysis.json), removing the dead set costs +1.13 nat on Qwen2.5-0.5B (MEASURED, 3-seed std 0.48), safe_recall is 0.315 on GPT-2 (MEASURED full_gpt2_head144_..._dimaware.json), structural removal gives no speedup at seq <= 256 (MEASURED timing JSONs), and Qwen-class GQA (g=7) leaves 3/48 KV groups compactable. What is NOT known is whether the per-head observable has any use for the lever that does matter on CPU — per-KV-head bit allocation — which is a heavy-tailed-sensitivity question nobody has asked. Chain D therefore opens with the paper's own missing controls (15 min) and ends with a genuine KV question. All of D1-D5 gate on ONE install decision (transformers; the scanner's other dependency coherence_lattice is private and must be reimplemented — the cosine measurement is ~100 lines). Licence note: the method is PolyForm-NC + patent-pending (64/031,983 MEASURED); research use only.
- D1: Null, stated as the paper's missing controls: on Qwen2.5-0.5B (fp32, 1.98 GB MEASURED), removing the 157 tau-dead heads (bundled flags) costs an eval delta-loss within 1 std (0.48 nat) of removing 157 uniformly random non-protected heads, AND mean-ablation (replace each removed head's write-back by its calibration mean) cuts the dead-set damage by >= 50% — i.e. the catastrophe is a LayerNorm/mean-shift artifact, not lost information. // FALSIFIER Dead-set removal is >= 1.0 nat better than every one of 10 random draws AND mean-ablation helps < 20% — the dead set is special and carries coupling structure. // EXP Reuse decisions[].dead from data/qwen25_05b_head336_small_theory_redundancy_v2_boundary2.json; 11 zero-ablation masks + 11 mean-ablation masks (means from 8 calibration sequences) on 16 x 128 WikiText-2 tokens; one forward per mask. Note the ground truth's weakness: the bundled 'unsafe' labels come from 8 x 128 TinyStories tokens with a 1%-of-baseline threshold (0.023 nat) — inside sampling noise — so this uses joint delta-loss, not those labels. // HW this box CPU // COST ~20 min CPU, ~3 GB RAM, one transformers install decision, $0 // IF-FAILS If the dead set IS special, the coherence map has content and D4/E4 get a real coupling signal — the owner's thread earns its next hypothesis. If the null holds (expected), tau_death is retired as a pruning criterion in one verdict node and the SNN import must source coupling from something causal (ablation Jacobians), not cosine.
- D2: Head pruning cannot buy CPU speed even in the best case: structurally deleting all 157 dead heads (47%) from Qwen2.5-0.5B removes < 8% of parameters (attention q/o slices only; GPT-2 lost 6.6% params for 29% of heads, MEASURED gpt2_structural_timing_v1.json) and, at g=7, < 7% of KV bytes/token (3/48 groups fully dead MEASURED), so decode tok/s changes < 5% at seq 128 and 1024. // FALSIFIER > 10% tok/s gain at either length (attention kernel cost is head-count-bound on this CPU in a way the parameter count hides). // EXP Arithmetic from config.json first (free); then one masked-forward timing pair at 128 and 1024 tokens with the A1 protocol; report bytes/token and params before/after. // HW this box CPU // COST ~20 min CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS A hidden attention-kernel cost that head removal buys on CPU — which would make KV-group compaction on small-g GQA models (SmolLM2 g=3: -27.5% KV bytes, +31% prefill at 1024 MEASURED) worth porting to llama.cpp.
- D3: Per-KV-head quantization sensitivity is heavy-tailed: on Qwen3-0.6B fp32 (28 layers x 8 KV heads = 224 KV heads, 2.4 GB), fake-quantizing one KV head's K to 4-bit per-token absmax and measuring KLD on 16 x 512 WikiText tokens gives a Gini >= 0.4 across heads (top 20% of heads carry >= 60% of summed KLD), and K-sensitivity exceeds V-sensitivity by >= 3x (7-27B: q4_0 K collapses, q4_0 V changes 1/500 answers, MEASURED #23470; K/V norm ratio up to 182x on Qwen2.5-1.5B, MEASURED #20969). // FALSIFIER Gini < 0.2 (uniform sensitivity; per-head allocation has nothing to exploit), or V as sensitive as K at 0.6B. // EXP torch hooks on k_proj/v_proj outputs post-RoPE (llama.cpp quantizes post-RoPE) — 224 x 2 single-head passes plus one all-heads pass; store per-head KLD, per-head K and V norm statistics; 4 threads fp32. // HW this box CPU // COST ~1.5 h CPU, transformers install, ~3 GB RAM, $0 // IF-FAILS Uniform: llama.cpp's single -ctk/-ctv choice is the whole KV-quant story at 0.6B, per-head anything is closed for Vision 1, and A3's result stands as final.
- D4: The bridge test for the owner's thread: the dead-head observable aggregated per KV group (mean z_h over the 2 q-heads per KV head on Qwen3-0.6B, 16 q / 8 kv) correlates with D3's per-KV-head KLD at Spearman >= 0.4 — one statistic would then serve both head death and KV bit allocation. // FALSIFIER |Spearman| < 0.2 over 224 KV heads (the coherence statistic has no KV application). // EXP Reimplement the scanner's cosine (pre-hook on block input for the reference residual, pre-hook on o_proj for per-head write-back, mean over positions and 32 batches — MEASURED coherence_anatomy_scan.py L150-206) in ~100 lines of torch on the same 8K tokens as D3; also record write-norm ratio r_h = ||s_h||/||x|| (the paper's confound: c_h conflates coupling magnitude with alignment); join with D3 and correlate both z_h and r_h. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30 min CPU after D3, $0 // IF-FAILS The coherence statistic leaves Vision 1 entirely: it is neither a pruning criterion (D1) nor a KV-allocation signal, and lives on only as an oscillator-map source in Chain E (E4) — a clean scoping of the owner's thread with two measurements behind it.
- D5: Mixed per-head KV precision beats uniform: using D3's ranking, 4-bit K on the least-sensitive 70% of KV heads and 8-bit on the rest reaches <= 1.5x the KLD of uniform q8 at <= 65% of q8 KV bytes/token, and beats uniform q4 K by >= 3x KLD at ~ +15% bytes. // FALSIFIER > 2x uniform-q8 KLD (per-head sensitivities interact; the sum of single-head KLDs does not predict the joint), or no advantage over uniform q4. // EXP torch fake-quant of the joint configuration; report KLD and bytes/token; stop at the measurement — llama.cpp lacks per-head KV types, and patching it is an MVP, not research. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30 min CPU, $0 // IF-FAILS Sensitivities are not separable per head: uniform KV types win and the design space collapses to A3's single choice — which also tells the ensembles/streaming work not to chase per-head eviction.

**E — Byte-neuron and oscillators as a CPU inference substrate: kill-tests before any architecture** — Frankly: prior art is dense (DWN ICML 2024 LUT neurons 98.31% MNIST at 23.4 KiB; difflogic; Tsetlin; rank-order coding; Kuramoto Attention 2606.11585v2 within 0.01 BPC of a transformer at 5M params but 50% throughput and 2.4x memory; recurrent logic nets 4.39 BLEU vs GRU 5.41 vs ~27 for a transformer — all MEASURED in the digest), and the owner's own trove says spike input loses to graded input and stacking micro-nets is a dead end (snn-architecture.md L26-28, L40 MEASURED). Under Vision 1 the byte-neuron's only claim to efficiency is integer-only inference, and the on-box evidence for it (242 binary GOPS/core) measures a dense XNOR GEMM, not an 8-input LUT neuron, which is a gather (32 ms/tick at N=8192 MEASURED under load). So the honest first hypotheses are kill-tests that need no models, no installs, and can run on the idle 8 GB box.
- E1: The byte-neuron is gather-bound, not popcount-bound: an 8-input LUT neuron network (uint8 state, 256-entry table walk, event-driven, 2% connectivity) sustains < 10% of the synaptic-ops/s of the dense NEON XNOR-popcount kernel (211-242 binary GOPS/core MEASURED at load ~10; scratchpad/pop.c), and the gap widens with N because of cache misses on the subscription map. // FALSIFIER LUT-walk >= 25 G synaptic-ops/s per core at N = 8192 or 65536 (the byte neuron IS competitive per op on CPU). // EXP C + numpy: N in {1024, 8192, 65536}, fan-in 8, thresholds set from fan-in (the fixed-threshold sim died at N=1024 and saturated at N=8192 — MEASURED lesson), report ns/synaptic-op and firing rate; compare to the dense kernel at 4 threads; run primarily on the idle 8 GB box (x86, so also tests ISA independence) and once here with the A1 protocol. // HW 8GB box (primary) + this box CPU // COST ~1 h, $0, no downloads, no installs // IF-FAILS The byte-neuron is a viable integer-only CPU substrate per op: E3 (state semantics) and a gradient-free training rule become the priority, and Vision 1 gains a candidate substrate that bitnet/T-MAC do not cover.
- E2: State precision bound before writing any byte-neuron code: snntorch 0.9.4's snn.Leaky state_quant (installed, MEASURED) on the trove's MNIST 784-1000-10, 25 steps, 1 epoch, seed 42 (fixed-beta baseline 96.10% MEASURED) loses <= 0.5 pp at 256 membrane levels, >= 2 pp at 16 levels and >= 10 pp at 2 levels — with a SPIKE-COUNT readout (the corpus used summed output membrane, which a 1-bit-out neuron cannot expose; that readout baseline must be run first, ~142 s/epoch on the owner's box, ESTIMATE 3-5 min here under load). // FALSIFIER 8-bit state loses > 2 pp (the neuron needs wider state; 'neuron = byte' is falsified at the cheapest point), or 1-bit state loses < 3 pp (the byte is over-provisioned; a bit suffices and the interesting object is the wiring, not the state). // EXP snn_standard_model/experiment_infrastructure.py + state_quant flag + spike-count readout; 4 runs (fp32 count-readout baseline, 256, 16, 2 levels); JSON results in the existing harness format. // HW this box CPU (or 8GB box) // COST ~20-30 min CPU, MNIST already on disk (~/.cache/torchvision MEASURED), $0 // IF-FAILS Either outcome sizes the neuron's state before a line of new code: the town learns whether the owner's byte is the right width from four runs of an existing harness.
- E3: The owner's semantic question (spatial byte over 8 upstream neurons vs temporal byte = one upstream's last 8 bits) decided by emulation: an 8-bit shift register + 256-entry LUT reproduces the spikyNN LIF spike train (beta 0.8, thr 1.0, w 0.8, 200 steps) with <= 20% spike edit distance, and the best-LUT distance scales with beta^8 (0.8^8 = 0.17 of membrane memory lies beyond 8 ticks). // FALSIFIER Best of all 256 LUTs > 35% edit distance at beta 0.8, or distance does not track beta^8 across beta in {0.5, 0.8, 0.95}. // EXP Exhaustive 256-LUT sweep in numpy against recorded LIF trains for N1 and N2 (spikyNN/snn_neuron.py), three betas; seconds of compute, deterministic, seed-free. // HW this box CPU // COST < 5 min, $0 // IF-FAILS The temporal byte cannot emulate leaky integration: the owner's 'which bits in what order' must mean the spatial (fan-in) byte — a LUT over 8 upstream neurons, i.e. DWN's object (ICML 2024), whose training rule (Extended Finite Difference + Learnable Mapping) then becomes the thing to reuse rather than invent.
- E4: Kill-test for 'generate coupled-oscillator maps from the pruning equation': a synaptic mask derived from a head-head coupling matrix K_hh' = E_i cos(s_h(i), s_h'(i)) over Qwen2.5-0.5B's 336 heads (write-backs 336 x 1024 x 896 fp32 ~1.2 GB, or streamed per layer), thresholded at 4/sqrt(d) and used as the synaptic mask of a 2-layer snntorch net on a toy sequence task, trains no better than a random mask of equal density over 5 seeds. // FALSIFIER Coupling-mask accuracy exceeds random-mask accuracy by > 2 SD across 5 seeds (the map carries transferable structure). // EXP Gated on the same transformers install as D1; hook write-backs for 8 x 128 tokens; build K; cluster/threshold; snntorch Leaky net with membrane readout (the trove's own lesson); 2 masks x 5 seeds on a synthetic sequence task; also simulate the 336-node Kuramoto system on K (mean-field trick: 154 us/step at N=4096 MEASURED) and report the order parameter r. // HW this box CPU // COST ~1 h CPU, ~3 GB RAM, $0 // IF-FAILS If the mask helps, the oscillator map has content and this is where Chain D (D1/D4) and Chain E coalesce into a second-order set; if the null holds (expected, since c_h conflates coupling magnitude with alignment and D1 likely shows the dead set is not special), the owner's map needs a causal coupling (per-head ablation Jacobian K_{h->h'}, ~1-1.5 h CPU) rather than cosine, and the town knows exactly which quantity to compute next.
- E5: Oscillator attention has no CPU exemption: a Kuramoto-attention layer (2606.11585v2: score via gated cosine, value step = Kuramoto coupling, bounded phase update) implemented in torch-cpu costs >= 1.5x the per-token time of softmax attention at d=256, seq 256 (GPU paper: 50% throughput, 2.4x memory MEASURED), because the exp() saved is cheaper on NEON than the extra sin/cos and complex accumulation. // FALSIFIER <= 1.1x per-token time at inference (oscillator attention is CPU-cheap; Vision 2's panel has a reason to train one). // EXP Untrained layers, forward only, 4 threads, 1000 tokens x 5 repeats, A1 protocol; no training, no data; count FLOPs analytically as the second column. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30 min, $0 // IF-FAILS A CPU-cheap oscillator attention would be the one place the owner's oscillator thread and Vision 1 meet directly; the town would then fund the 1M-param enwik8 replication (which the critic showed does not fit a 4 CPU-hour budget — it would be a second Camber candidate in a later month, not this one).

coalescence:
- CP1 — the bytes-touched ledger (after A1-A4 and B1): one table per model on each box — GGUF bytes, KV bytes/token, tok/s at ctx {512, 8K}, effective GB/s, KLD vs f16 — tests the second-order hypothesis 'tok/s on these boxes = f(bytes touched per token) with one ISA constant'. If it holds, every later lever (KV quant, GDN, ternary, looping, per-head KV) is evaluated as a bytes-touched delta before it is run; if it fails, the town knows it is compute-bound and re-ranks toward kernels (B1, E1) over bytes.
- CP2 — precision x reuse (C3 with B4/B5): whether a shared/looped block tolerates the same bits as a dense one, combined with whether weight and KV quant stack additively, yields the second-order question 'what is the RAM-minimal architecture for the 8 GB box' (looped + Q4 + constant-state hybrid vs dense + IQ3 + q8 KV). This is decided from C2's checkpoints and B3/B5's KLDs without new training.
- CP3 — one per-head statistic or none (D3/D4 with A3): if D4 holds, the coherence observable becomes a KV-allocation tool and the owner's thread enters Vision 1 through the KV door, not the pruning door; if D4 fails, the statistic is scoped out of Vision 1 in a single verdict and lives only in Chain E — either way Chains A and D merge into 'KV precision policy for tiny GQA models on CPU'.
- CP4 — substrate or not (E1/E2/E3 with D1/E4): E1-E3 decide whether the byte-neuron is a viable integer-only substrate and what its state semantics are; D1/E4 decide whether the coherence-derived map has transferable structure. Only if BOTH are positive does the second-order hypothesis 'coupling map as the byte-neuron subscription wiring' exist; if either is negative, the SNN thread is re-homed as a Vision 2/3 question (accuracy, cooperation) and stops competing for Vision 1 tokens.
- CP5 — cross-panel harness (A5, B2 with the ensembles panel): the 200-item MC/GSM8K harness with per-item correctness vectors, thinking mode fixed off, and the A1 logging protocol is the shared eval every panel's H2+ needs; adopting it before any chain's second hypothesis is what makes results comparable across the three visions, and A5 decides whether prompt caching can be used inside it without changing logits.
- CP6 — the Camber decision (after C1 and before C2): C1's cache-residency result on both boxes is the go/no-go for spending the month's single burst; if C1 fails on both boxes, C2 still runs (the elastic-depth and quant-across-loops questions stand on their own) but the town's stated reason for looped depth changes from 'CPU speed' to 'RAM on the 8 GB box'.
critique of the owner plan:
- Head pruning via the coherence equation cannot be a Vision-1 lever on CPU, and the trove already proves it: tau_death's lift over random head selection is 1.00-1.02 (MEASURED data/base_rate_analysis.json — the paper's own table), safe_recall is 0.315 on GPT-2 (MEASURED), removing the dead set costs +1.13 nat on Qwen2.5-0.5B, structural removal is SLOWER at seq <= 256 (GPT-2 Medium -46% at 128, MEASURED), heads are < 8% of parameters (MLP dominates), and Qwen-class GQA at g=7 leaves 3/48 KV groups compactable. The cheaper path is KV-head-level precision (Chain D3-D5) and KV quantization that already ships in llama.cpp (A3); the coherence statistic should be tested once as a KV-head signal (D4) and otherwise scoped out of Vision 1. Also: the method is PolyForm-NC + patent-pending 64/031,983 (MEASURED) — research use only.
- 'qwen3.8 50b' does not exist and is almost certainly a speech-to-text artifact of a spoken, hedged line ('maybe'); the only public Qwen3.8 weights are 27B dense (Q4_K_M 17 GB MEASURED), Flash-Next (125B total, 6B active, includes a 51B n-gram embedding — the likeliest '50b'), and 2.4T-A95B. None fits this box (6 GB available) or the 8 GB box, and the owner's own later correction ('all models tiny enough to run on CPU + regular RAM', g14.md L108 MEASURED) supersedes it. The on-charter line is Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B (Apache-2.0, hybrid GDN), plus Gemma 4 E2B/E4B, SmolLM3-3B, Phi-4-mini, LFM2. Recording any '50B' candidate in a node would propagate an error; it belongs as a one-line owner question, not a hypothesis.
- The box itself is the first bug: load avg 9.5-11.5 on 4 cores, swap 100% used, MemAvailable 6 GB, 2.01M major page faults (MEASURED 22:21Z). Every microbenchmark the readers cite (242 binary GOPS, 54 f32 GOPS, 20.1 GB/s, 2-3x jitter on encoders, a 61 s test that should take 30) is a load artifact; the critic reproduced f32 4-thread OpenBLAS at 9.9 GOPS vs the reader's 54. No Vision-1 number is trustworthy until the desktop tenants are paused or the idle 8 GB box becomes the benchmark box. Cheapest path: make the 8 GB box the benchmark host (A1) and require load/MemAvailable/fault logging on every tok/s.
- The KV-cache and streaming thread as framed ('fold the papers in') imports GPU literature at 4B-32B with zero CPU or sub-4B numbers (H2O, SnapKV, PyramidKV, TriAttention, Random Attention — none in llama.cpp; Random Attention drops 6.5 pts vs full cache at 4x, a column the reader omitted). On this box decode is plausibly bandwidth-bound, which makes KV quant a SPEED lever (A3) — the literature never says so — but at the owner's real prompt lengths (2-8K) KV is < weights until ~5K tokens on a 0.6B, and the binding wall-clock cost is prefill (A5), not KV bytes. Pick levers that exist in the runtime (q8_0 KV, --keep/context-shift, --cache-reuse, hybrid GDN) and measure them before importing eviction methods that would need a fork.
- Looped/recurrent depth: the RLT paper has zero experiments (rlt.txt MEASURED), Ouro at 4 loops costs ~10B-dense FLOPs per token (ESTIMATE) so looping saves RAM not compute, Ouro-1.4B needs transformers<4.56 + trust_remote_code, and bf16 on this box runs at 2 GFLOPS vs 50 fp32 (MEASURED by the critic) — any 'bf16 on CPU' plan is dead on arrival. The one CPU-specific argument (cache residency of the shared block) has never been measured by anyone and costs 30 minutes (C1); quantization error compounding across loops is unmeasured anywhere and gates both boxes (C3). Training the toy pair on CPU is ~100+ hours; it is the single correct use of the month's Camber budget (C2, 20-30 min, ~$1.50), and the burst's first 5 minutes should resolve the still-unknown XS GPU type (goal:g14.3(b)).
- The byte-neuron / oscillator / SNN thread, judged on Vision 1 alone, is behind heavy prior art and the owner's own negative results: DWN (ICML 2024) already trains 6-input LUT neurons with learnable wiring; bit-logic recurrent nets sit at 4.39 BLEU vs ~27 for a transformer; the trove's snn-architecture.md says spike input loses to graded input and stacking micro-nets is a dead end; and the 242-GOPS popcount figure measures a dense BNN, not the gather-bound LUT neuron (32 ms/tick at N=8192 MEASURED). Its honest Vision-1 promise is integer-only inference with a gradient-free training rule nobody has costed. Cheapest path: three kill-tests with no models or installs (E1-E3, < 2 h total, the 8 GB box is idle), and route accuracy/cooperation questions to the Vision 2/3 seats. Separately, five chains currently gate on ONE decision — installing transformers and building llama.cpp — which should be taken once by the director, not re-litigated per seed.

### VISION 2 — smarter inference over time on CPU-only tiny models: test-time compute (sequential thinking vs parallel sampling), verifier-gated selection, confidence cascades, graph-routed specialist slots trained on the corpus this graph already holds (782 hypotheses / 170 verdicts / COMPLETE.md failure categories), depth recurrence and oscillator synchronization as test-time knobs, and a standing benchmark harness so the tiny-model ceiling is a measured curve month over month. Grounded on this box as read 2026-09-13: 4x arm64-N1 (asimddp only), 6 GB available of 23, swap full, no llama.cpp/ollama/transformers; MNIST IDX present; torch-cpu 2.10, snntorch 0.9.4, sklearn 1.8 installed. Every chain starts with a kill-test or a regime calibration, and every hypothesis names what is learned on failure — the town moral is knowledge per token, not tokens saved.

**A. Regime calibration: where does test-time compute pay on THIS CPU (sequential vs parallel vs adaptive)** — Every Vision-2 lever spends CPU-seconds; without a measured tok/s regime and a thinking-vs-sampling comparison at matched tokens, every other chain's cost line is a guess (the critics found 3-15x errors in token estimates because Qwen3 tinies default to thinking mode). Step 0 is unbudgeted anywhere in the trove: build llama.cpp from source (~10 CPU-min, record commit hash) and download Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B Q4_K_M + Q8_0 GGUFs (~6 GB disk, 34 GB free). Needs a director GO for the build; no paid API, no GPU.
- V2-A1: Single-stream decode on 4 N1 cores is memory-bandwidth-bound: measured tok/s x GGUF bytes for Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B at Q4_K_M and Q8_0 lands within 0.5-1.0x of the measured single-process copy bandwidth (MEASURED 16.5-20 GB/s in the trove, contested by the critic), and Q8_0 is slower than Q4_K_M by roughly the bytes ratio (~1.8x). Prefill (pp512) is compute-bound at <100 tok/s for the 4B. // FALSIFIER tok/s x bytes < 0.4x bandwidth for the 0.8B and 2B, or Q8_0 within 25% of Q4_K_M (then dequant compute, not bandwidth, limits; int8-dotprod kernels and ternary become the lever, and batched sampling in chain B is nearly free). // EXP llama-bench -p 512 -n 128 -t 4 on 6 GGUFs (3 models x 2 quants) with free RAM, load average and pgmajfault logged before each run; compute effective GB/s = tok/s x file size; run the numpy copy microbench in the same minute so the bandwidth denominator is measured under the same contention. Record llama.cpp commit. // HW this box CPU (first); repeat on the 8 GB box as a control row // COST ~1 h wall incl. build; $0; 0 GPU // IF-FAILS If compute-bound: batching k samples per step is almost free on this box (Vision 3/B chains get cheaper), KV quant is a memory not a speed lever, and ternary/int8-dotprod kernels are the Vision-1 priority. Either way the town gets its first build-hash-pinned tok/s table, which nothing in the public literature has for a 4-core N1.
- V2-A2: At matched generated tokens on 200 GSM8K items, ONE thinking-mode sample (budget B in {256,512,1024}) beats maj@k of no-think samples at the same total tokens for Qwen3.5-2B and 4B by >=5 pts, while for Qwen3.5-0.8B the ordering reverses (thinking loops/derails; the artificialanalysis note on 0.8B thinking-loop tendency). // FALSIFIER maj@k no-think >= thinking-1 on 2B/4B at matched tokens, or the 0.8B gains >=5 pts from thinking with a 512 cap (then sequential compute is the right axis at every tiny size and self-consistency is moot below 4B). // EXP llama-server OpenAI API, temperature 0 for thinking (1 sample) vs T=0.7 for no-think k in {2,4,8}; fixed answer-extraction regex; per-item correctness vectors stored; paired McNemar over items; report accuracy vs total tokens AND vs wall seconds; the /no_think toggle and thinking budget cap logged per run. // HW this box CPU (0.8B/2B); 4B runs may need the 8 GB box if firefox/ffmpeg tenants stay // COST ~4-6 CPU-h across 3 models (ESTIMATE: 200 items x ~1.5k tokens x 6 configs at 10-40 tok/s); $0 // IF-FAILS Learns at which parameter count sequential reasoning stops working — the tiny-model-specific fact the literature (Snell, Liu) never measured below 1B — and gives chain B its sampling regime for free (the k samples are stored).
- V2-A3: The accuracy-per-CPU-second frontier at <=4B on this box is not monotone in parameters: Qwen3.5-2B with a 512-token thinking cap beats Qwen3.5-4B no-think per CPU-second on GSM8K+IFEval (100+100 items), while Gemma-4-E2B (5.1B real params, 2.3B compute) sits in the 2B speed tier not the 4B tier. // FALSIFIER 4B no-think dominates the whole frontier (accuracy AND accuracy/second), or E2B decodes at 4B-tier tok/s (then per-layer embeddings do cost bandwidth on CPU). // EXP Reuse A2's harness; add Gemma-4-E2B and SmolLM3-3B; modes explicitly labelled (the tiny-models critic showed 2026 model cards mix thinking/non-thinking); plot accuracy vs CPU-seconds with bootstrap CIs; the town moral 'knowledge per token' is literally the y/x of this plot. // HW this box CPU; 8 GB box for the 3-5 GB models // COST ~4-8 h wall unattended; $0 // IF-FAILS If 4B dominates, the lattice should be built from one 4B generalist plus tiny scorers (chain C), not from 0.8B/2B generators — a major simplification of the owner's 'many tiny models' plan. If it holds, the 2B is the town's default worker.
- V2-A4: Adaptive early-stopping self-consistency (stop sampling when the running plurality margin >= m, ESC/Aggarwal-style) cuts samples >=2.5x at <=1 pt accuracy loss for the 2B, but for the 0.8B the plurality margin is uninformative (2608.11403: agreement does not predict when voting helps) and the saving is <1.5x. // FALSIFIER The 0.8B gets the same >=2.5x saving (agreement IS informative at tiny scale), or the 2B loses >2 pts at any stopping rule (early votes are systematically wrong). // EXP Offline replay over A2's stored k=8 samples: simulate stopping rules m in {2,3,4} and a max-k; compute accuracy and mean samples used; no new generation needed. Then confirm online on 50 items. // HW this box CPU (replay is seconds) // COST <30 CPU-min; $0 // IF-FAILS Tells the town whether vote agreement is a usable confidence signal at each size — the input chain B3 needs to choose between vote-gating and logit-margin gating.

**B. The verifier is the lever: tiny samplers + a cheap external check beat bigger greedy models** — The one part of Vision 3/Vision 2 the literature SUPPORTS is homogeneous sampling plus a verifier on verifiable tasks (Liu 2025, Wu 2024, Large Language Monkeys); the refuted part is diversity/debate. This chain tests the supported half at the only scale the box can reach (k<=16), with the verifier cost accounted, and ends by asking whether the verifier can itself be a tiny model trained on the town's own samples — which is the bridge to the lattice (chain C).
- V2-B1: Qwen3.5-0.8B (or 2B, per A3) best-of-16 with a FREE program-execution verifier (extract Python, run it, accept a numeric answer) beats Qwen3.5-4B greedy on 200 GSM8K items at matched CPU-seconds, while maj@16 of the same samples does NOT (coverage pass@16 exceeds pass@1 by >=15 pts, i.e. there is headroom for a verifier to select). // FALSIFIER pass@16 - pass@1 < 10 pts (no headroom; sampling is wasted on this model), or program-verified BoN <= maj@16, or 4B greedy beats both at equal wall-clock. // EXP Time 4B greedy on 200 items; give the small model the same wall-clock in samples (program-of-thought prompt); store all samples; compute pass@k, maj@k, exec-verified BoN; log the backfire set (items where k=1 was right and selection turned it wrong) and DM vs DC counts per the 2605.27559 four-regime scheme. // HW this box CPU // COST ~10-20 CPU-h total at 0.8B (ESTIMATE, no-think mode enforced); $0 // IF-FAILS If no headroom: the tiny model's errors are systematic (not sampling noise) and the lever is distillation/fine-tune (chain C), not test-time compute. If headroom exists but exec-BoN fails: tiny models cannot write runnable programs often enough — coverage of PoT itself becomes the slot to fine-tune.
- V2-B2: A learned process reward model helps only on the easy tail at tiny scale: Skywork-o1-Open-PRM-1.5B (int8 dynamic quant, ~1.5 GB, since fp32 6 GB does not fit) as BoN selector over B1's stored 2B samples adds >=+1 pt over maj@16 on MATH-500 levels 1-3 and is <= maj@16 on levels 4-5; the 7B PRM (Camber burst) closes >=half of the remaining gap to oracle pass@16. // FALSIFIER PRM-1.5B beats majority on levels 4-5 by >=2 pts (PRMs generalize down to tiny generators), or the 7B PRM adds nothing over the 1.5B (PRM size is irrelevant for tiny policies — then the whole Liu-2025 'needs a 7B PRM' dependency is moot). // EXP CPU: score ~3200 stored responses with PRM-1.5B int8 via torch-cpu (needs transformers install — director GO); last/min/product aggregation; stratify by level. CAMBER BURST (decisive, ~20 min XS): score the SAME stored samples with the 7B PRM in bf16 — the only place a GPU changes the answer, because a 7B in fp32 cannot fit any box here. // HW this box CPU (1.5B scoring) + Camber XS ~20 minutes (7B scoring) // COST ~2-3 CPU-h + 20 Camber-min (~$1 at $3/h); transformers install gate // IF-FAILS Either way the town learns the marginal value of PRM size on ITS generators — the question Liu 2025 leaves open for sub-2B policies — and whether a Weaver-style distilled verifier (B4) has any signal to distil.
- V2-B3: A confidence-gated cascade (small model answers; escalate to Qwen3.5-4B when the answer-token top-2 logit margin < tau) reaches >=95% of 4B accuracy on GSM8K at <=50% of 4B's CPU-seconds, and margin-gating beats vote-agreement gating (A4) by >=3 pts at equal escalation rate. // FALSIFIER No tau reaches 95% under 60% cost (margin is uninformative — tiny models are confidently wrong, consistent with the 20.5-nat dissent margin in 2608.11403), or agreement-gating matches margin-gating. // EXP llama-server with n_probs at the answer token for the small model on 200 items; sweep tau; run 4B only on escalated items; Pareto of accuracy vs CPU-seconds; compare to A4's agreement gate and to 4B alone. // HW this box CPU // COST ~3-5 CPU-h (reuses A2/B1 outputs plus 4B on the escalated subset); $0 // IF-FAILS If neither gate works, routing must be decided from the INPUT (chain C3), not from the small model's output — which changes the lattice topology from cascade to router.
- V2-B4: A tiny verifier trained on the town's own (question, sampled answer, exec-correct) triples from B1 — a sklearn logistic/GBM over cheap features (answer agreement count, program ran, length, margin) or a <=100M cross-encoder — reaches held-out AUC >= 0.75 for 'this sample is correct' and, used as the BoN selector, recovers >=50% of the exec-verifier's lift on items where no program was produced. // FALSIFIER AUC <= 0.6, or the learned selector <= maj@16 (surface features carry no signal about correctness at tiny scale; only execution does). // EXP Feature extraction over stored samples (seconds); sklearn 1.8 is installed; 5-fold CV by question; then replay BoN with the learned scorer; hold out one benchmark (IFEval-style or MATH) to test transfer. // HW this box CPU // COST <1 CPU-h; $0; zero downloads // IF-FAILS If surface features fail, the lattice's scorer slots must be execution/harness checks (mechanical, per the owner's 'should-be-harness-code' framing), not learned — a clean answer to the prompt-vs-model migration question in goal:g14.

**C. The lattice: graph-routed specialist slots and fine-tune slots on the corpus this graph already has** — goal:g14 names the first slot (a gap-spotter over parent reports vs COMPLETE.md failure categories) and the migration path (prose -> scored model). MEASURED today: 782 hypothesis nodes, 170 verdict nodes, but the verdict labels are 34 proved / 6 lean-disproved / ~130 lean-proved and 0 disproved — a near-constant label. The honest first hypothesis is therefore a kill-test on whether the corpus carries trainable signal at all, before any fine-tune is scheduled (goal:g14.1 says 'idea -> hypothesis ONLY, no run scheduled' — this chain keeps that).
- V2-C1: KILL-TEST: no classifier trained on the graph's 170 verdict nodes (hypothesis body + experiment body -> verdict class {proved, lean_proved>=75, lean<=65, lean_disproved}) beats the majority-class baseline by more than 5 pts on held-out nodes, because the label distribution is ~90% lean-proved and the text of a hypothesis does not predict its verdict. // FALSIFIER tf-idf + logistic regression (or a 384-d embedding + logreg) beats majority by >=10 pts with 5-fold CV grouped by goal — then the corpus DOES carry signal and fine-tune slots are worth scheduling. // EXP python3 + sklearn only: parse .agi/nodes/hypothesis/*.md and verdict/*.md, join by parent id, stratified 5-fold CV, report accuracy, macro-F1, and calibration; also test 'confidence' regression. Minutes. // HW this box CPU // COST <20 CPU-min; $0 // IF-FAILS If the kill-test fails (classifier wins), the town has a labelled dataset for its first learned slot. If it holds, the graph learns that its verdict labels are uninformative — itself a finding about verdict-writing discipline (needs hard negatives), which is worth more than a slot.
- V2-C2: The owner's named first slot — a gap-spotter that flags COMPLETE.md failure categories from a parent report — is prompt-solvable by Qwen3.5-2B few-shot at recall >= 0.6 / precision >= 0.5 against the Prime's own category labels for loops L1-L3, and by the 0.8B at recall < 0.4 (the slot needs 2B-class reading comprehension, not 0.8B). // FALSIFIER 2B recall < 0.4 (the categories are not surface-detectable from the report; the Prime used graph context the slot does not see), or 0.8B matches 2B (the slot is trivially pattern-matchable and should be regex/harness code, not a model). // EXP Build the label set from COMPLETE.md section 5 across its grid versions (grid.py payload build:COMPLETE.md --version N; MEASURED 11 versions) plus the per-loop HANDOFF slices; hold out one loop; run 2B and 0.8B via llama-server with the closed category list in the prompt; report per-category recall and the count of labels per category (the real deliverable: how many loops of labels exist). // HW this box CPU // COST ~1-2 CPU-h; $0 // IF-FAILS Failure tells the town the slot cannot be scored yet and how many more loops of labels it needs — turning goal:g14.1's 'no run scheduled' into a dated condition rather than a wait.
- V2-C3: An INPUT-feature router (sentence embedding of the question + logreg trained on per-item correctness vectors from A2/B1, 'will the 0.8B/2B get this right?') sends items to 0.8B vs 4B and reaches >=90% of 4B accuracy at <=40% cost, beating B3's output-margin cascade at equal cost; and error correlation between same-family tinies (0.8B/2B/4B) is higher than across families, so the router's features transfer across sizes. // FALSIFIER Router <= cascade at every cost point (the small model's own attempt is worth more than any input feature — the lattice is a cascade, not a switchboard), or router AUC < 0.6 on held-out items. // EXP Reuse stored per-item outcomes; embeddings via a tiny encoder (onnxruntime or sentence-transformers-class model, ~100 MB); 5-fold CV grouped by item; compare Pareto curves; compute Kish neff and pairwise phi WITHIN difficulty strata (the ensembles critic's confound). // HW this box CPU // COST ~1-2 CPU-h; $0 // IF-FAILS Decides the lattice topology (router vs cascade) with data from the town's own workloads, and gives the first measured error-correlation number for sub-2B models, which the ICML-2025 correlated-errors paper never measured.
- V2-C4: A rank-8 LoRA on Qwen3.5-0.8B for ONE narrow slot (the C2 gap-spotter or B1's program-of-thought format), trained on <=500 examples in fp32 torch-cpu, is feasible (<4 CPU-h) and lifts the slot's held-out metric by >=10 pts over few-shot; the same recipe on 2B/4B needs a Camber burst. // FALSIFIER CPU LoRA on 0.8B exceeds 6 CPU-h or does not fit 6 GB (then all fine-tuning is Camber-only and the 3 h/month cap allows ~6 slot-tunes a month), or the lift is <3 pts (the slot is prompt-solvable and the migration path should stop at prose). // EXP transformers + peft (install gate, director GO), fp32 (bf16 matmul is 2 GFLOPS on this box per the critic's measurement), seq len <=512, batch 4, 300 steps; held-out eval on the slot. CAMBER BURST (decisive, ~30 min XS): the same LoRA on Qwen3.5-4B with the same data, to measure whether slot gains scale with base size — the town's only justified training spend this month. // HW this box CPU (0.8B) + Camber XS ~30 minutes (4B) // COST ~4 CPU-h + 30 Camber-min (~$1.50); install gate // IF-FAILS Feasibility numbers (steps/s, RAM) for CPU LoRA on ARM fp32 are not published anywhere; even failure yields the town's fine-tune cost table for goal:g14.1.
- V2-C5: Self-distillation raises the tiny ceiling: SFT of Qwen3.5-0.8B on 2B/4B thinking traces that B1's exec-verifier marked correct (~1-2k traces) raises 0.8B GSM8K no-think accuracy by >=5 pts, and the gain is mostly format/PoT compliance (program-produced rate rises >=20 pts) rather than reasoning. // FALSIFIER Gain <2 pts (capacity-bound; distillation into 0.8B transfers nothing usable), or gain appears without any rise in program-produced rate (it IS reasoning transfer — then repeat on harder sets). // EXP Trace filtering from stored B1 samples; CPU SFT if C4 showed feasibility, otherwise fold into the SAME Camber 30-min burst as C4 (one training job, two adapters); eval on held-out 200 items with the standing harness. // HW this box CPU or the C4 Camber burst (no extra GPU time) // COST ~4 CPU-h or 0 extra Camber-min; $0-1.50 // IF-FAILS Tells the town whether the 'tiny-model ceiling rising over time' vision is a training story (distill) or an inference story (chains A/B) at 0.8B — and which of the two the monthly Camber allowance should feed.

**D. Depth recurrence as a test-time knob: loops, elastic depth, and CPU cache economics** — The owner named the Recurrent Looped Transformer first; the trove shows it has zero experiments and belongs to the cross-token-feedback lineage, while the measured loop-count-as-compute results (Ouro, Huginn) are intra-token. On CPU the one genuinely new question is whether loops cost compute but not DRAM bandwidth (weights re-read from cache). This chain tests looping as Vision 2 (more accuracy per parameter at eval) with a FLOPs-matched arm the literature omits.
- V2-D1: A 4-layer tied transformer (d=256, ~3M non-embedding params, plain torch fp32) looped 4x reaches >=90% of a 16-layer untied model's accuracy on p-hop induction and 4-digit addition, but a 4-layer untied model trained for 4x the tokens (FLOPs-matched arm) closes >=half of that gap — i.e. part of the 'depth' win is just compute. // FALSIFIER Looped <80% of 16L on both tasks (looping does not substitute for depth at toy scale), OR the FLOPs-matched 4L untied matches the looped model (looping is only extra training compute, not a test-time knob). // EXP nanoGPT-style char model; synthetic generators; 50k examples x len 32 (the critic's corrected budget, not 200k x 64); train 4L-untied (1x and 4x tokens), 16L-untied, 4L-x4-looped for equal wall-clock; eval accuracy vs eval loop count 1..8. // HW this box CPU (or 8 GB box, serial) // COST ~8-16 CPU-h across 4 arms (ESTIMATE from 15 GFLOPS sustained); $0 // IF-FAILS Either failure mode is decisive: no depth substitution at toy scale kills D2-D4 cheaply; FLOPs-matched parity says looping is a training-efficiency story (Vision 1 footprint) and not a Vision-2 lever.
- V2-D2: Elastic-depth training (random loop count in [1,8], loop-index conditioning, shortcut-consistency loss — LoopFormer recipe) gives one checkpoint whose per-token latent-diff exit uses <=50% of max loop-evals at <=1 pt loss, whereas the fixed-4-loop model from D1 loses >10 pts at 2 loops (Ouro's T=1..4 collapse). // FALSIFIER The elastic model is worse than fixed-4 at the same average budget, or the exit criterion fires uniformly (no per-token variance — adaptive compute needs a training signal beyond consistency). // EXP Reuse D1 code; add FiLM on loop index + consistency term; evaluate forced loops 1,2,4,8 and latent-diff / argmax-stability exits; log loop histogram per token; test the Ouro-vs-Huginn design fork (per-loop deep supervision vs implicit) as two loss functions on the same model — the cheapest discriminating experiment in the looped literature. // HW this box CPU // COST ~6-10 CPU-h; $0 // IF-FAILS Reveals whether adaptive per-token compute is trainable at tiny scale without deep supervision — the design decision that also governs whether cross-loop transport survives (Jacobian-lens), which chain E's oscillator import needs.
- V2-D3: CPU cache economics: for weight sets of 8/16/32/64 MB, tok/s of a looped model falls <20% per extra loop when weights fit in the N1's system-level cache, versus a dense equal-depth model whose tok/s falls by the full bytes ratio — so looping trades DRAM bandwidth for compute on exactly this hardware. // FALSIFIER tok/s per loop degrades the same as adding dense layers at every weight size (no cache benefit; the loop's activations or KV dominate traffic), or the crossover size is <8 MB (irrelevant for any useful model). // EXP Pure torch fp32 microbench: same block, weights sized 8/16/32/64/128 MB, decode 128 tokens at loops 1,2,4; measure ms/token; correlate with the cache size from lscpu/sysfs; one afternoon, no downloads. // HW this box CPU; repeat on 8 GB box (different cache) // COST ~2 CPU-h; $0 // IF-FAILS Kills the only CPU-specific reason to prefer looped over dense tiny models; if it holds, it sets the target weight size (<=cache) for any looped/recurrent slot the lattice trains, and feeds Vision 1.
- V2-D4: On the one open sub-2B looped checkpoint (ByteDance/Ouro-1.4B, int8 dynamic quant, transformers<4.56 + trust_remote_code), accuracy on 100 GSM8K rises monotonically from 1 to 4 loops, quantization error does NOT compound across loops (int8 vs fp32 delta at 4 loops <= 2x the delta at 1 loop), and a 3-loop draft / 4-loop verify self-speculative scheme gives >=1.3x tok/s at identical greedy output. // FALSIFIER int8 delta at 4 loops > 3x the 1-loop delta (quantization compounds — looped models need higher precision on CPU, bad for the 8 GB box), or 3-loop draft acceptance < 70% (self-speculation is not worth it; prior art LayerSkip already suggests weak drafts). // EXP Install gate (transformers pinned 4.54.1, director GO); fp32 weights (5.6 GB) do NOT fit 6 GB — int8 dynamic quant only; total_ut_steps in {1,2,3,4}; measure accuracy, tok/s, and a hand-written speculative loop on 50 prompts. // HW 8 GB box preferred (idle, ~1.4 GB int8); this box if tenants cleared // COST ~6-10 CPU-h at ~1 tok/s (ESTIMATE); ~3 GB download; $0 // IF-FAILS First measurement anywhere of quantization compounding across loops — it gates every looped-model plan for the 8 GB box and Camber, and its failure would redirect the town's depth-recurrence interest to training its own tiny looped slot (D2) instead of importing one.

**E. The coherence / oscillator / byte-neuron thread, frankly: kill-tests first, then the one Vision-2 hook (test-time synchronization steps)** — The trove's critics converged: the dead-head threshold's precision equals random selection (lift ~1.0), recall is 31.5%, joint removal is catastrophic (+1.13-2.84 nats), and head pruning saves 6-8% of params — it is not a pruning lever for a CPU box. The owner's own SNN lessons say spike input loses to graded input and stacking micro-nets is a dead end. The honest first hypotheses are kill-tests that can rescue the thread's one informative question (is the dead set structured, or a mean-shift artefact?) and the one novel claim (bit ORDER as code). The Vision-2 payoff, if any, is E4: synchronization steps as test-time compute.
- V2-E1: KILL-TEST (two-in-one): on Qwen2.5-0.5B, removing the bundled 157 tau-dead heads is within 1 std (0.48 nats, 3-seed spread) of removing 157 random non-protected heads (the dead set is not special), AND mean-ablation (replace each head's write-back by its calibration mean) recovers >=70% of the +1.13-nat zero-ablation damage (the 'catastrophe' is a LayerNorm/mean-shift artefact, not lost information). // FALSIFIER Dead-set removal is >1.0 nat worse than random (the coherence statistic finds structure — worth an oscillator map), or mean-ablation recovers <30% (dead heads carry real information; the 'mildly toxic' story is wrong and removal must be compensated, not zeroed). // EXP transformers install gate (director GO); Qwen2.5-0.5B fp32 (2 GB) on 16 x 128 WikiText-2 tokens; 10 random masks + dead mask x {zero, mean} ablation; reuse data/qwen25_05b_head336_*.json for the dead set and labels; ~22 forward sets. // HW this box CPU // COST ~20-30 CPU-min; ~2 GB download; $0 // IF-FAILS Either outcome settles whether the owner's equation deserves an oscillator map (structure found) or should be recorded as a descriptive statistic with a base-rate caveat (no structure) — and the mean-ablation number is the paper's own unrun future work.
- V2-E2: KILL-TEST for the byte-neuron as a quantized LIF: snntorch Leaky with state_quant to 8 levels (3 bits) and 256 levels (8 bits) on 784->1000->10 MNIST, 25 steps, 1 epoch, with a SPIKE-COUNT readout (the trove's 96.10% baseline used a summed-membrane readout a 1-bit-out neuron cannot expose) loses <=1 pp at 8 bits and <=3 pp at 3 bits versus fp32 with the same readout. // FALSIFIER >3 pp loss at 8-bit state, or the spike-count-readout fp32 baseline itself is >3 pp below the membrane-readout 96.10% (then the byte-neuron's real handicap is the 1-bit output channel, not the state width). // EXP Zero installs: snntorch 0.9.4 + MNIST IDX already at ~/.cache/torchvision; reuse snn_standard_model/experiment_infrastructure.py with a readout flag and state_quant; 3 runs x ~142 s/epoch-class; 5 seeds if the first result is within 2 pp. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30-60 CPU-min; $0; no downloads // IF-FAILS Bounds what ANY uint8-state, 1-bit-out neuron can do before a line of byte-neuron code is written; failure redirects the thread to LUT neurons (DWN/difflogic), which are a different object with published MNIST 98.3% at 23 KiB.
- V2-E3: Bit ORDER carries trainable information (the owner's one genuinely novel claim): a byte-neuron whose 8-bit state is a shift register of one upstream's last 8 outputs, firing via a 256-entry LUT trained by DWN's Extended Finite Difference, beats an order-blind popcount neuron by >=5 pts on delayed-XOR (delay 4) and sequential-row MNIST, at <=3x the ticks. // FALSIFIER Gain <2 pts (EFD cannot train an order-sensitive LUT — the address is non-stationary — so the byte-neuron needs a local/Tsetlin rule, not gradient), or the popcount neuron matches it (order is redundant with count at this fan-in). // EXP numpy/torch only: 1024 byte-neurons, event-driven gather; thresholds derived from fan-in (the trove's hand-set thresholds died/saturated); EFD + Learnable Mapping per DWN; compare shift-register vs popcount rule on both tasks; report accuracy, firing rate, ms/tick. // HW this box CPU // COST ~3-4 CPU-h; $0 // IF-FAILS No prior art exists either way on gradient-training order-sensitive LUT neurons; a negative is publishable knowledge for the town and decides whether the neuron-as-byte model is spatial (fan-in 8) or temporal (shift register) before any network is built.
- V2-E4: VISION-2 HOOK: an AKOrN-style sphere-Kuramoto block trained with T=8 synchronization steps on 16-bit parity and 4x4 Sudoku improves accuracy monotonically when evaluated at T=16 and T=32 (>=+5 pts over T=8), and the gain saturates when the order-parameter increment |R_t - R_{t-1}| < 0.01 — giving the oscillator thread a measured 'more steps = smarter' curve and a halting criterion. // FALSIFIER Accuracy plateaus or falls before 2x training steps, or R_t saturates by step 4 regardless of accuracy (synchronization is not the mechanism; the block is just a deep net). // EXP torch-cpu implementation of the AKOrN update (x' = Omega x + Proj_x(c + sum J x), renormalize), 64-256 oscillators; train T=8; eval T in {4,8,16,32,64}; log R_t per step; compare wall-clock to a matched MLP; use the mean-field O(N) trick (154 us/step at N=4096 measured) for the coupling. // HW this box CPU // COST ~3-4 CPU-h; $0 // IF-FAILS If oscillator nets do not offer test-time scaling at toy scale, the coupled-oscillator import has no Vision-2 payoff and should be scoped strictly as a Vision-1 substrate (or dropped) — the town stops spending on it as a 'smarter inference' idea.
- V2-E5: If E1 finds structure (dead-set removal > random): a head-head coupling matrix K_hh' = E_i cos(s_h(i), s_h'(i)) on Qwen2.5-0.5B predicts joint-ablation interaction — high-|K| pairs sub-additive, strongly negative K super-additive, |K| < 4/sqrt(d) additive — with |Spearman| >= 0.3 over 100 sampled pairs; this K, not the scalar c_bar_h, is the only well-posed 'coupled-oscillator map' the equation can generate. // FALSIFIER |Spearman| < 0.15 (the write-back geometry does not predict interaction; no oscillator map is derivable from this statistic and the SNN import thread closes), or E1 found no structure (this hypothesis is not run). // EXP Hook all 336 write-backs streamed per layer on 8 x 128 tokens (<4 GB); build K; sample 50 high-|K| and 50 near-zero pairs; 100 two-head-mask evaluations; export K for E4's coupling J if the correlation holds. // HW this box CPU // COST ~20 CPU-min; $0 (gated on E1 outcome) // IF-FAILS Closes the owner's 'equation -> oscillator map -> SNN' pipeline with a measured negative at ~20 minutes, freeing the thread's budget for E3/E4 where the novelty actually is.

coalescence:
- CP-1 (A + B -> the test-time compute allocator): once A2/A3 give the sequential-vs-parallel regime and B1/B3 give verifier lift and cascade cost, merge into ONE second-order hypothesis: for a fixed CPU-second budget on this box the optimal split across (thinking tokens, parallel samples, verifier calls, escalations) is a function of the small model's pass@1 alone, and it is a single Pareto sweep with four knobs over the same stored samples. No MVP until that sweep has a measured frontier with CIs.
- CP-2 (B4 + C1/C3/C4 -> one scorer, three heads): the verifier (B4), the router (C3) and the gap-spotter (C2/C4) are the same object class — a tiny scorer trained on the graph's own outcomes. Second-order hypothesis set: a single small encoder + per-slot linear heads matches per-slot fine-tunes at a fraction of the training cost; this is where the owner's 'lattice, not a ladder' gets its first measurable shape, and where the C1 kill-test decides whether the corpus is trainable at all.
- CP-3 (A4 + D2 + E4 -> iteration-to-convergence as the universal test-time knob): adaptive self-consistency, elastic loop exit, and Kuramoto sync-step halting are all 'stop when converged'. Second-order hypothesis: one latent-difference criterion (||h_t - h_{t-1}|| or |R_t - R_{t-1}|) governs the accuracy-vs-compute saturation in all three, so a single halting rule can serve the lattice's sequential slots; falsified if the three curves saturate at unrelated points.
- CP-4 (A1 + D3 + E1 -> Vision 1 feeds Vision 2): if decode is bandwidth-bound (A1) and cache-resident loops are cheap (D3), then weight-reusing Vision-2 levers (looping, self-speculation, batched sampling) are nearly free on this box while parameter-adding ones are not; if E1 says head pruning buys nothing, the Vision-1 memory budget goes to KV quant and hybrid-GDN models, which then sets the model line every Vision-2 chain runs on.
- CP-5 (all chains -> the rising-ceiling ledger): every chain reports against one standing 200-item harness (GSM8K + IFEval slices + the #23470 answer-flip protocol, paired McNemar, build-hash pinned, free-RAM and load logged) so the 'tiny-model benchmark ceiling rising month over month' becomes a dated curve in the graph; second-order hypothesis: the ceiling rises more per month from inference-side chains (A/B/D) than from training-side (C4/C5) at the 3-GPU-h budget.
critique of the owner plan:
- 'qwen3.8 50b' does not exist (HF API 2026-09-13: Qwen3.8-27B, Flash-Next 125B-A6B with a 51B n-gram embedding, 2.4T-A95B) and is most plausibly a speech-to-text artefact in a spoken transcript the owner hedged with 'maybe'. Under the owner's own later CPU-only correction it is off-charter: 27B at Q4 (~17 GB) fits neither this box (6 GB available, swap full) nor the 8 GB box. Vision 2 should drop it entirely; the 'get off OpenRouter in bursts' candidate is Qwen3.5-2B/4B (or 9B on a cleared box), and the account's own $39 qwen3.8-27b OpenRouter spend is not the loop's.
- The dead-head equation is not a pruning lever and should not be sold as one: its 95-100% precision equals random selection on the paper's own base-rate table (lift 0.99-1.02), recall is 31.5% on GPT-2, joint removal costs +0.87 to +2.84 nats, structural removal gives no CPU speedup at short sequences, and head pruning saves only 6-8% of parameters (MLPs dominate). The only informative uses left are a 20-minute kill-test (random-set + mean-ablation controls, E1) and, if structure survives, a pairwise write-back coupling matrix (E5). The cheaper Vision-1 path the trove already ranks first is KV quantization and hybrid Gated-DeltaNet models (9x less KV per token).
- 'Two or more tiny models are exponentially smarter than the sum' is refuted as stated: Kish effective votes saturate at ~2-3 regardless of ensemble size (correlated errors), coverage grows log-linearly in samples, Self-MoA beats heterogeneous mixing, debate is a martingale and HURTS a 1B model on GSM8K by 9 pp with 94% miscorrection. The supported half is homogeneous sampling + an external verifier on verifiable tasks. Restate Vision 3 as 'verifier-gated marginal accuracy per CPU-second', and put the town's test-time compute budget into chains A/B, not into debate or diverse-model mixing.
- Fine-tuning lattice slots on 'the corpus this graph already has' presumes labels with variance. Measured today: 170 verdict nodes are 34 proved, ~130 lean-proved, 6 lean-disproved, 0 disproved; the gap-spotter has roughly three loops of COMPLETE.md category labels. Any slot trained on this learns the prior.
