The owner's migration path (prose first, scored model second) is right; the first learned slot should be a verifier/router trained on per-item correctness the town generates itself (B4/C3), and the graph should start writing hard negatives (real disproved verdicts) before scheduling any fine-tune.
- 'Many hypotheses chased in parallel' collides with the box: 6 GB available of 23, 4 GB swap fully used by firefox/ffmpeg/VNC, load average ~10, pgmajfault already 2M — every measurement here records contention, and the trove's own timing numbers jitter 2-3x run to run. The cheaper path is serial runs on the idle 8 GB box for anything under 5 GB, this box only after tenants are paused with owner say-so, and every tok/s row carrying free-RAM, load and llama.cpp commit. Also unbudgeted everywhere: step 0 is a llama.cpp build plus ~6 GB of GGUFs and a transformers install decision that gates ~60% of all proposed experiments.
- The spiking / byte-neuron thread is being framed as a Vision-2 (smarter inference) lever, but the owner's own trove says the opposite: graded input beat spike input across all V2 variants, spike-count readouts killed >85% of V4 runs, stacking specialist micro-networks was a dead end, and recurrent bit-logic nets reach 4.39 BLEU where a transformer reaches ~27. It should be scoped as a Vision-1 substrate question with kill-tests first (E1/E2, zero installs) and one narrow novel claim (bit order as code, E3); the only Vision-2 hook is test-time synchronization steps (E4), and if that fails the thread should be recorded and parked, not MVP'd.
- Camber's 3 GPU-hours/month buys exactly two decisive things: PRM-7B scoring of stored samples (B2, ~20 min) and one LoRA/distillation run on the 4B (C4+C5, ~30 min, one job two adapters). Spending any of it on inference benchmarks or on the 27B is waste. The first five minutes of the first burst should be nvidia-smi, because the XS GPU type (and thus its bandwidth and the real $/h: 1 credit = $1, XS = $3/h) is still unverified in the graph and goal:g14.3 reasoned from a false 50B premise.

### VISION 3 — cooperation between tiny models. Frank reading of the trove: the only cooperation that measurably beats the best member is (a) homogeneous sampling + an external verifier on verifiable tasks, (b) prefix/KV sharing and draft/verify for throughput, and (c) routing/cascades for cost. Debate is a martingale and HURTS at ≤1B (Llama-3.2-1B GSM8K −9 pp, 94% miscorrection, 2605.27559); MoA with small proposers scores below self-consistency (Wunderlich 2026, 51.2% vs 68.7%); error correlation caps effective votes at ~2-3 regardless of ensemble size; coverage is log-linear in compute, never exponential. So the chains below treat 'exponentially smarter' as the null to kill and instead measure WHERE super-additivity is reachable on 4 arm64-N1 cores, using one shared per-item correctness corpus so every chain feeds every other. Box state MEASURED 2026-09-14 02:27Z: load 7.5/10.8/11.4, MemAvailable 7.2 GB, swap 3978/4095 MB used, pgmajfault 2,021,448, no llama.cpp/ollama binaries, transformers absent, 4x N1 with asimddp and no i8mm/sve. Step 0 for every chain except D is a llama.cpp source build (~10 CPU-min) plus Qwen3.5-0.8B / Qwen3-0.6B / Qwen3-1.7B / Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M GGUFs (~4.5 GB) — needs director approval, and thinking mode must be forced off or every token estimate is 3-15x wrong.

**A. The cooperation substrate on this CPU: prefill/decode asymmetry, draft/verify, shared KV** — Every accuracy-per-CPU-second claim in chains B, C and E silently assumes a tok/s number that does not exist for a 4-core N1 (the literature has no CPU-only ARM speculative or GDN numbers). Measure the regime first: on a bandwidth-bound decode with a compute-bound prefill, the cheapest cooperation is the one that converts decode tokens into prefill tokens (verify) or into cache hits (shared prefix).
- V3-A1: On this box single-stream Q4_K_M decode is memory-bandwidth-bound while prefill is compute-bound: for Qwen3.5-0.8B and Qwen3.5-4B, tok/s(decode) x GGUF_bytes lands within 0.5-1.0x of the measured copy bandwidth (ESTIMATE 16-20 GB/s single process, MEASURED by the tiny-models reader with a 2x ambiguity on read+write counting), and pp512/tg128 ratio is >= 5x. Corollary: Q8_0 decodes at <= 0.6x Q4_K_M tok/s. // FALSIFIER tok/s x bytes is < 0.3x the copy bandwidth on both models (then dequant compute dominates and Q4_0 repack, not bytes, is the lever), or pp512/tg128 < 3x (then verification is not cheaper than generation and chain C3 is dead). // EXP Build llama.cpp from source (record commit; -DGGML_NATIVE=ON). llama-bench -p 512 -n 128 -t 4 for Qwen3.5-0.8B, Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3.5-4B at Q4_K_M and Q8_0; sample MemAvailable and pgmajfault before/after each run; report tok/s, pp/tg ratio, tok/s x bytes vs the numpy copy microbench re-run in the same minute (state whether copy counts read+write). // HW this box CPU (record load average; repeat once on the 8 GB box for a contention-free control) // COST ~10 CPU-min build + ~4.5 GB download + ~30 CPU-min bench; $0 // IF-FAILS If compute-bound: KV/weight byte savings buy nothing on this box and the town's efficiency levers move to kernels (repack, int8 dot-product), and speculative decoding cannot win because verification costs as much as generation. Either way we get the first published tok/s table for a 4-core N1 with 2026 models.
- V3-A2: Draft/verify (llama.cpp speculative, Qwen3.5-0.8B or Qwen3-0.6B drafting for Qwen3.5-4B) yields <= 1.3x tok/s on prose and <= 1.6x on code/JSON on 4 N1 cores at any --draft-max in {2,4,8}, with acceptance 55-75%; the zero-model n-gram draft (--spec-type ngram-map-k) reaches >= 1.2x on repetitive JSON re-emission and ~1.0x on prose. Outputs at temperature 0 are byte-identical to plain decoding. // FALSIFIER >= 1.5x on prose or >= 2.0x on code with a model draft (then the CPU regime is GPU-like and two-model pipelines are worth more than the literature's 1.4-2.9x GPU numbers suggest), or any temperature-0 output divergence (then the cooperation is not lossless and every downstream accuracy number needs re-checking). // EXP llama-speculative / llama-server --spec-type {draft-simple, ngram-map-k, none}; 5 prose + 5 code prompts x 256 tokens, plus 10 prompts asking to transform a 1K-token JSON; record tok/s, acceptance rate, draft length, RSS; diff outputs against the no-spec run. // HW this box CPU // COST < 1 CPU-hour after A1; $0 // IF-FAILS A 'fail' in either direction is the missing datum: nobody has measured speculative decoding on CPU-only ARM (llama.cpp #21453 is an empty proposal). If drafts give <1.1x, the town stops budgeting draft models and spends RAM on KV instead; if n-gram drafting wins, draft-free cooperation is the CPU default.
- V3-A3: Two cooperating agents sharing a 4K-token system prefix on one llama-server (-np 2, --cache-reuse 256, --slot-save-path, optionally --kv-unified) see the second agent's TTFT fall by >= 5x versus a cold re-prefill on the N1 (papers: 11-136x on M4 Pro at 8B), with byte-identical temperature-0 outputs, and the saved slot restores after a server restart at the same gain. // FALSIFIER TTFT gain < 2x (then prefill on this box is cheap enough that sharing is not the lever and A1's pp number was wrong), or outputs differ after KV reuse/shift (then reuse is lossy and cannot be used for verification pipelines). // EXP llama-server Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M -c 16384 -np 2 --cache-reuse 256 --slot-save-path DIR; send the same 4K prefix with 5 different 200-token tails; time first token cold vs warm; save slot, restart, restore, re-time; repeat with -ctk/-ctv q8_0 vs f16 and record RSS and /metrics prompt-cache hits. // HW this box CPU // COST ~30 CPU-min; $0 // IF-FAILS If sharing does not pay, the graph-as-coordinator model must serialize agents with independent contexts and the 8 GB box's role changes from 'second agent host' to 'independent ladder runner'. We also learn whether the kv-unified buffer — the one substrate that lets several tiny models literally share memory — is usable on this build.
- V3-A4: Three Q4_K_M models (0.6B + 1.7B + 4B, ~4.1 GB weights) plus 4K-context KV for each fit co-resident on this box under current tenants (7.2 GB available at 02:27Z) with pgmajfault flat during a 10-minute mixed-decode run; on the 8 GB box the same set fits only if nothing else is resident. // FALSIFIER pgmajfault rises > 1000/s or tok/s of any member drops > 30% versus its solo A1 number while co-resident (then every multi-model chain must serialize and the RAM budget, not tokens, is the binding constraint on cooperation). // EXP Launch three llama-server instances (or one with -np 3 and three models via separate ports), drive a round-robin of 30 GSM8K prompts, sample /proc/vmstat pgmajfault and MemAvailable each second; compare per-model tok/s to solo. // HW this box CPU; repeat on 8 GB box // COST ~20 CPU-min per box; $0 // IF-FAILS A failure converts 'cooperating tiny models' from a concurrency question into a scheduling question; it also gives the director a hard number for the owner's banked decision on whether firefox/ffmpeg/VNC (10+ GB RSS, swap exhausted) may be paused for research runs.

**B. Where super-additivity actually lives: error correlation, process diversity, verifiers** — One 200-item per-item correctness corpus (4 models x greedy, plus 3 prompt regimes on one model, thinking OFF) is the raw material for every later claim about voting, routing, MoA, cascades and the neff ceiling. It is the cheapest dataset that lets the town replace 'exponential' with a measured marginal-accuracy-per-CPU-second curve, and its failure branches are as informative as its successes.
- V3-B1: Four tiny models from different families (Qwen3-0.6B, Llama-3.2-1B, SmolLM2-1.7B or SmolLM3-3B, Gemma-3-1B), thinking off, greedy on 200 GSM8K items, have mean pairwise error correlation phi-bar >= 0.3 computed WITHIN difficulty strata (Rasch-style or 3 bins by number-of-models-correct), so Kish neff <= 2.0, and their plurality vote exceeds the best single member by less than the independent-Condorcet prediction and by < 1 SE (paired bootstrap over items, SE ~3.5 pts at n=200). // FALSIFIER neff >= 3.0 (phi-bar <= 0.11) within strata, or plurality vote beats the best member by more than the Condorcet-independent prediction with bootstrap CI excluding zero (then independence exists at the tiny end of the curve, the ICML-2025 'larger = more correlated' result extrapolates downward, and Condorcet-style exponential gains are on the table). // EXP llama-server per model (thinking disabled via chat template / no_think), same prompt, same answer-extraction regex, 200 items; store per-item correctness vectors and generated tokens; compute pairwise phi on error indicators overall and within strata, neff, plurality vs best-single vs Condorcet prediction; McNemar per pair. // HW this box CPU (or 8 GB box for the contention-free rerun) // COST ~240k generated tokens; 3-5 CPU-hours background; $0 // IF-FAILS If errors are near-independent at ≤1B, the owner's 'exponential' has a mechanism (Condorcet) and the next hypothesis set is about voter count vs accuracy > 0.5 per member. If correlated (expected), the town learns the neff ceiling for its own ladder and stops proposing k-way votes of tiny models; the correctness matrix is still the routing dataset for E1.
- V3-B2: Process diversity beats model diversity: three prompt regimes on ONE model (Qwen3-1.7B: natural-language CoT, program-of-thought executed, equation-only) have lower within-strata phi-bar than three same-regime samples of the best regime, and their 3-way vote gains >= 2 pts more over the best single regime than self-consistency@3 of that regime (paired bootstrap, 200 items). // FALSIFIER phi-bar across processes >= phi-bar within one process, or the cross-process vote <= SC@3 of the best regime (then diversity of reasoning process buys nothing at 1.7B and the only remaining lever is the verifier). // EXP Same 200 items; 3 regimes x greedy plus 3 samples at T=0.7 of the best regime; PoT answers are executed in a sandboxed python subprocess (timeout 5 s); compute phi within/across, vote accuracies, and the backfire set (items where voting turns a correct k=1 wrong). // HW this box CPU // COST ~200x6x400 tokens ≈ 0.5M tokens; 4-6 CPU-hours; $0 // IF-FAILS A null tells the town that 'different processes' (the 2605.29800 recommendation) does not decorrelate tiny models, so cross-model cooperation must be verifier-based; the PoT execution rate alone (how often 1.7B emits runnable code) is a reusable capability number either way.
- V3-B3: A free program-execution verifier is the lever, not the vote: Qwen3-0.6B best-of-8 selected by executing its own PoT programs beats maj@8 of the same samples by >= 3 pts, and at matched wall-clock (measured in A1) reaches within 3 pts of Qwen3.5-4B greedy on GSM8K; maj@8 alone does NOT reach 4B greedy. // FALSIFIER Program-verified BoN <= maj@8 + 1 SE (verifier adds nothing on top of consensus for a 0.6B generator), or maj@8 alone >= 4B greedy at equal wall-clock (then sampling suffices and the verifier is redundant). // EXP Reuse B2's PoT regime on 0.6B: 8 samples/item at T=0.7; select by (a) plurality of executed results, (b) first program that runs without exception, (c) plurality of final answers without execution; compare to 4B greedy on the same 200 items; report accuracy per CPU-second from A1 timings. // HW this box CPU // COST ~200x8x350 tokens ≈ 0.56M tokens; 6-10 CPU-hours at 0.6B; $0 // IF-FAILS If the verifier adds nothing, 'detection without correction' is confirmed at 0.6B for even a perfect executor: the samples themselves lack coverage, and the town's cooperation budget should go to the bigger model rather than more samples. If sampling alone reaches 4B, the cost model flips: RAM for one 4B < RAM for 8x0.6B decode time.
- V3-B4: A learned tiny PRM (Skywork-o1-Open-PRM-Qwen-2.5-1.5B, torch-cpu fp32, ~6 GB RAM) as BoN selector over Qwen3-1.7B samples adds >= +1 pt over maj@8 on MATH-500 levels 1-3 but is <= maj@8 on levels 4-5 (mirrors its AIME24 10.0 vs 13.3); a 7B PRM on the SAME stored samples widens the gap by >= 2 pts on levels 4-5. THIS is the single decisive Camber burst: score ~1600 stored responses with the 7B PRM in 20-30 min on the XS. // FALSIFIER PRM-1.5B beats maj@8 on levels 4-5 by >= 2 pts (tiny PRMs suffice on the hard tail and the town never needs the GPU), or the 7B PRM does not beat the 1.5B by >= 2 pts on the same samples (verifier size is not the gap, and the '0.5B > GPT-4o' result is not reachable by scaling the verifier alone). // EXP Store 8 samples/item for 200 MATH-500 items (stratified by level) from Qwen3-1.7B; score with PRM-1.5B on CPU (last-step, min, product aggregation; check RSS against MemAvailable first — 7.2 GB at 02:27Z); then ONE Camber XS session (record nvidia-smi output in the same session to close goal:g14.3(b)): PRM-7B scoring of the identical files; compare per-level BoN accuracy with paired bootstrap. // HW this box CPU for generation + 1.5B scoring; Camber XS ~20-30 min for 7B scoring (the only Camber use in this panel) // COST ~0.64M generated tokens (8-12 CPU-hours background) + 1-3 CPU-hours scoring + ~0.5 GPU-hour ($1.50 at 3 credits/h); RAM is the risk // IF-FAILS Either branch bounds the verifier the town can afford: if the 1.5B PRM is enough, the whole 'small samplers + verifier' program runs CPU-only; if only the 7B moves the hard tail, the town knows the cost of the missing capability (~12x the policy) and can decide whether to distill (Weaver-style 400M) or drop hard-tail claims. The nvidia-smi line alone repays the burst.
- V3-B5: Adaptive early-stopping self-consistency (ESC / 'Let's sample step by step') cuts the sample budget of B3's 0.6B and B2's 1.7B runs by >= 2.5x at <= 0.5 pt accuracy loss, because tiny models converge early on the items they can solve and never converge on the ones they cannot. // FALSIFIER Budget reduction < 1.5x at <= 0.5 pt loss (then agreement is not an early signal for tiny models, consistent with 2608.11403's finding that plurality agreement does not predict when voting helps). // EXP Replay the stored samples from B2/B3 offline (no new generation): simulate window-based stopping (stop when the last w=3 answers agree) and report expected samples per item and accuracy vs fixed k=8; stratify by difficulty. // HW this box CPU (offline replay, minutes) // COST < 10 CPU-min; $0 // IF-FAILS A null says agreement is uninformative at tiny scale, which also kills 'agreement as the cascade gate' (E1) before it is built; the per-item convergence histogram is itself a reusable difficulty estimator for the graph.

**C. Kill-tests of the folk cooperation ideas at tiny scale: debate, MoA, and the prefill-side verifier** — The owner's 'two models talking' intuition maps to debate and mixture-of-agents, and the trove already predicts both hurt below ~3B. Running the kill-tests first, cheaply, on the same corpus prevents the town from building an MVP on them; the surviving idea (big model verifies, small model generates) exploits the CPU-specific prefill/decode asymmetry from A1.
- V3-C1: Two rounds of debate between two Qwen3-1.7B instances (thinking off) on 200 GSM8K items LOWER accuracy relative to round-0 majority by >= 3 pts, with correct->wrong flips (miscorrection) outnumbering wrong->correct flips by >= 2:1 (the 2605.27559 Llama-3.2-1B pattern: −9 pp, 94% miscorrection), while a 4-sample majority at the same token budget is >= round-0. // FALSIFIER Debate accuracy >= round-0 majority (martingale null holds or better) or flips are symmetric within bootstrap CI (then the tiny-model miscorrection result does not replicate on Qwen3-1.7B and debate is merely useless, not harmful). // EXP 200 items, 2 agents, 2 rounds, each sees the other's answer+reasoning; log every belief flip and classify per the 2605.27559 four-regime scheme (detect/correct/miscorrect/miss); compare to maj@4 at equal tokens. // HW this box CPU // COST ~200x4x400 ≈ 0.32M tokens; 3-5 CPU-hours; $0 // IF-FAILS If debate is neutral or positive on Qwen3-1.7B, the harm is family-specific and worth mapping across the ladder; if harmful (expected), the town has its own measured miscorrection set — which items a tiny model wrongly 'corrects' — the failure-as-knowledge artifact the moral asks for, and debate leaves the plan for good.
- V3-C2: Small PROPOSERS, not small aggregators, wreck mixture-of-agents (Wunderlich Table 2 inverted by the ensembles reader): three tiny proposers (0.6B/1B/1.7B) + Qwen3.5-4B aggregator scores BELOW 4B greedy alone by >= 3 pts and below plurality vote of the three proposers by >= 1 pt, and the aggregator copies the plurality answer on >= 70% of items; whereas 4B proposer x3 samples + 1.7B aggregator stays within 2 pts of 4B SC@3. // FALSIFIER Tiny-proposer MoA >= 4B greedy − 1 SE (then a strong aggregator can rescue weak proposers on this task and MoA-tiny is a viable cheap pattern), or aggregator agreement with plurality < 50% (it is doing real synthesis, not copying). // EXP Reuse B1's stored proposer outputs; MoA aggregation prompt to 4B and to 1.7B; 200 items; measure accuracy, plurality-agreement rate, and aggregation tokens; second arm swaps roles (4B samples, 1.7B aggregator). // HW this box CPU // COST ~200x2x600 tokens aggregation ≈ 0.24M tokens; 2-4 CPU-hours; $0 // IF-FAILS Either result fixes the direction of the MoA size asymmetry for the town's ladder; if the strong aggregator rescues weak proposers, the graph-as-coordinator should put the biggest model LAST (aggregate), not first (route).
- V3-C3: Big-verifies-small beats big-generates on CPU because verification is prefill (compute-bound, fast) and generation is decode (bandwidth-bound, slow): Qwen3.5-4B scoring 8 stored Qwen3-0.6B samples per item as select-only judge (no rewriting) costs <= 0.6x the CPU-seconds of 4B greedy generation (A1 pp/tg ratio) and reaches >= 95% of 4B greedy accuracy on GSM8K; a 'judge then rewrite' variant costs more and is not more accurate (detection without correction). // FALSIFIER Select-only judging reaches < 90% of 4B greedy accuracy (small samples lack coverage — consistent with a B3 failure), or its CPU-seconds exceed 0.8x of 4B generation (prefill is not cheap enough here, contradicting A1). // EXP For each of 200 items: prefill the 8 stored 0.6B answers into 4B with a 'which answer is correct, reply with the index' prompt (single short decode); time it; compare to 4B greedy on the same item; add a rewrite arm (4B may emit its own answer) and count DM vs DC. // HW this box CPU // COST ~200x(8x350 prefill + 20 decode) tokens ≈ 0.6M prefill tokens; 2-4 CPU-hours; $0 // IF-FAILS If judging is neither cheaper nor accurate enough, the prefill/decode asymmetry is not exploitable at this scale and cooperation reduces to routing (chain E); if it works, it is the one CPU-native form of 'two models > one' and the coalescence point with A1 becomes the town's cost law.

**D. The owner's byte-neuron pub/sub as a cooperation channel — starting with the honest kill-test** — The owner's own trove already records the two results that most threaten the byte-neuron as a cooperation mechanism: continuous (graded) input beats spike input across all V2 variants, and 'stacking specialist micro-networks at the prediction level is a dead end' (machinelearning-modularnn-spike-viz/knowledge/snn-architecture.md L26-28, L40). A 1-bit-per-tick channel between nets is exactly the mode that lost. So the first hypothesis must try to kill the idea cheaply on this box with zero installs (snntorch 0.9.4, numpy, torch-cpu all present), and only if it survives do we test the genuinely novel part (bit ORDER as code) and the head-coupling map.
- V3-D1: Kill-test: an 8-bit-state, 1-bit-out-per-tick neuron (snntorch Leaky with state_quant to 256 levels, spike-count readout) loses >= 3 pp versus the fp32 Leaky with membrane readout on delayed-XOR (delay 4) and sequential-row MNIST at T=25, 5 seeds — i.e. the byte neuron's output channel, not its state precision, is what costs accuracy (state_quant alone loses < 1 pp). // FALSIFIER 8-bit state + 1-bit/tick spike-count readout is within 1 pp of fp32 membrane readout on both tasks over 5 seeds (then a byte-neuron is a lossless substitute and the pub/sub channel is wide enough to carry cooperation; the owner's prior spike-vs-graded lesson was task-specific). // EXP Fork snn_standard_model/experiment_infrastructure.py into scratch: arms = {fp32 membrane readout, fp32 spike-count readout, state_quant=256 membrane, state_quant=256 spike-count}; 2-layer Leaky 64->128->out; 5 seeds; 3 epochs; report accuracy mean±sd and per-layer spike density. No downloads (MNIST IDX is in ~/.cache/torchvision). // HW this box CPU // COST ~20 runs x 2-5 min ≈ 1-2 CPU-hours; $0; no installs // IF-FAILS If the byte neuron survives, the town has a trainable 1-bit-channel neuron with a measured cost and D2/D3 are live. If it dies (expected from the owner's own lessons), the byte-neuron thread is re-scoped from 'cooperation mechanism' to 'encoder/front-end' and the SNN vision stops competing with chains B/C for tokens — a decision worth far more than the 2 CPU-hours.
- V3-D2: Bit ORDER carries information cheaply (the owner's only claim with no prior art): a byte neuron whose 256-entry LUT reads an 8-bit shift register of ONE upstream's last 8 outputs (temporal byte) separates delayed-XOR(delay 4) and a 2-stream phase-offset task at >= 90% where an order-blind popcount-threshold neuron over the same bits scores <= 60%, at <= 2x ticks; trained by DWN-style Extended Finite Difference with Learnable Mapping. // FALSIFIER The order-sensitive LUT neuron gains < 2 pts over the popcount neuron on delayed-XOR, or EFD fails to train the LUT (address non-stationarity) so accuracy stays at chance for both — then 'order as code' is either unnecessary or untrainable by gradient and the thread needs a Tsetlin/Hebbian rule before it can be evaluated. // EXP Pure numpy/torch: 1024 byte-neurons, event-driven gather (32 ms/tick baseline at N=8192 measured by the oscillators reader); two firing rules (LUT over shift register vs popcount >= k); EFD gradient per DWN 2410.11112; delayed-XOR and a two-channel phase-offset stream generator (new, ~40 lines; the synthetic.py 'oscillatory' pattern has no phase parameter); also the seed-2 spikyNN sweep: which of the 256 LUTs best reproduces the recorded LIF spike train at beta 0.8 (report edit distance vs beta, not against a single bar). // HW this box CPU // COST ~3 CPU-hours; $0; no installs // IF-FAILS A negative pins down whether the owner's 'which bits, in what order' semantics is (a) the same as an 8-tap binary FIR = a coarse LIF (no new capacity), or (b) untrainable — both are decisions the town cannot make from prior art because none exists for order-sensitive LUT neurons.
- V3-D3: A trained 1-bit/tick pub/sub channel makes two independently trained byte-neuron nets cooperate super-additively on a task that needs both inputs (parity of two 8-bit streams delivered to different nets): with Learnable-Mapping-trained subscription bits the pair reaches >= 85% where each net alone is at chance (~50%) and a pair with a RANDOM fixed channel stays <= 60%; the gain is not exponential but the marginal accuracy per added channel bit is concave. // FALSIFIER Random-channel pair >= learned-channel pair − 5 pts (the channel content does not matter, only its existence), or learned pair <= 60% (a 1-bit/tick channel cannot carry the needed information at these tick counts, confirming the owner's stacking-is-a-dead-end lesson at the bit level). // EXP Two DWN-style nets (D2 harness); net A sees stream 1, net B stream 2; k in {1,2,4,8} channel bits per tick from A to B; train B's mapping over A's outputs; report accuracy vs k and vs ticks; compare to a single net that sees both streams (upper bound). // HW this box CPU // COST ~2-3 CPU-hours; $0 // IF-FAILS This is Vision 3 at its smallest possible scale: if two bit-nets cannot beat one net given a learned channel, the byte-neuron pub/sub is not a cooperation mechanism and the oscillator/coupling thread should be pursued as a pruning/analysis tool only. The k-curve is the town's first measured 'marginal knowledge per channel bit'.
- V3-D4: Heads cooperate like coupled oscillators only if their write-backs phase-lock: on Qwen2.5-0.5B a head-head coupling matrix K_hh' = E_i cos(s_h(i), s_h'(i)) (same token position, within and across layers) predicts joint-ablation interaction — pairs with |K| > 4/sqrt(d) show sub-additive joint damage (redundant/synchronized) and strongly negative K super-additive damage — AND this survives a mean-ablation control (replace write-backs by calibration means instead of zeros). Pairs with |K| < 4/sqrt(d) behave additively. This is the only well-posed 'coupled-oscillator map' the dead-head paper supports: oscillators are tokens; heads are couplings; K_hh' is the head-level projection. // FALSIFIER |Spearman| < 0.15 between K_ab and joint-ablation excess loss over 100 sampled pairs, or the interaction structure vanishes under mean-ablation (then 'catastrophic joint removal' is a LayerNorm-statistics artifact and there is no coupling map to import into any SNN). // EXP GATED on a transformers+datasets install (~1.5 GB with weights; director approval): hook all 336 head write-backs on Qwen2.5-0.5B for 8x128 WikiText-2 tokens (stream per layer, < 4 GB RAM), build K (336x336), sample 50 high-|K| / 50 near-zero pairs, run 100 two-head-mask evaluations twice (zero-ablation and mean-ablation); join with bundled per-head delta_loss labels in scratchpad/dead-head/data/qwen25_05b_head336_*.json; add the 157-random-vs-157-dead removal control from the same run. // HW this box CPU (fp32, ~2 GB weights; run when MemAvailable > 6 GB) // COST ~25-40 CPU-min; $0; blocked on pip install decision // IF-FAILS A null closes the owner's 'dead-head equation -> oscillator map -> SNN' pipeline at its first joint: no head-level coupling structure exists to export, and the town redirects the coherence statistic to KV-group quantization bit-width (the vision-1 panel's lever). A positive gives the SNN thread a real adjacency matrix with a measured meaning (redundancy vs antagonism), which D3 can then import as a subscription mask.

**E. The graph as coordinator: routing and cascades learned from the town's own correctness vectors** — Routing is the one cooperation pattern with strong prior art (RouteLLM 85% cost cut at 95% quality; FrugalGPT) that does not require independence or a verifier, and the agi graph is already the object that records per-item outcomes. This chain turns chain B's correctness matrix into the graph's first learned router and states the knowledge-per-CPU-second metric the moral asks for.
- V3-E1: A confidence-gated cascade 0.6B -> 4B gated on the 0.6B's answer-token top-2 logit margin reaches >= 95% of Qwen3.5-4B greedy accuracy on GSM8K while spending <= 50% of 4B's CPU-seconds (A1 timings); gating on sample agreement (maj@4 unanimity) does NOT reach 95% at <= 60% cost (2608.11403: agreement does not predict when a tiny model is right). // FALSIFIER No margin threshold reaches 95% of 4B accuracy under 60% of its cost (the 0.6B has no usable self-knowledge and routing needs an external difficulty model), or agreement-gating matches margin-gating (then the cheaper signal wins and logits need not be exposed). // EXP llama-server with n_probs at the answer token for 0.6B on the 200 items; sweep tau; escalate to 4B on the same items; plot accuracy vs CPU-seconds Pareto against 4B-alone and 0.6B maj@k; second arm uses B5's agreement signal as the gate. // HW this box CPU // COST reuses B1/B3 outputs + 4B on escalated items; 2-4 CPU-hours; $0 // IF-FAILS If margin does not route, the town learns that tiny-model calibration is the missing capability and that every cascade needs an external router (E2) — a concrete, cheap thing to train. Either way the Pareto curve is the first measured 'accuracy per CPU-second' object in the graph.
- V3-E2: An oracle router over {0.6B, 1.7B, 4B} built from B1's per-item correctness vectors has accuracy >= 4B greedy at <= 40% of its cost (upper bound), and a learned router on prompt-only features (token count, digit count, operator count, 0.6B margin from E1, PoT-runs-without-error from B2) trained by 5-fold CV recovers >= 50% of the oracle's cost saving at <= 1 pt accuracy loss. // FALSIFIER Oracle saving < 25% (the models' correct sets nest — bigger always subsumes smaller — so there is nothing to route), or the learned router recovers < 25% of the oracle gain (difficulty is not predictable from cheap features at this scale). // EXP Offline on stored vectors: compute the oracle assignment; train a logistic/GBM router with sklearn (installed) on 200 items with 5-fold CV; report cost-accuracy curve and the feature importances; write results as a verdict-shaped table the graph can store per item. // HW this box CPU (minutes, offline) // COST < 15 CPU-min; $0 // IF-FAILS Nesting (bigger subsumes smaller) is itself the key structural fact: it means the tiny models add no coverage and the only cooperation left is throughput (chain A). A router that fails to learn tells the town which cheap features carry no difficulty signal, so it stops logging them.
- V3-E3: Operationalizing the moral: across every scheme measured in chains A-E, marginal accuracy per CPU-second beyond the best single member is concave in spend and bounded by the verifier/prefill schemes (B3, C3, E1) at the top, votes in the middle, and debate/MoA-tiny at or below zero; no scheme's curve has a convex (accelerating) segment with bootstrap CI excluding zero — i.e. 'exponentially smarter than the sum' is falsified on this hardware and should be replaced in vision 3's node by a measurable target (marginal accuracy per CPU-second, with CI). // FALSIFIER Any scheme shows an accelerating segment in accuracy vs log(CPU-seconds) with CI excluding zero (then a real super-additive regime exists and becomes the town's central object of study). // EXP No new runs: assemble every (scheme, CPU-seconds, accuracy, CI) row from A-E into one table; fit concave vs convex segments per scheme with bootstrap over items; render as the vision:local-maxxing-3 body with the owner's verbatim preserved. // HW this box CPU (offline) // COST < 30 min of analysis; $0 // IF-FAILS If a convex segment appears, the owner's intuition is vindicated at a specific, named scheme and budget, and every other chain re-orients around it; if not, the town has a defensible restatement of vision 3 and stops spending tokens chasing an exponent.

coalescence:
- CP1 (after A1-A3 + first B1 timings): the CPU cost law. Merge into a second-order hypothesis set: 'On a bandwidth-bound decode / compute-bound prefill box, cooperation pays only where it converts decode tokens into prefill tokens (verify/select: C3, B3) or into cache hits (shared prefix: A3), and never where it adds decode tokens (debate, MoA, k-way sampling without a verifier).' Every later est_cost line is re-derived from A1's tok/s table.
- CP2 (after B1 + B2 + C1 + C2, one shared 200-item corpus): the neff ceiling. Merge the per-item correctness matrix, within-strata phi-bar, flip logs and MoA copy-rate into 'What is the effective vote count of this town's ladder, and does anything (process diversity, aggregator size) move it?' Decide here whether ANY vote-based scheme survives into an MVP; expected outcome is neff <= 2-3 and votes dropped.
- CP3 (after B3 + B4 + C3 + E1): the verifier question. Merge into 'Which verifier is worth its cost on CPU: free program execution, a 1.5B PRM, a 4B select-only judge, or nothing?' The single Camber burst (B4, PRM-7B on the same stored samples, ~30 min) is the only external datum this set needs; the nvidia-smi from that session closes goal:g14.3(b) for the whole town.
- CP4 (after D1 + D2 + D3, joined with B2's process-diversity result): the channel-width question. Merge into 'How many bits per tick must a cooperation channel carry before two nets beat one, and is that below the 1-bit/tick the byte-neuron offers?' If D1 kills the 1-bit channel, the byte-neuron thread re-homes under the SNN-as-encoder work of the vision-1 panel rather than under vision 3.
- CP5 (after D4, joined with the vision-1 panel's dead-head chain): the coupling-map question. Merge into 'Does a head-head write-back coupling matrix K_hh' carry interaction structure that survives mean-ablation?' Positive -> the SNN thread receives its adjacency mask (D3 import); negative -> the coherence statistic is redirected to per-KV-group quantization bit-width and the oscillator-import line is closed with a verdict node, not left as an open charter.
- CP6 (after E2 + E3, final before any MVP): restate vision 3 in the graph. Merge every scheme's (CPU-seconds, accuracy, CI) row into one Pareto table and mint vision:local-maxxing-3 with the operational target 'marginal accuracy per CPU-second beyond the best member, bootstrap CI over items', the graph-as-coordinator being the learned router of E2 trained on the town's own correctness vectors. This is the second-order hypothesis set the owner asked for; chains A-E are its inputs, not MVPs.
critique of the owner plan:
- 'Exponentially smarter than the sum' has no measured instance anywhere in the 2023-2026 literature the trove read. Coverage is log-linear in samples (Large Language Monkeys, four orders of magnitude); error correlation caps effective votes at ~2-3 (Kish neff, 9 judges from 7 families ≈ 2.0-2.5); the only exponential available is Condorcet convergence, which needs per-member accuracy > 0.5 AND independent errors — and models agree 60% of the time when both err (ICML 2025). Restate vision 3 as 'marginal accuracy per CPU-second beyond the best member, with CI' (E3) before minting its node; chasing an exponent will spend tokens confirming a null.
- Debate and mixture-of-agents — the obvious 'two models talking' designs — are measured to HURT at the owner's scale: Llama-3.2-1B GSM8K debate −9 pp with 94% miscorrection (2605.27559); all-8B MoA 51.2% vs self-consistency 68.7% (Wunderlich 2026); debate belief is a martingale (Debate-or-Vote 2025). The cooperation that works is homogeneous sampling + an external verifier, and the headline 'Qwen2.5-0.5B > GPT-4o' result depends on a 7B PRM — 12x the policy — which this town cannot run on CPU. Chain C runs the kill-tests cheaply; chain B measures the verifier the town can actually afford.
- The owner's own trove already refutes the byte-neuron as a cooperation channel twice: 'continuous (graded) input consistently outperforms spike mode across all V2 variants' and 'stacking specialist micro-networks at the prediction level is a dead end' (machinelearning-modularnn-spike-viz/knowledge/snn-architecture.md L26-28, L40, MEASURED by the graph-prior-art reader). A 1-bit-per-tick pub/sub channel is precisely the losing mode; prior art (DWN LUT neurons, difflogic, Tsetlin) covers every piece except bit ORDER, and recurrent bit-logic nets reach 4.39 BLEU on WMT14 vs ~27 for a transformer. The honest first move is D1's kill-test on snntorch state_quant (zero installs, ~2 CPU-hours), and only the order-sensitivity claim (D2) is genuinely new.
- 'qwen3.8 50b' does not exist (HF API 2026-09-13: Qwen3.8-27B, Flash-Next 125B-A6B with a 51B n-gram embedding, 2.4T-A95B) and the source is a spoken transcript ('Canberra cloud' in the same line), so it is most plausibly a speech-to-text artifact for 27B, '0.8B', or the 51B embedding. More important: the owner's own 01:0xZ correction ('all models tiny enough to run on CPU + regular RAM') makes the entire ≥27B line off-charter except for a 3 GPU-hour/month burst; the ladder is Qwen3.5-0.8B/2B/4B, Gemma 4 E2B/E4B, SmolLM3, Phi-4-mini. Meanwhile qwen/qwen3.8-27b is already the account's largest OpenRouter spender ($39.20, 49% — not the loop): the biggest cost lever is outside the research plan entirely.
- The dead-head equation is the wrong lever for head pruning on this hardware and does not yield an oscillator MAP as stated. The paper's own base-rate table gives lift ~1.0 over random head selection (93-99.9% random precision), AUC(z_h -> unsafe) is 0.17-0.47 on the three small models, removing the dead set together costs +1.1 to +2.8 nats, structural head removal saves only 6.6-8.4% of parameters and gives no CPU speedup at seq ≤ 256; the paper's oscillators are TOKENS (Lohe flow on S^(d-1)) and heads are couplings, so there is no head-head or neuron-neuron matrix to import into an SNN. The cheaper, well-posed path is D4: a head-head write-back cosine matrix K_hh' tested against joint ablation WITH a mean-ablation control (~30 CPU-min), and the KV-cache thread (q8_0 KV, GDN hybrids at 12 KB/token) is where the CPU wins actually are.
- The plan under-budgets the substrate and the statistics. This box is not idle (MEASURED 02:27Z: load 7.5/10.8/11.4, 7.2 GB available, swap 3978/4095 MB used, pgmajfault 2.02M): every tok/s measured now includes firefox/ffmpeg/VNC contention, and there is no inference runtime at all (ollama purged 2026-09-13, no llama.cpp, no transformers). Step 0 for chains A/B/C/E is a llama.cpp build plus ~4.5 GB of GGUFs — a director decision, not a research step — and the idle 8 GB box is the better ladder host. Thinking mode must be forced off or token estimates are 3-15x wrong; 200-item slices give SE ≈ 3.5 pts so every falsifier needs paired bootstrap/McNemar; and the Camber budget should buy exactly two things — one nvidia-smi to identify the XS GPU (5 min, $0.25) and one PRM-7B scoring pass over stored samples (B4, ~30 min) — nothing else in vision 3 needs a GPU.
